# Open Threads

## How to read this file

**This file is a rolling window. It holds the global thread list, the whole of the volume currently being written, and nothing older, and everything older is in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-08.md`, verbatim, with every thread number exactly as it was here.** The window moved for Volumes 01 to 06 during the Volume 07 Batch 0004 review, for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close, and for Volumes 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, **and after that last cut this file holds the global thread list and nothing else.** As of the review of Batch 0004 this file was **661 KB**; it was about 98 KB through Volume 07 and **is now about 20 KB, and it stays about that size for as long as the window is maintained.** The rule that makes the move safe is the same one the other three ledgers use and it is worth restating: **threads are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, only moved, and a volume is never archived while a close is reading it.**

**Read this guide, then the section for the volume you are writing. For the Volume 09 first prompt the useful reading is this guide and the seventeen threads under *Threads the Volume 08 close hands to Volume 09*, which are in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-08.md` and which are the successor's whole instruction, with `outline/volumes/volume-08-close.md` §7 behind them when a thread demands the reasoning.** **The live window is empty and opens on Volume 09; every section above that, covering the initial threads, the outline-phase answers, and Volumes 01 to 07, is in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-07.md` under the same numbers and is reachable by grep, not by reading.**

**The window was moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close and for Volumes 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, in each case after the close had audited the whole volume, and every section through the Volume 08 close is in the archive under its own numbers. A Volume 09 writer needs this guide and the Volume 08 close's seventeen threads and nothing older, and needs `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-08.md` whenever a thread demands its own chapter.**

**The same warning as in `state/continuity.md` applies. Do not trust a line-number pointer into a chapter file. Refer to a passage by its chapter and its opening words.**

## Initial story threads

1. **The cracked toll bell:** Why does the Bellweather anchor respond to Aren, and what does its first crossing cost him? The local mystery pays off in Volume 1 — a damaged anchor that still remembers refusing a severance writ, a permanent roadmark, the remembered pitch of one verse, nine lost days, and three days of fever. The bell's provenance and its two-pence purchase stay live for the whole series, because at Chapter 900 it is silent under his hand.
2. **The living Bellweather path:** Bellweather-Living has its own people, debt system, and refusal to be restored by the main world. Established in Volume 1 through the guild, the toll houses, and the Hushwater ferry cooperative, and carried forward as a recurring relationship rather than a one-volume exhibit.
3. **The official Sundering account:** Who authorized the cull, what did Aren actually know, and why was his disgrace manufactured? Edric's name is put on paper in Volume 1's midpoint, by the toll houses rather than by the guild. The full Council context, the post-Sundering inquiry, and the cost of Aren's own silence are spread through Volumes 2, 6, 11, and 18.
4. **The Gatehouse's consent rule:** The archive was built to prevent forced historical selection, but the Council altered it. The First Witness is revealed in Volumes 11–15; Volume 01 may only show its *effect* — a private-keeper clause being applied to Aren in a provincial letter — and must not name the mechanism.
5. **Ilyra Kest's crossing:** Aren’s mother used the Gatehouse to carry him from a sealed path. Her route is answered by the final volume; her exact survival is intentionally unknown and is untouched in Volume 01 except as the verse whose pitch he loses.
6. **Aren and Mara:** Their relationship begins as evidence custody versus intrusion. Volume 1 stages it as a fight over a filing system, with her winning the terms of his only evidence. It develops through disagreement and changed decisions, never instant attraction or dependence.
7. **Aren's command flaw:** Volume 1 stages his first failure of the pattern inside the path (Chapters 6 and 41–47): a solo Bind that puts the force into the pier and hurts a man, because he is the best reader of the load path in the room. Volume 1's smaller version is volunteering for a cost nobody asked him to pay, in Chapter 7.
8. **Roads without Aren:** Established in Volume 1 by the guild bench, the path's stewards, and the refusal to be grateful. The limited channel that opens at the volume's close is run by a Merefen steward and a Bellweather-Living steward with one independent witness each; Aren witnesses and translates. Volume 18 can therefore remove him without removing the network.
9. **The named dead:** Ferris Oat, Hanna Vey, Corin Slate, Bess Alder, Anwen Rusk, and Bryn Rusk exist as records, not as figures. They are the reason the case has a moral weight the Crown cannot dismiss. Jos Rusk, who survives and claims for them, is a different person and must stay distinct from Pei Tarrow; both first appear in Batch 0002, never together.
10. **Hushwater's silence:** The drowned hamlet's name in older records hints that the Bellweather-Living path was known to the archive system before the cull. Volume 01 gives the hint its physical form — Hushwater Reach, a living hamlet on a channel that is drowned in the main world, with a crossing book in a living hand — and explains nothing. The cooperative counts hands and hearths, not souls, so its nearest estimate is about **sixty** and nobody can be more definite; the erased village of the same name is **eighty**, and the two figures must never be printed together or compared on the page. This stays a hint until Volume 09.

## Answers to the questions the outline phase had to settle

- **What exactly is Aren doing, and for whom, in the first three chapters before any crossing occurs?** He is holding a road at flood stage for a town that cannot pay him properly, in the cheapest labor available, and he is doing it because it is the only problem he is allowed to have. The first three chapters are one working day argued over by four parties with money at stake, his own bond, and the bell he should have sold.
- **What makes him ring the bell on the anniversary storm rather than any other night?** Three reasons, all concrete: the storm is the only night the river is loud enough to cover the sound of a working; the town's custom puts him among the ferrymen ringing for the drowned, so one man with a bar is nobody's story; and the Archive's deadline gives him a night he privately resents. The cracked bell is the hinge of a refusal made in a storm on that date, and it answers the same weather. **Prose note:** the Archive deadline is now the **second of Greenmonth**, the province's renewal date, and the nineteenth is the human date. Both deadlines point at the same week and only the first one matters to a filing system.
- **How does Mara first become involved, given that she works above a salt merchant and Aren works a bridge?** She needs a sworn tradesman's account of what the bell is attached to, because the Archive cannot record an anchor on a clerk's word, and the bell is the only surviving object she can put in front of a magistrate. She finds him through the Old Toll Bridge's parish repair marks, by asking the ferrymen who signed the sill. She offers custody and a claim number in exchange for a promise not to ring it — undated, and he agrees to the whole of it. He does not tell her the bell rings by itself, and Chapter 4 is therefore a broken promise rather than a technicality.
- **Which of the seven villages gives Volume 01 its most immediate human stake?** **Hushwater.** It is the only one with a living claimant in Merefen, and its crossing book is the document that can be checked against a ferryman's memory. Jos Rusk is the first claim Mara personally refutes, in Batch 0002, and he is wrong about the cause and does not want to be argued with. Alder Cross carries the second stake, through Bess Alder's parish list, which is what splits the Crown's four thousand back into two hundred dead who can be named. Volume 01 keeps the two figures on separate pages and never prints a combined total.

## Final-volume questions

- Can the Gatehouse be opened to thousands without making one person's memory the lock?
- Can path communities share roads while retaining the right to refuse contact?
- Will Aren surrender the private power that makes him useful?

## Threads Volume 09 Batch 0001 carries to Volume 09 Batch 0002

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Batch 0001 and it is Batch 0002's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing here renumbers anything above it and nothing here resolves anything the block left open.** Eighteen threads, and the eighteenth is what the successor must not do.

1. **The frame, derived twice, and the fact that the year does not turn inside Volume 09.** Day 931, the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Monday, to day 1140, the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth YR 318, a Sunday. Two hundred and nine days elapsed, two hundred and ten inclusive, five blocks of forty-two days: 931–972, 973–1014, 1015–1056, 1057–1098, 1099–1140. **The whole of Volume 09 is YR 318.** Market days are Saturdays and Thursdays, sixty in the volume at twelve a block, thirty of them Saturdays; Block 0001's twelve are 934, 936, 941, 943, 948, 950, 955, 957, 962, 964, 969, 971. **Block 0002 is days 973 to 1014, the eighth to the nineteenth of Longlight, and the batch's ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived and not read off a card.**

2. **The song, and it is now canon, in full, and Batch 0002 may not add a tenth line and may not print the word that names the founding paper.** Nine lines: **Ask the far side. Two books. Never a person twice. Read it yourself. Say it in a room. Leave the name off. Anybody may come. Feed the stranger first.** The ninth is **Coldharbour**, a road, held by a woman of about forty-four and not said. **A voice of thirty-four words, said once in a market, is the volume's engine and is not to be improved on:** *a road's name only stays a road's name as long as nobody says it where a load is being settled.*

3. **Where the eight practices are held, as Batch 0001 established them, and none of these people is a keeper of anything.** Seven, *Anybody may come*, at the kilns. Four, *Read it yourself*, at the arch, held by a mason of about sixty-eight who cut his punch-mark into the bed of the ninth voussoir from the left forty years ago and has been told twice by a clerk not to say so. Three, *Never a person twice*, in a fold thirty miles south, held by a man of about forty-one who has held it since he was eleven. Eight, *Feed the stranger first*, at a roadhouse, held by a woman of about fifty with a column of about three hundred and seventy figures on four feet of board and no head on it. **Two was refused by Mara Vey in about nine words. Five, six and nine are not yet on the page in a place.** A Batch 0002 writer who needs a fifth place has three candidates the block has already named: a fen market where a bench sits on Saturdays; a man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar; a man of about thirty-one with a canvas bag about nine days' ride away. **And a sixth is a man of about thirty-four on a coast about two days off who wrote nine words asking whether the first of the eight is still being done anywhere, and was not answered.**

4. **The ninth copy, and it is at a bar, and nobody may improve on it.** A sheet with **nine figures and no head and a date at the bottom**, put on the end of a bar of nine feet of oak at the head of a made road four miles west of Marl on 949 and under a stone on a bench inside a shed nine feet by seven. **A man of about nineteen cannot read it, counts a barrow of bricks, and has not asked anybody to read it and is not going to.** **His four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth and this block did not say it to him and the next block may not either and about eight people in that town are still right or wrong about it and none of them has said a word.** He is paid nine pence a day for the three days in seven the bar is up; the bar is up four; a hundred and seventy days gave about ninety-seven up-days and about seventy-three paid days, and **there are about seventy-three twists of paper in the left pocket of a coat that is not his.** The bar four miles west is the only one of the three this block touches, the kerb bar and the market bar were not touched, and **the three may not be merged in any later block.**

5. **The leak, and it is not recalled, and it is not repaired, and it is going to arrive.** Chapter 957, a market morning, a market town of about two thousand on the salt. **A man of about thirty-three with a barrow sang all nine lines in about eleven seconds to settle an argument about a load of dressed stone. About forty people heard it; about nine were within about nine feet; about thirty-one were within about sixty feet of a nine-hundredweight load with a chalked corner.** A woman of about nineteen said it to a man of about thirty, who wrote it on the back of his own hand and put a bar of soap over it in the evening. **A man of about forty-three said there was no such road and a man of about twenty-six said there was and that his grandfather had a field by it.** **Nobody put it back.** The block's cost is irreversible and Block 0002 inherits the whole of it, and `outline/volume-09.md` puts the consequence in Block 3: an office with a column invents a place, a figure and a trade for a road in a fortnight.

6. **The distribution, and how it actually happened, and this is the block's real discovery.** **About a hundred and four sheets went out of the room over the weigh-house on the fixed day, eight of them the same eight words, in the middle of the load, arranged by a keeper of records who will not produce the list of nine rooms she named four of the eight from, and carried by a man with a barrow who does not know what he said, and witnessed by a man of thirty-two with a limp who is on no list.** Two of the eight are already on their way back. **Three of the eight have refused the paper in writing and are doing the thing anyway.** **A successor may not turn this into a scheme, and may not have anybody congratulate themselves about it, and may not have the protagonist call it his.**

7. **Three refusals, in full, and none of them is improved on in Block 0002.** Brenna Tull: a sheet of paper with eight words on it in a kiln yard is a thing that can be picked up, and she is right and nobody has the answer to it. The man of about forty-one: *if I take a sheet off a man with a stick and put it on a bench in this fold then I am a person twice, and I will be a person twice in a song and in a room, and there is no way to be the man who walks it and the man who has it.* The woman of about fifty: she read seven of them out loud to an empty room and did not read the eighth, and the paper went back on the same cart in the same band. **A fourth refusal is owed a full scene somewhere in this volume, and the man of about twenty-one's refusal of the boundary walk is the reason it can be paid: he is going to tell his boy that he does not have to do it, and he has told nobody, and about a year from now there will be a boy of about ten who knows.**

8. **The two collections with no head on them, side by side, in about four minutes, with no speech.** **Four feet of board, about three hundred and seventy figures, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen. About four hundred entries, four years, a boy of seventeen who can read figures and not words. About nine marks in common, and the ninth is a mark neither of them can read because the person who can read it is a mason of about sixty-eight about forty miles off.** **This is the closest thing in the book to a distributed record and it was made by two people who had never heard of each other ten days before and who are each somebody's ninth mark, and a Block 0002 writer must not improve on it and must not let anybody explain it.**

9. **The clerk of about sixty and his two kinds of minute and no third, and it is the volume's second-best object after the bar.** A walk: who came, in what order, which side of a hedge they stood on. A figure: what was cut into the stone and when. **He is not going to keep a third, and the reason is that a man in a town two hundred miles off could then tell him how many people were in a field of about nine acres and could not tell him one thing about the hedge — because the arguing stops being about the hedge and starts being about which of nine men said what, and it turns into the thing itself.** And **about four inches of a stone in a hedge is in his head and in neither minute, and a man of about sixty-seven who farms the near field is the reason it was eleven years overdue and nobody has ever asked him about that.**

10. **The yard, the board under the near wheel, and the three sentences.** **Tovan Kest is the man of twenty-five and the yard sent a barrow, two men and a length of board four miles for a day, and he agreed in about nine seconds because it is not a job anybody has to think about.** The four sentences in Chapter 409 are *put a board under the near wheel* / *it is soft for nine feet and then it is not* / *it is soft for nine feet going and it is hard coming* / and a man of thirty-two with a limp would not have thought of it. **The breach is not repaired and the word for what they are was not printed once and is at 2 across four hundred and ten chapters and is spent, and the far end of the span built over nineteen mornings is a hand's width out and will be for about two hundred years, and this block does not report it as a repair because it is not one.**

11. **The man of about nineteen with a book of about four hundred entries who cannot read words, and the ninth line he ruled four times for nothing.** **He has said out loud, to two grown men who had not asked him, that he cannot read his own book, and he is seventeen, and he has never shown the book to anybody, and he showed it in Chapter 408 after being fed a plate before anybody asked him his name.** `outline/volume-09.md` §10 item 5 makes him the person who identifies the sealed path, by an arithmetic and not by a crossing. **A Batch 0002 writer must not spend that, and must not make him into a device for it, and must not have anybody ask him to read the book out loud.**

12. **The word, said twice in a field, and the fuse.** **A mason of about sixty-eight has been saying Coldharbour to anybody who stops at that arch for forty years — about nine men, not one of whom he knows, and not one piece of paper in this kingdom with it in it except a man's mouth.** **He said it twice in a field on the fifth of Greenmonth with nobody in earshot, and about seventeen days later a man with a barrow said it in a market, and the difference between those two facts is the whole of Block 2's problem.**

13. **The five words in from off a cart, and the one question that has not been answered.** About nine days' ride away, a man of about thirty-one with a canvas bag: there is a better place to put the words and he will know where it is and he will not say. **From a road, a man of about thirty-four on a coast about two days off, nine words and no letter and no seal and a place in the county on the back in a hand nobody recognised: whether the first of the eight is still being done anywhere.** **The keeper of records read it standing up in about four seconds and did not answer it for about a minute and a half. Batch 0002 may answer it and may not, and if it is answered the answer may not come from her, because her refusing to answer it is the only thing she has done wrong.**

14. **The three things this block must not do, inherited and confirmed.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled to reopen and nobody was sent to find out. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure, and the man of thirty-two with a limp is not put in it. **The about eleven hundred are on nothing, the register is not destroyed and not withdrawn, the two villages are not put back on a road and are not told why, the corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on, and its rate is not a figure.**

15. **The want of the woman of about twenty-nine, and it was not restated and must not be.** **She is in one room for one day, in Chapter 402, and is not asked anything, and a man of about thirty-four asks her what she makes of it in the ordinary way and she says she has nothing and he says sorry, and nobody asks her twice and the chapter says there is a form in this kingdom for asking a person a question and that the form is not the reason. That is all. Nothing more is printed about it in this block and nothing more may be printed about it in Block 0002.**

16. **The barred tokens and the denominators, counted before they are enforced.** Across the ten new files, whole word, every hit read: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` and the barred kin-term are **all at zero**. The kin-term is at **2** across four hundred and ten chapters and **is spent, and no Volume 09 chapter may print a third.** `hemp`, `thirty-six`, `fifty-two`, `sixty-one` and `sixty-six` rest on denominators that are wrong by a factor of between ten and a hundred and twenty across the whole manuscript, and **a Block 0002 writer counts before it enforces and does not inherit a prohibition out of a figure it has not checked.** The word that names the founding paper is at zero and is barred and **may not be printed in Volume 09 at all, including by a character who is quoting a document.**

17. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets.** Block 0001 is **22,935 in 10 chapters**; the manuscript is **1,312,189 in 410**. `about` **670 in 22,935, 29.21 per 1,000** on a whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, which is this project's declared basis and is a third above Volume 08 Block 0005's 26.79 and **is not a figure to steer to and is not a figure to bring down.** `the whole of` **35**. `about four people` **3**. `about four hundred chapters` **0**, and *this volume*, *this book* and *this chapter* **0** with the bare noun tested. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons **0, 0, 0**; one em dash. `---` rules **0**. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. The retired reporting frame at **0** with a hard stop. **A style quota and a continuity rule are different instruments, this project ran one as the other for four blocks of Volume 08, and the block that lifted the quotas is the block this one was written to follow.**

18. **And what the successor must not do, in one list.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper. Do not resolve the shut road, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or the want of the woman of about twenty-nine. Do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach in a scene and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley or the press's opening day. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater, and the living hamlet's near-sixty and the erased eighty may not be printed together or compared on the page. Do not print a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word; Stage 4 is half-spent, the qualification is owing in Volume 10, and the ledger does not move. Do not add a tenth asking, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry or a nineteenth speech figure to a class that is at its number. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not schedule the seed vault, the roadmark, the mechanism, the bell's device or Chapter 900. Do not spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic before Block 3, do not spend the mason's seventh-line-of-a-stone by pointing at it, and do not have anybody explain the ninth mark. And do not improve on a want: eleven, four, nine, seven, the four inches, the hundred and twenty days, the hundred and seventy days, the three hundred and seventy and the four hundred are on the page in about four hundred and ten chapters and each of them is said once.**

## Threads Volume 09 Batch 0002 carries to Volume 09 Batch 0003

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Batch 0002 and it is Batch 0003's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, and Batch 0002 is its predecessor. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing here renumbers anything above it and nothing here resolves anything Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 left open.** Eighteen threads, and the eighteenth is what the successor must not do.

1. **The frame, derived twice, and the fact that the year does not turn inside Volume 09.** Day 973, the eighth of Longlight YR 318, a Monday, to day 1014, the nineteenth of Rainmonth YR 318, a Sunday. Two hundred and nine days elapsed, two hundred and ten inclusive, five blocks of forty-two days: 931–972, 973–1014, 1015–1056, 1057–1098, 1099–1140. **The whole of Volume 09 is YR 318**; Thawmonth 1 YR 318 is day 876 and Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236. Market days are Saturdays and Thursdays, sixty in the volume at twelve a block, thirty of them Saturdays; **Batch 0002's twelve are 976, 978, 983, 985, 990, 992, 997, 999, 1004, 1006, 1011 and 1013, re-derived from the same anchor and not read off a card.** **Batch 0003 is days 1015 to 1056, the twentieth of Rainmonth to the first of Fallowmonth, and Batch 0003's ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived and not read off a card.** Month boundaries remaining inside the volume: 1026, 1056, 1086 and 1116.

2. **The song, canon, in full, and unchanged, and the ninth word is out.** Nine lines: **Ask the far side. Two books. Never a person twice. Read it yourself. Say it in a room. Leave the name off. Anybody may come. Feed the stranger first.** The ninth is **Coldharbour**, a road, and about forty people heard it in a market on day 957 and about nine of them heard it properly and one man of about thirty has it on the back of his own hand under a bar of soap and nothing over it. **Batch 0003 may not add a tenth line, may not un-say the ninth, may not have anybody regret it in a speech, and may not print the volume's twenty-word engine sentence again, which is said once, in Chapter 407, and which Chapter 419 deliberately did not reprint.** The word that names the founding paper is at zero and is barred and **may not be printed in this volume at all, including inside a quoted document or in a character's mouth.**

3. **The four governances, as Batch 0002 established them, and the answer each one gave.** **Hereditary custody**, a fold thirty miles south, a boundary of two miles and nine stones, a man of about forty-one who has held it since he was eleven, a boy of nine told in nine words on day 1004 that he does not have to, and about two hundred people who will know by the end of the year and not one of them knows why. **A rotating clerk**, the fen bench that sits on Saturdays, a man of about fifty-eight on his second year with four months left, a clerk who is a year of a man's life, about eighteen men in that county who have been the clerk of it before him, about eleven who did not want to be and not one of the eleven said so in the book. **A public lot**, a room over a store in a township of about nine hundred people, a hat anybody may put a name in, about nine names a year, a chair that is empty three months in every twelve, nobody in that township can say which three, and a man of about thirty-eight seven miles off whose name stayed in the hat and who had already said no to the chair in a kitchen nine miles off and does not know either of those things. **No gate at all**, a dale of about nine hundred people, a hurdle put across twice a day, and nine words: a man with a form stayed here four years. **None of the four is wrong, none refused the protagonist, and none is improved on in Batch 0003.**

4. **The column, and what is on it now, and where.** Four of the eight laid side by side in chalk on a bench-board in a room over a cooper's shop that is let by the day and whose door is not locked, with four heads — what the thing is, where it is, how often, and **who holds it** — and about nine inches of clean board under the fourth head, because the protagonist wiped the four names out with the flat of his hand on day 1013 and left the heads. **He said that a column with a head on it and nothing under it is a column anybody can put a name in later, and that this is the worst of four possibilities.** **The column is the single most dangerous object in the volume right now, because it is a thing on a board in a room that the next man with a form can pick up, and Batch 0003 is the block in which an office with a column invents a place, a figure and a trade for a road in a fortnight.** A Batch 0003 writer must be able to name the mechanism by which the invention happens and must not have anybody in Batch 0003's block notice the bench-board, because the two weeks are too short.

5. **The question from a road, still unanswered, and the answer in a field.** Nine words, no letter, no seal, a place in the county on the back, from a man of about thirty-four about two days off a coast, asking **whether the first of the eight is still being done anywhere.** In a drawer with about four hundred other returns since day 971, about six weeks by day 1014. **She has not answered it and Batch 0003 may answer it and may not, and if it is answered the answer may not come from her, because her not answering it is the only thing she has done wrong in this volume.** **The answer exists: a mason of about sixty-eight has asked the far side about four hundred times, the last time of all on day 1002 at a drain arch in a wall in the next village, and he has said the words to about nine men in forty years and not one of them has come back and he has not stopped asking.** Nobody carried it from the field to the drawer and nobody is going to.

6. **The fifth place, the byre, and the three sheets.** A man of about fifty-three, about nine acres, a byre of about thirty feet by about fifty, nine milking stalls, a shelf above the pails, **three sheets folded in four by three different hands, the newest about a fortnight old, and he has never known what they are for and has never asked and has not thrown them away, because a sheet of paper with writing on it is not his to throw away.** His wife read the newest out loud twice, once in front of a man of about twenty-two looking for a hoe, and about four people in that county have now heard eight words out loud in a byre this year. **A list of nine places is not a distribution and is not a list of people, and that is the block's finding and nobody has said it to anybody.**

7. **The sheet in the coat that is not his.** The man of about thirty-three with a barrow was handed a sheet at the last moment on day 999 by a woman of twenty-nine who put no head on it and did not say where it went, and **he has not opened it and is not going to, and he was carrying it on day 1007 when he stopped nine feet off a bar four miles west of Marl, looked at a different sheet under a stone, did not ask what it was, and could not say afterwards why he did not ask.** **About eight days carried as at day 1007 and about three weeks as at Batch 0003's early days, and any figure of it in Batch 0003 must be derived from that and not invented.**

8. **The bar and its three figures, and the day that is not paid for.** Nine feet of oak, nine pence a day for three days in four of the four up-days in seven, and about a hundred and thirty up-days and about ninety-eight paid days and about ninety-eight twists of paper at day 1007 out of two hundred and twenty-eight days since the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth. **Nine pence for about ninety-eight days is eight hundred and eighty-two pence, which is eighteen marks and eighteen pence.** **A Sunday is the day he is not paid for and is about six times as busy.** The sheet with nine figures and no head has been under a stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven since day 949. **His four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth, and Batch 0002 did not say it to him, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it, four of them wrong by a day, and not one of them has said a word and nobody is going to be the first.** **The three bars remain three objects and the kerb bar and the market bar at Marl were named in Chapter 418 only to be held apart.**

9. **The two collections with no head on them, untouched, and the ninth mark nobody may explain.** Four feet of board, about three hundred and seventy figures at nine to the inch, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns. About four hundred entries, four years, a boy of seventeen who can read figures and not words. About nine marks in common and a ninth that neither of them can read. **Batch 0002 did not touch either collection and neither may Batch 0003, nobody may explain the ninth mark, nobody may ask the boy to read his book out loud, and `outline/volume-09.md` §10 item 5 makes his arithmetic the identification of the sealed path in Block 3 and not before.**

10. **The clerk of about sixty and his two kinds of minute and no third.** A walk: who came, in what order, which side of a hedge they stood on. A figure: what was cut into the stone and when. **About four inches of a stone in a hedge is in his head and in neither minute, and a man of about sixty-seven who farms the near field is the reason it was eleven years overdue and nobody has ever asked him.** **Batch 0002 did not schedule a second renewal and neither may Batch 0003; the first of Longlight is next at day 1141, which is outside this volume, and a Batch 0003 writer who wants a stone must find another stone.**

11. **The yard, the man of twenty-five, and the breach, untouched and unrepaired.** A yard of nine men four hundred yards up a road, a barrow and two men and a length of board that went four miles for a day on the first of Longlight because a barrow going four miles is not a job anybody has to think about, and three sentences in about four hours all of them about load. **The breach is open, nobody walks a road with anybody, nobody says the word for what the two of them are, and the barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and twenty chapters and is spent and Batch 0003 spends no third.** **The far end of the span built over nineteen mornings is a hand's width out and will be for about two hundred years and must not be reported as a repair.**

12. **The three things this block must not do, inherited and confirmed.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled to reopen, and nobody is sent to find out; about nine people are on the wrong side of it and the word *failed* is used of nothing. The offer is unanswered, is not answered with a figure, and the man of about thirty-two with a limp is not put in it. **The about eleven hundred are on nothing, the register is not destroyed and not withdrawn, the two villages are not put back on a road and are not told why, the corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on and its rate is not a figure and is not printed, and the hearing and the quarantine instrument both stand.**

13. **The want of the woman of about twenty-nine, still not restated, and now two people.** She is in one room for one day in Chapter 402 and is not asked anything. In Chapter 419 a woman of about thirty-nine with a stall of hinges and a daughter of about eleven says, in the ordinary way, that she has asked for a list of things twice and been given a form, and that a form has a head on it that says the name of the person who is to do it, and that she is not talking about the column and nobody in that room knows she is. **He did not answer her and nobody asked her twice. Batch 0003 may not restate either want, may not explain the first, may not make the second about the column, and may not have either woman thank anybody.**

14. **The bar figures, the two columns and the twenty-four unprinted referents, counted before they are enforced.** Across the ten new files, whole word, every hit read: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` and the barred kin-term are all at zero, and so are `stage`, `ledger`, `qualification`, `system` and `panel`. The kin-term is at **2** across four hundred and twenty chapters and **is spent.** `hemp`, `thirty-six`, `fifty-two`, `sixty-one` and `sixty-six` rest on denominators that are wrong by a factor of between ten and a hundred and twenty across the whole manuscript, and **a Batch 0003 writer counts before it enforces and does not inherit a prohibition out of a figure it has not checked.** The two nines — the day-book two hundred miles off and the page under the fourth step — are separate, are never added, and neither is added to a figure of persons. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent.

15. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets.** Batch 0002 is **19,774 in ten chapters**; the manuscript is **1,331,965 in four hundred and twenty chapters**. `about` **614 in 19,774, which is 31.05 per 1,000** on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis, above Batch 0001's 29.21 and not a figure to steer to and not a figure to bring down. `the whole of` **29**. `about four people` **4**. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes **0, 0, 0, 0**. `---` rules **0**. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. The retired reporting frame at **0** with a hard stop and no occurrence converted into another form. Ten money columns at **16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56 and 70 pence**, **nine distinct totals in ten columns, the one repeat being the fold's own year printed twice on purpose, and none of them one of the thirty-two recurring totals or one of Batch 0001's four**; **and the pair of a boy's boots at twenty-six pence is a line item outside the seventy in both chapters, printed separately in both and added to neither, and it is not an eleventh column. Eleven printed speech-length figures, eleven footed, and a twelfth was found uncounted by the block's own audit and is now on the page and in the ledger.** **A style quota and a continuity rule are different instruments, this project ran one as the other for four blocks of Volume 08, and the block that lifted the quotas is the block these two were written to follow, and the two blocks that followed it have both found their defects by reading the files and not by measuring them.**

16. **The two defects this block's own hand found, and the standing lesson, which is now nine batches running.** **Ten things that no sweep could see, in ten chapters, and every one of them was found by a person reading the files: two barred numerals that had been printed as money totals, two speech figures stated low by two and twenty-eight words, a man of about thirty-eight with a drove who was nine miles off one place and a day and a half off another, a future month used for a past date, a set of nine stones that did not foot, a woman of about forty-four doubled across two counties, two men of about thirty-four in a room called the same thing, a mason of about sixty-eight described as not having been in a room in about eleven years in the same batch in which he is in one, and a line of a woman's want in Chapter 414 that repeated a Chapter 402 beat and was cut because Thread 13 forbids it.** **Five instrument passes and a bold-parity sweep had returned clean on those sites and a reader who had not written them found every one inside a day.** **A repair pass is not a safer thing to review than a batch, a script that edits prose is not a repair pass until a person has read the result, and a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.**

17. **The one System panel of the volume, and it belongs to Batch 0003.** `outline/volume-09.md` §4 and §8 Block 3: **one panel, in Block 3, at a threshold, one line, technically true, concealing a moral consequence, and it does not tell him what to do.** **Batch 0002 printed no panel and printed zero for *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification*, and Batch 0003 prints the volume's one and does not print a second.** Stage 4 is half-spent, the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and the ledger does not move.

18. **And what the successor must not do, in one list.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper. Do not print a tenth line of the song, do not add a second System panel, and do not print a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word. Do not resolve the shut road, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine. Do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach in a scene and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater, and the living hamlet's near-sixty and the erased eighty may not be printed together or compared on the page. Do not add a tenth asking, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry or an eleventh printed speech figure to a class that is at its number. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not schedule the seed vault, the bell's device, a second oath-stone renewal or Chapter 900. Do not spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic before the identification is made, do not point at the mason's mark, and do not have anybody explain the ninth mark. Do not have anybody soften the leak or un-say the ninth word. Do not have anybody agree with the nine words that say a column turns a thing into a man. And do not improve on a want: eleven, four, nine, seven, the four inches, the hundred and twenty days, the two hundred and twenty-eight days, the three hundred and seventy and the four hundred are on the page in about four hundred and twenty chapters and each of them is said once.**

---

## Threads Volume 09 Batch 0003 carries to Volume 09 Batch 0004

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Batch 0003 and it is Batch 0004's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, and Batch 0003 is its predecessor. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing here renumbers anything above it and nothing here resolves anything Batch 0001, Batch 0002 or Batch 0003 left open.** Eighteen threads, numbered from nineteen so that no number in this file is used twice, and the last is what the successor must not do.

19. **The frame, derived twice, and the fact that five of Batch 0003's ten chapter days were in Harvestmonth and eight of its thirteen datelines were.** Day 1015, the twentieth of Rainmonth YR 318, a Monday, to day 1056, the first of Fallowmonth YR 318, a Sunday, forty-two days inclusive, five blocks of forty-two days: 931–972, 973–1014, 1015–1056, 1057–1098, 1099–1140. **The whole of Volume 09 is YR 318**; Thawmonth 1 YR 318 is day 876 and Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236. **Batch 0003's chapter days were 1016, 1017, 1019, 1020, 1029, 1031, 1033, 1034, 1038 and 1056, which are a Tuesday, a Wednesday, a Friday, a Saturday, a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday, a Saturday, a Wednesday and a Sunday, and five of them were given as days of Rainmonth in the first printing and all five are days of Harvestmonth, because Harvestmonth begins at 1026.** **Seven weekday labels in four of those chapters were wrong in the first printing and are now right, and the day of month and the day number were never wrong: derive the weekday from the day *number* and the day of month from the day *number*, and never one from the other, because a check that returns a single figure for both halves is one derivation and not two.** **Batch 0003's twelve market mornings are 1018, 1020, 1025, 1027, 1032, 1034, 1039, 1041, 1046, 1048, 1053 and 1055, re-derived.** **Batch 0004 is days 1057 to 1098, the second of Fallowmonth to the thirteenth of Embermonth, and its ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived and not read off a card.** Month boundaries remaining inside the volume: 1086 and 1116. **And `outline/volume-09.md` §17's calendar table gives 1098, the thirteenth of Embermonth, as a Saturday and 1099 as a Sunday; on the carried thirty-day month from the day-930 anchor, 1098 is a Sunday and 1099 is a Monday, and the same table already gives 965, the thirtieth of Greenmonth, as a Wednesday when it is a Sunday. The block's own datelines foot without either row, and the 1098 and 1099 rows are in the block's own range, so the next block must derive the last day and not copy it.**

20. **The three invented things, with the day each was entered and the person who entered it, and the standing instruction that none of the three is a lie.** A county form with **three heads cut about forty years ago in a room two counties off by a man who is not alive: a place, a figure and a trade.** **A place, `Coldharbour`, entered on the twenty-second of Rainmonth, day 1017, by a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, from a man of about forty-four who farms about forty acres and has a ditch at the bottom of it where a boat used to come up, and who said, in seventeen words, *It was a harbour once and it is my field now and both of those are true*.** The field is about two hundred yards off a made road; a lane off that road crosses it, goes to a stile and goes on half a mile to join the nine-mile road. **A figure of persons, `four hundred and seventy`, entered on the twenty-fourth of Rainmonth, day 1019, by the same man, from a day and a half of counting at that stile by about nine people, none of them on a roll; the nine figures are fifty-eight, forty-four, thirty-nine, seventy-one, thirty-three, sixty-two, forty-seven, sixty and fifty-six, and the count is honest and counts people crossing a lane to go down nine miles of a road, and there are about thirty-four people in the field and the lane.** **A trade, `salt carting`, entered on the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth, day 1020, a Saturday and a market day, by the same man, from two carriers he asked in a yard in Merefen in about nine minutes.** The form went out on the next fixed Saturday, day 1027, in the middle of a band of about a hundred and four sheets tied with a cord, and by day 1056 it is being said in about nine houses in four counties by people who are not looking at a form. **Batch 0004 may not remove any of the three, may not make any of them a lie, may not make any of them a forgery, may not have anybody correct any of them, and there is no villain to catch and nobody is going to be brought to anything.**

21. **The form itself and what is on it, because the office that wrote it is the office that wrote Block 4's receipt, and a writer who forgets that loses the volume's argument.** A form with three heads, a place, a figure and a trade, and no line for a name and no line for anything else. **A second form behind it with a head that takes a trade, and a trade is how a man is found**, and a man of about thirty-four asked the protagonist in Chapter 428 for somebody's trade against the place, and the argument for it was good and was entirely in his own interest. **A clerk of about nineteen, in his second year, in a room over a shop in a street four hundred yards long in a fen town of about four thousand people, is the only person in that room who can say what the form wants and is not the villain and nobody refused anything in that room.** About nine hundred sheets a month go out of that room into nine counties and about a hundred and four of them go out on a fixed Saturday in a band a hundred and eight with four taken out of it, and the two figures are counts of two different series and are not added. **A record with a place, a figure and a trade on it is exactly the thing this volume has spent thirty chapters arguing against, and `outline/volume-09.md` §11 says so out loud in connection with Block 4's receipt, and the successor must find the two of them meeting and must not have the protagonist notice it.**

22. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet, three more days after the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth, and nobody has stopped him and he is not a villain.** He said in the nine feet in front of a rail in Chapter 413 that a road has none of the three and that he would not say it twice, and he did not say it again, and a room did not stop him, and the room was not wrong to let him go on. He then found all three in four days and wrote all three down and did not compare them. **On the sixth of Harvestmonth he was at the gate of the field at about the fourth hour with a form rolled inside his coat, two hours early, waiting for a farrier, and he was not working and he was not going to say anything to anybody about it. On the ninth he asked the protagonist for somebody's trade against the place. On the thirteenth of Harvestmonth, day 1058's morning, the chapter 428 print says he is on a made road about nine miles off and has not spoken to the protagonist since the twenty-first of Rainmonth.** He is on the strength of a county's own road and answers to a man about eleven years older than him who does not know the sheet is in the band. **Batch 0004 may not convict him, may not have him explain himself, and may not turn him into the volume's antagonist, who is the marshal and is not named anywhere.**

23. **The man of about thirty-three with a barrow, and two pieces of paper in that county, and one of them is his and he has not opened it.** He was handed a fold of paper on the fixed day by a woman of twenty-nine who put no head on it and did not say where it went, and he put it in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his and has not opened it: about 8 days carried at day 1007, **37 days at day 1036, 38 days at day 1038.** He was asked about the invented thing **in a yard in a market town of about two thousand people on the salt on the fourth of Harvestmonth, day 1029, by a man of about fifty-three who takes loads up a made road and is not an officer and is on nobody's strength**, who told him a place with a figure on it gets carts put on it. **He asked the man of about nineteen at the bar four miles west of Marl what is on the sheet on that bench, on the thirteenth of Harvestmonth, day 1038, and it is the wrong question, and the boy said `I do not know and I cannot read and nobody has ever asked me either` — fifteen words — and was believed, and the boy did not ask him back what the far end of that road is called, which is the question the man with the barrow could have answered and the boy could not.** There are now two pieces of paper in that county with about nine figures on them and no head on them and a date at the bottom, and **not one person in four counties can read either of them.** The first has been under a stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven since the fourteenth of Greenmonth, about 89 days at day 1038; the second is in the inside pocket. **Batch 0004 may not have him open either, and may not have the boy read either, and may not have anybody ask him a third question.**

24. **The bar four miles west of Marl, its figures derived and not invented, and what is under a stone on a bench in a shed.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, down on the other three, and the man of about nineteen is paid **nine pence a day for three of the four up-days, and the one he is not paid for is the Sunday.** **The start of it is day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, and the three canon figures are 170 days on 949, 193 on 972 and 228 on 1007, all three foot, and Chapter 429's figure is 259 days on day 1038, which is thirty-seven weeks exactly, a hundred and forty-eight up-days and a hundred and eleven paid days, about a hundred and eleven twists, and nine hundred and ninety-nine pence printed outside the day's column and added to nothing.** A mark is forty-eight pence. The bundle behind the stove is about two hundred and nine days old and has not been opened. **His four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth and Batch 0003 did not say it to him, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it and not one of them has said a word, and Batch 0004 may not say it either.** A boy of about eleven with a pail stands on the shoulder of a ditch four hundred yards west at every hour of the daylight, and about two hundred carts a week go the long way round a quarter of a mile of it, and about two hundred people know exactly why and not one of them has ever started it. **The three bars remain three objects and the kerb bar and the market bar at Marl were not touched in this block.**

25. **The question from a road, unanswered for about eleven weeks, and the answer nine miles from a drawer.** Nine words, no letter, no seal, a place in the county on the back, from a man of about thirty-four about two days off a coast, asking **whether the first of the eight is still being done anywhere.** In a drawer with about four hundred other returns since the seventh of Longlight. **She has not answered it and Batch 0003 did not have her answer it and the protagonist stood in the same field the answer came from on the sixth of Harvestmonth and carried nothing back and wrote nothing in a notebook that is eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them.** The answer is a mason of about sixty-eight who has asked the far side about four hundred times, the last time of all on the seventh of Rainmonth at a drain arch in a wall in the next village, in nine words, to a stranger who had asked him a question about a job. **Nobody carried it from the field to the drawer and nobody is going to, and if Batch 0004 answers it the answer may not come from the keeper of records, because her not answering it is the only thing she has done wrong in this volume.**

26. **The one System panel of the volume, and it is spent.** `chapter-0426.md`, day 1031, at about the fourth hour, with a man's left hand flat on a dressed lintel about four feet long with nothing cut on it, and the hand warm at about the fourth hour as it is about nine days in ten and he has never told anybody. **One line, technically true, concealing a moral consequence, and it does not say what to do: *Every one of the three is true and that is why you cannot do anything with them.*** It is not a voice, not a wall and not a man in a room; nothing of it is in the notebook. **A System panel is not an explanation and this one explains nothing.** **Batch 0004 prints no second panel, and `stage`, `ledger` and `qualification` stay at zero, and Stage 4 is half-spent, the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and the ledger does not move and no chapter in Batch 0004 may say that it does.** The outline's crossing budget is one crossing in Block 4, and no cut is made in this volume and none may be scheduled.

27. **Ivo Serrat's arithmetic, what it identified, and what it did not open, and the tenth mark in a file two hundred miles off.** The boy is seventeen, has carried glass between kilns for four years, can read figures and not words, and has about four hundred entries in a book he made himself and has never shown anybody. **His own mark — the seven he has been making since he was thirteen — is in the county's invented figure, because the man of about thirty-four turned the boy's own piece of paper over on a counter on the twenty-fourth of Rainmonth and added the nine figures on the back of it, and a hand cannot be dated and a piece of paper cannot be asked who was standing there.** **The arithmetic is the identification of a road that is nine miles and is a day's walk and was shut about four hundred years ago, on no sheet in nine counties, and it is an arithmetic and not a crossing and nothing was opened: he has not been down the far half of it, nobody asked him to, and there is nothing at the end of it but a field with a gate on it.** The two collections now stand at **ten** marks in common, the tenth being in a file two hundred miles off, and **the person who could read the mark is a mason of about sixty-eight about forty miles off who does not know that either of them has it, and nobody in Batch 0004 may explain the mark, point at the mason's mark, or ask the boy to read his book out loud.**

28. **The four days of Chapter 428, and the four unpaid things, and nobody's remark.** The ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth of Harvestmonth, days 1034 to 1037. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wanted somebody's trade against the place. The man of about forty-four who farms Coldharbour wanted the figure of four hundred and seventy to stand, because a place with a figure on it gets its lane made up and the carts stop going round the quarter of a mile. The man of about thirty-three with a barrow wanted the sheet in his coat read. A mason of about sixty-eight wanted one sentence carried four miles.** He did none of the four and refused none of the four and said he would once and did not, and **nobody in any of the four places said one word to him about not doing it.** **The mason's top course is a hand's width out of level and will be a hand's width out of level next spring, and a man of about fifty-one laying a drain arch four miles off is going to be free on the first of a month, and nobody has told either of them about the other, and the first of a month inside the volume is day 1086 and Batch 0004 must not turn that into a meeting.** The farmer's lane is not made up. **Batch 0004 may not remark on any of the four, may not have the protagonist print a word about them, and may not let him work out the moral of anything.**

29. **The four governances, as Chapter 411 found them and as Batch 0003 left them, and the fifth answer.** Hereditary custody, a fold thirty miles south, a boundary of two miles and nine stones, a man of about forty-one, a boy of nine told in nine words that he does not have to. A rotating clerk, the fen bench that sits on Saturdays, a man of about fifty-eight on his second year with four months left. A public lot, a room over a store in a township of about nine hundred people, a hat anybody may put a name in, a chair empty three months in every twelve. **No gate at all**, a hurdle across a dale lane, a man of about forty-four, nine words. **And a fifth answer, a room that is let by the day and whose keeper goes home, which is the protagonist's own bench-board room over a cooper's shop and which Batch 0003 did not visit and had a tenant for about nine times in six weeks.** None of the four is wrong, none refused the protagonist, and **none is improved on, refused, ranked or made to lose in Batch 0004.** A place with no gate is not a refusal.

30. **The song, canon, in full, unchanged, and the ninth word is out and is not going back.** Nine lines: **Ask the far side. Two books. Never a person twice. Read it yourself. Say it in a room. Leave the name off. Anybody may come. Feed the stranger first.** The ninth is **Coldharbour**, a road, and about forty people heard it in a market on day 957 and about nine of them heard it properly and one man of about thirty has it on the back of his own hand under a bar of soap and nothing over it. **Batch 0004 may not add a tenth line, may not un-say the ninth, may not have anybody regret it in a speech, and may not reprint the volume's twenty-word engine sentence, which is said once, in Chapter 407.** The word that names the founding paper is at zero and is barred and **may not be printed in this volume at all, including inside a quoted document or in a character's mouth.** The ninth line on the bench-board is still ruled and empty, four hands have ruled it, and **nobody in Batch 0004 may find that board, read it, tidy it, improve on it or explain it.**

31. **The protagonist's sheet, and it is the block's last act and it is a cost.** **Nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and against each of the three the name of a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and nothing else.** It is the only record in the world of when the three things were entered and by whom, it is in the inside pocket of a coat, and **he is not going to give it to anybody.** It was written on the eleventh day of Harvestmonth in the eighth hour of an evening on a bench on a made road, about three weeks before the first of Fallowmonth. **There are about nine people in nine counties who could have written that sheet and about four of them have thought about writing it; the clerk of about nineteen could produce the office's own out of a file in a quarter of an hour and would not think of it; and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet does not know it exists.** **Batch 0004 may not have him give it away, may not have him lose it, may not have it read, and may not have a clerk of about nineteen ask him for it, because a copy of it in that office would make him a person with a copy.**

32. **The two collections with no head on them, untouched, and the arithmetic that is now spent.** Four feet of board, about three hundred and seventy figures at nine to the inch, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns. About four hundred entries, four years, a boy of seventeen. **They now stand at ten marks in common and the tenth is in a file two hundred miles off.** **Batch 0004 may not explain the tenth, may not ask the boy to read the book, and may not re-spend the identification, which is made and identified and closed.** The woman's want is not restated, nobody is thanked, and the board is not read out.

33. **The two figures of the room and the two nines and the other nines, all still separate.** About nine hundred sheets a month out of the room over the weigh-house into nine counties, and about a hundred and four in one band on the fixed day, which is a Saturday, and a band is a hundred and eight with four taken out of it, and the cart goes out twice a month and is back a fortnight later. **The two are counts of two different series, neither is derived from the other, and no chapter may add them or convert one into the other.** The day-book is at nine, the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is at nine, the register is at seven questions and no findings, **no two of the nines are added and none is added to a figure of persons**, and the unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and no chapter prints a fifth.

34. **The three things Batch 0004 must not do, inherited and confirmed.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled to reopen, and nobody is sent to find out; about nine people are on the wrong side of it and the word *failed* is used of nothing. The offer is unanswered, is not answered with a figure, and the man of thirty-two with a limp is not put in it. **The about eleven hundred are on nothing, the register is not destroyed and not withdrawn, the two villages are not put back on a road and are not told why, the corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on and its rate is not a figure and is not printed, and the hearing and the quarantine instrument both stand.** The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero, and the valley is unreachable.

35. **The want of the woman of about twenty-nine and of the woman of about thirty-nine, still not restated.** The first is in one room for one day in Chapter 402, is not asked anything, and says she has nothing. The second is in one room in Chapter 419 and said, in the ordinary way, that she has asked for a list of things twice and been given a form, and that a form has a head on it that says the name of the person who is to do it, and that she is not talking about the column and nobody in that room knows she is. **He did not answer her and nobody asked her twice. Batch 0004 may not restate either want, may not explain the first, may not make the second about the column or about the three invented things, and may not have either woman thank anybody.**

36. **The barred tokens and the denominators, counted before they were enforced, and the measurements this block leaves.** Across the ten new files, whole word, every hit read and not only counted: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` and the barred kin-term are all at zero, and so are `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` and the four barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six`, **and the barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and thirty chapters and is spent.** `hemp`, `thirty-six`, `fifty-two`, `sixty-one` and `sixty-six` rest on denominators that are wrong by a factor of between ten and a hundred and twenty across the whole manuscript, and **a Batch 0004 writer counts before it enforces.** The measurements, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets: Batch 0003 is **16,904 in ten chapters**; the manuscript is **1,348,869 in four hundred and thirty chapters**; the volume is **59,615 in thirty chapters**. `about` **489 in 16,904, which is 28.93 per 1,000** on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis, below Batch 0002's 31.05 and Batch 0001's 29.21 and **not a figure to steer to and not a figure to bring down.** `the whole of` **32**. `about four people` **3**. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes **0, 0, 0, 0**. `---` rules **0**. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. **Ten money columns, ten footed, ten distinct totals at 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144 and 34 pence, none of them one of the forty recurring totals and none of them one of Batch 0002's ten.** **Seven printed speech-length figures, seven footed, none preceded by *about*.** **The name budget is three of five spent and this block spent none, and a capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns only `Ivo Serrat` and one sentence opening.**

37. **And what the successor must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the eighteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a tenth line of the song, do not un-say the ninth word, do not soften the leak, and do not have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not print a second System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not answer the question from a road and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not repair the breach between the two men in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater, and the living hamlet's near-sixty and the erased eighty may not be printed together or compared on a page. Do not add a tenth asking of that bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry or a ninth mark's explanation. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not schedule the seed vault, the bell's device, a second oath-stone renewal or Chapter 900. Do not re-spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic, do not point at the mason's mark, and do not have anybody ask the boy to read his book out loud. Do not remove, correct, contradict or soften the three invented things, and do not make any of them a lie or a forgery. Do not have anybody find the bench-board, read it, tidy it or improve on it, and do not improve on Chapter 419's four heads and nine inches of clean board. Do not have the protagonist overrule anybody, hand out his own sheet, say a word about the four days he spent not doing four things, or work out the moral of anything. Do not make a man of about thirty-four with a sheet into a villain. Do not turn the first of a month into a meeting between a mason of about sixty-eight and a man of about fifty-one. And do not improve on a want.**

---

## Threads Volume 09 Batch 0004 carries to the Volume 09 close

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Block 0004 and it is the close's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan and is binding, and the Volume 08 close record and `state/continuity.md` items 1066 to 1080 are the facts. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Thirty-one threads, numbered from thirty-eight so that no number in this file is used twice, and the last is what the close must not do.**

38. **The frame, derived twice, and the two halves reported as two numbers.** **Block 0004 is days 1057 to 1098, the second day of Fallowmonth to the thirteenth of Embermonth YR 318, forty-two days inclusive, and the ten chapter days were 1057, 1057 and 1058, 1062, 1068, 1071, 1074, 1078, 1080, 1084 and 1098, which are a Monday, a Monday and a Tuesday, a Saturday, a Friday, a Monday, a Thursday, a Monday, a Wednesday, a Sunday and a Sunday. The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately and are 0 and 0, and the market-morning claim is 0, as three figures and not one.** **The close is days 1099 to 1140, and its ten chapter days are the close's to choose inside that range, derived and not read off a card. Day 1099, the fourteenth of Embermonth, is a Monday and not a market day, and day 1116, the first of Wolfmonth, is a Thursday and a market morning, and day 1140, the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, is a Sunday and is the volume's last day and is not a market day.** Month boundaries remaining: 1116. **The whole of Volume 09 is YR 318 and the year does not turn.** **`outline/volume-09.md` §17's rows for 1098 and 1099 are both one day out and were not copied, and the 965 row is at `state/continuity.md` 1047; the close derives its own days and does not copy any of the three.**

39. **The close's frame, from the outline, and what it is not.** `outline/volume-09.md` §8 Block 5: **nine people in a room, each of whom has said no to something the other eight wanted, agreeing to a loose council rather than a single office, and a community asking him to leave and him leaving, and the ninth word in a mouth and not in a song, and the volume's last page a Sunday the county does not trade.** **And §10: the council's own first act is to agree that nothing about it goes on any sheet, and the word that names the founding paper is not printed anywhere in this volume, and the volume says so out loud.** **The volume's last page is a room with nine people in it and not a road with nobody on it, and the same weekday as Volume 08's close with the opposite contents, and that is a rhyme and not a copy.** **The close may not date the bell, may not date a press, may not renew an oath-stone, may not schedule a cut, and may not schedule the seed vault or Chapter 900.**

40. **Condition three is earned and closed, and the fourth is not.** **A transfer witnessed and not conducted, Chapter 437, day 1078: the protagonist was offered one end of a handcart thirty feet from a gate and refused in one word, and the reason he gave no reason is that all four of the possible reasons were about himself, and a man who helped is a man who was there on purpose, and a witness is a thing a person is and not a thing a person is put in.** He did not conduct the transfer, did not get the sheets back, did not overrule anybody, and was not the reason it worked. **The fourth condition, which is not one of the three and is the volume's own, is that he is asked to leave and he goes, and the ledger does not move, and no chapter says the ledger does not move.** The close owns that leaving. **The close may not re-open the witnessing and may not re-litigate the refusal.**

41. **The receipt, the form, and the one thing the close is for.** **The volume's second receipt, in a fair hand, accurate, in nine counties, left under a door with the door off its sill, and it has a place on it and a figure of men on it and a trade on it in that order, and a name at the foot of it.** **Those three things are the same three things as the three heads on a form cut about forty years ago in a room two counties off by a man who is not alive, and the chapter that lets one man see both of them at once is Chapter 437 and he has said one word about it to nobody in forty-one days and there is nobody he could say it to.** **A record with a place, a figure and a trade on it is exactly the thing this volume has spent forty chapters arguing against, and `outline/volume-09.md` §11 puts that on the page in connection with this receipt.** **The close may print that sentence again in a room and may not have anybody agree with it, and may not have the nine of them put a place, a figure and a trade on anything about themselves.** The receipt cannot be given back and the sheets cannot be returned and the taking is not reversed in the close.

42. **The eleven, and the decision not to make a list, and what the close inherits from it.** **Eleven people in eleven places, nine counties, about four weeks, no two of them ever in a room with one another, connected by a man of thirty-two with a limp who is on no list in any of the nine.** A woman of about forty-four at a kiln; a man of about forty-one at a boundary; a mason of about sixty-eight at an arch; a woman of about fifty at a roadhouse with a board; a woman of about thirty-seven at a roadhouse with a plate; a man of about thirty-three with a barrow; a man of about nineteen at a bar; a man of about twenty-six who carts salt; a man of about twenty-two with a handcart; a woman of about fifty-eight who sweeps a yard; and Ivo Serrat, seventeen. **He has decided, at the eleventh hour of a Sunday in a room taken by the day, not to tell one of them about another of them and not to make a list, and the reason is printed and is not a reason anybody would call decent, because a list of eleven people in nine places is a column.** **A council of nine that agrees nothing about itself goes on any sheet is the close's direct answer to that decision, and the close may make that connection explicit in one line and may not have him explain it.**

43. **The boy, the book, and the arithmetic, all spent.** **Ivo Serrat, seventeen, carried glass between kilns for four years, can read figures and not words, has about four hundred entries in a book he made himself out of sheets he buys and folds, and has never shown it to anybody in four years. On the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth, day 1084, he counted nine worn places on the lower board of a nine-foot shelf, worked out that a string does not wear a board, and worked out that nine of about forty is about three hundred and sixty, and said it out loud to nobody in twenty-two words, and wrote it at the end of the book on a page of figures with no head on it.** **The book has not been shown to anybody and the man of thirty-two with a limp was in that yard at about the seventh hour and did not see him count the board and will not be told.** **The close may not ask the boy to read the book, may not show the book, and may not re-spend the arithmetic, which is made, identified and closed, and it was the last time a number did any work in nine counties in this volume.** The two collections stand at ten marks in common, the tenth in a file two hundred miles off, and nobody has explained the tenth and the close may not.

44. **The woman of about fifty and the four figures, spent and not to be re-opened.** **Four feet of board, no head on it, about three hundred and seventy figures, thirty-one years, three hands, four of them in the hand of the man who wrote the receipt, and a woman who cannot read who matched a hand in about nine minutes and is the only person alive who could say which of the three hundred and seventy figures are his, and she has not said it and is not going to.** **Her want is not restated by the close, nobody thanks her, and the board is not read out and is not taken off the wall and is not improved on.** A figure with no head cannot be refused and cannot be handed back, and that is a property of the absence and not a rule anybody set.

45. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet, still not a villain, and the connection the volume refuses to print.** **He is one of the four men. He is also the man who invented a place, a figure and a trade for a road in three counties in four days in Batch 0003. The volume has never said whether those two facts are connected and never says it here, and the close may not say it, may not convict him, may not have him explain himself, and may not turn him into the volume's antagonist, who is the marshal and is not named anywhere.** He wrote the receipt and four copies of it at dusk on the second and does not know how many he wrote out, and he is not going to be told. **The three invented things stand: `Coldharbour`, entered on the twenty-second of Rainmonth; `four hundred and seventy`, entered on the twenty-fourth; `salt carting`, entered on the twenty-fifth, in YR 318. The close may not remove any of them, may not make any of them a lie, a forgery or a fiction, may not have anybody correct any of them, and may not have the receipt convict anybody.**

46. **The four men, what they took, and the fact that nobody is going to be found.** **A strength of four with a warrant and a paper carrying a seal and a name, and no lock to break, and about forty-one sheets counted twice off a shelf in three lots and into a satchel in three lots, six figures and not one of them the same twice, both counts foot.
