Nothing burnt, nothing lied, nobody asked anything, and an accurate receipt under a door.** On the twenty-third the building was cleared for a circuit: nine packets, a stool, a day-book and a dressed stone on two handcarts, about three hundred and sixty sheets uncounted, and a second line under the receipt that says the building was cleared and does not say what went out of it. **Nobody in nine counties can say who sent them, and the reason is on the page in Chapter 439 and is that the four men did not know what was on the shelf, and the man who sent them is a person who did not think about a shelf at all.** **The close may not name the sender, may not print a seal's office, may not bring anybody to anything, and may not resolve this.** The four men are not villains and the crowbar goes back on its bracket inside two minutes and that is the whole of it.

47. **The building, the slate and the woman who is not alive, and what the close may say about them.** **A two-room building of about twenty-two feet by eleven in a paved yard behind a house of assizes in a town of about nine hundred people, about four hundred years older than the yard wall, belonging to a company that wound up about nine years ago and kept by one woman for about seven years after that until she died in a winter, and never once on the clerk's schedule of nine things, because a list is a list of what somebody is answerable for.** A slate in the yard wall with a name in letters an inch high and a month, a day and a year under it and no line under those, which about nine people in that town could read. **The close may have the yard emptied, the building gone, the slate still in the wall, or the building let to somebody, and may not have anybody appointed to the building, and may not have the slate's name read out or explained, and may not say the word that names the founding paper on it.** Nobody knows why the company wound up and the close may not find out.

48. **The three that had to be there and the three that must not be, in this volume.** **There are nine practices. Held: seven, *Anybody may come*, at a yard of four kilns; four, *Read it yourself*, at an arch over a beck; three, *Never a person twice*, in a fold thirty miles south; eight, *Feed the stranger first*, at a roadhouse nine miles on, and the same keeper is nowhere near any of it. Two, *Two books*, is held by nobody and was refused by the keeper of records in about nine words. Five, *Say it in a room*, is at a public lot. Six, *Leave the name off*, is still not in a place on the page.** **The ninth line of the song is `Coldharbour` and it is out, and the close hands the ninth word to a person in a room who will say it and who is not a keeper of anything and is not on anything, and may not print a tenth line, may not un-say the ninth, may not have anybody regret it in a speech, and may not reprint the volume's twenty-word engine sentence, which was said once in Chapter 407.** Nothing is recalled.

49. **The four governances, and the fifth answer, none of them improved on.** Hereditary custody, a rotating clerk, a public lot, and no gate at all, and a fifth answer nobody had thought of, which is a room that is let by the day and whose keeper goes home. **None of the four is wrong and none of them was improved on, refused, ranked or made to lose in Block 0004, and the close may not improve on, refuse, rank or make lose any of them either.** A place with no gate is not a refusal. **A loose council of nine with no crown, no office and no register is not a fifth governance improved on, and the close may not present it as the answer to the other four.**

50. **The bar four miles west, and everything derived on it, and the two sheets in that county.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, nine pence a day for three of the four, the Sunday unpaid, **and the figure on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth is three hundred and one days, forty-three weeks exactly, a hundred and twenty-nine paid days, a hundred and twenty-nine twists, twenty-four marks and nine pence, cross-checked on the page from Chapter 429's two hundred and fifty-nine on the thirteenth of Harvestmonth and from the start at day 779.** **The man of about nineteen's four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth, and four blocks have now not said it to him, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it, and the close may say it and the volume's own instruction is that the close is the last place it could be said and it may not be said in a way that changes anything.** The sheet under the stone has been there since the fourteenth of Greenmonth, day 949, and is about a hundred and ninety-one days old on day 1140, and has not been read. The second sheet is in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his, has been since a fixed Saturday in Rainmonth, day 999, and is about a hundred and forty-one days old on day 1140, and was not opened on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth. **A third paper went into that coat on the second of Fallowmonth and was opened: a receipt in a fair hand, and he could do nothing with it. The close may not have either of the two opened, may not have the boy read either, and may not have anybody ask him a third question.** The three bars remain three objects and the close may not merge them, and only this one is touched.

51. **The protagonist's sheet, the notebook, and the eleven lines, and what the close may not do with either.** **His sheet is nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and one name against each, and it is the only record in the world of when the three things were entered and by whom, and it is in the inside pocket of a coat, and nobody in this block came near it.** **The notebook is a different object: eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them, the eleventh line never used, no twelfth line, and he has not added a line in forty chapters and does not add one on the twenty-third of Fallowmonth when he works out that he is the connection, or on the thirteenth of Embermonth when he decides not to make a list, and he turns it over once and puts it back in the coat.** **And the separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at in this volume and the close may not look at it, and the count of the looks at it is not printed.** **The close may not have him give the sheet away, lose it, or have it read, and may not have a clerk of about nineteen ask him for it, and may not have him write a word about the eleven people.** Two unreconciled numbers in his coat stand unreconciled.

52. **The kerb, the stage, the mechanism and the thirteen unlooked-at items, all still held.** **Stage 4 is half-spent and the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and a sweep of the ten new files returns 0 for *stage*, 0 for *ledger* and 0 for *qualification*, and the ledger does not move and no chapter in this block says that it does, and the close may not print a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word and may not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move.** **The mechanism is not explained. Nobody learns why he can cross, what a cut does to an anchor, what the roadmark is, why a standing light does not turn, or why the works under the abbey were rewritten. No cut is made in this volume and none is scheduled, and the one System panel was printed in Chapter 426 and is spent, and the close prints no panel, and a System panel is not an explanation.** **The thirteen unlooked-at items stay unlooked at, and the close adds none:** the seed vault and the keeper's decision; the nine bushels of barley in a four-foot aisle; the four hundred miles of shut road; the marginal instruction and whose words it is; either line in a stone and the length of either; the roadmark and what it is; the mechanism and why he can cross; the third word of the bell's tile; the number of persons the office will not print on an offer; the count of the looks at a leaf that says *a person*; the word in front of a place name; the name of the antagonist; and the account of the worst winter in eighteen years, which nobody asks to see and nobody offers a copy of.

53. **The shut road, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages and the corridor, all unmoved and all for the close to leave exactly where they are.** **The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled to reopen, and nobody is sent to find out; about nine people are on the wrong side of it in a place of about four hundred, and the word *failed* is not used about it or about anything.** The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure, and the condition was a person, and the steward refused the person, and the man of thirty-two with a limp was the only person the offer was open to and he did not put himself in it, and **the close may not resolve it and may not schedule its resolution.** The about eleven hundred are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The register stands with seven entries, all questions, none a finding. The two villages are not back on a road and are not told why. The corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on, and its rate is a rate nobody set, and **the close may not print the corridor's rate as a figure and may not let anybody put it in a column, and the reason the rate works is that nobody can check it.** The hearing stands. The quarantine instrument stands and is not repealed and the instrument is not dissolved.

54. **The city, the valley, and the three protected numerals, all at zero and all for the close to leave at zero.** **The city is four hundred miles off on a shelf and this volume has sent it nothing. Its three protected numerals are at zero across forty chapters and the close may not print a strip of paper, a room off a lane, a shelf eleven feet long, a mark in a corner, a lamp, a council of eleven, a man of fifty-two, a man of sixty-six, a man of sixty-one, nine bundles of cord and reed, or a child of nine years old.** The valley is unreachable and is not approached. **The close is the last ten chapters of the volume and it may not spend any of this.**

55. **The two nines, the register, and the unattributed statement, all separate and all spent where spent.** **The count on the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is nine and is not moved, no chapter enters a tenth asking of that bench, and the Crown schedule is not put to that bench. The day's book is at nine. The register is at seven questions and no findings. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and the close prints no fifth and prints none by quoting a close. No two of these nines are added and none is added to a figure of persons.** **Block 0004 introduced a clerk's schedule of nine things in a yard behind a house of assizes, which is a different nine and is a list of what a court is answerable for, and the two are not the same object and the close may not add them to each other and may not put a tenth thing on either.** About nine hundred sheets a month go out of a room over a shop into nine counties and about a hundred and four go out in one band on a fixed Saturday, and the two are counts of two different series and no chapter may add them or convert one into the other, and the close may not either.

56. **The two collections with no head on them, and the third collection he is carrying, and what the close may not do.** **Four feet of board with about three hundred and seventy figures and no head, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns. About four hundred entries, four years, a boy of seventeen, in a book he made himself. And the protagonist's nine inches by six with three lines and no head.** **All three are the same kind of thing and the close may not put them in one column, may not print them together, may not add their counts to each other, and may not have anybody read any of them out.** The two first stand at ten marks in common, the tenth in a file two hundred miles off. **The close may not print a third collection's figure beside either of the other two's**, and the count of four hundred and seventy people who went down a road is a different series from about nine hundred sheets a month and is not a figure of persons on any of the three.

57. **The song, the ninth word, and what may and may not be said aloud in the close.** **Nine lines, two parts, on the beat of a bar coming down on a rim, eight practices and one road. The ninth is `Coldharbour`, it is out, and about forty people heard it in a market on day 957 and about nine of them heard it properly.** **Nothing is recalled. No character may regret it in a speech. The close hands the ninth word to a person in a room who will say it again when asked, and that is the whole of what may happen to it, and the close may not un-say it, may not soften the leak, may not add a tenth line, and may not reprint the volume's twenty-word engine sentence.** **The word that names the founding paper is not printed in this volume in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and the close may not print it either, and the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule is not answered in prose.** **A close that cannot approach a rule without naming it leaves the naming to Volume 11 and does not break the bar to get there.**

58. **The breach, the span, and the word that is spent.** **The breach between the two men is open. Nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure. Nobody says the word for what the two of them are, and the word is printed nowhere in the volume and the barred kin-term is at two across four hundred and forty chapters and is spent.** **Nothing in Block 0004 repairs it in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, and the close may not repair it, may not report the span as a repair, and may not have either of the two men apologise, explain, or be in a room the other one is in on a matter that matters.** **Chapter 409 is three sentences about a barrow wheel and it is not a reconciliation, and a close that reads it as one has spent the volume's only reconciliation.**

59. **The keeper of records, the question from a road, and the four unpaid things, all still open and all the close's to leave or not.** **She is twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane, fourpence a year, a body corporate with a door, a number and the capacity to sue and to be sued, out of a deed of thirteen words. A list of nine places she keeps and has never shown, of which she named four of the eight off it and would not say where they are. The breach is open. Nobody walks a road with anybody.** **And she has not answered the one question that came back from a road, unanswered for about eleven weeks at day 1056 and about twenty-three weeks by day 1140, and her not answering it is the only thing she has done wrong in this volume, and the answer is a mason of about sixty-eight who has asked the far side about four hundred times, and nobody carried it from the field to the drawer and nobody is going to.** **If the close answers it, the answer may not come from her. The four unpaid things of Chapter 428 stand: the man of about thirty-four wanted somebody's trade, the man of about forty-four who farms `Coldharbour` wanted the figure of four hundred and seventy to stand, the man of about thirty-three wanted the sheet in his coat read, and a mason of about sixty-eight wanted one sentence carried four miles. Nobody remarked on any of them, and the close may not remark on any of them either, and may not turn the first of a month inside the volume into a meeting.** **The first of Fallowmonth has been and gone and the first of Embermonth has been and gone and the first of Wolfmonth is day 1116 and the close may not schedule anything for it.**

60. **The offer, the corridor, and the two women whose wants were never restated, one more time because they are the ones a close forgets.** **A woman of about twenty-nine is in one room for one day in Chapter 402, is not asked anything, and says she has nothing. A woman of about thirty-nine is in one room in Chapter 419 and said, in the ordinary way, that she has asked for a list of things twice and been given a form, and that a form has a head on it that says the name of the person who is to do it, and that she is not talking about the column and nobody in that room knows she is. He did not answer her and nobody asked her twice. Four blocks have not restated either want, have not explained the first, have not made the second about the column or about the three invented things, and have not had either woman thank anybody. The close may not restate either want, may not explain the first, may not make the second about the council, and may not have either woman thank anybody.** The council of nine agrees nothing about itself goes on any sheet, and that agreement is about the shape of the room and not about either woman's want, and the close may not join them.

61. **Hushwater, the press, the bell, the oath-stones, and the crossing budget, all declined and all for the close to decline again.** **Hushwater is not taken in this volume. The living hamlet's nearest estimate is about sixty and the erased village is eighty and the two figures may never be printed together or compared on a page, and the close may print neither and may not compare them.** The press inherits with no opening day and this volume may not date it, and the bell may be rung once and unexplained and nothing may be scheduled for it, and Chapter 900 depends on it. **No second oath-stone renewal may be scheduled; the first of Longlight was day 966 and it is gone.** **The crossing budget is about three attempted crossings a season and this volume has had one in Block 3 and none in Block 4 and none may be made in Block 5, and no cut is made in this volume and none may be scheduled.** Assembly travel is ordinary.

62. **The name budget, with the arithmetic, and the two slots that are left.** **The budget for Volume 09 is five. Batch 0001 spent three and Batches 0002, 0003 and 0004 spent none: 3 of 5, with 2 remaining, and both are available to the close.** The three are `Brenna Tull`, 44, `Saber Vahl`, 68, and `Ivo Serrat`, 17. **The close's share is at most two, and if it spends one it must say so in its own state entry with the arithmetic, and a capitalised two-word sweep over its ten files must return only established names with every hit read before the sweep is reported.** A name is the only asset in this manuscript that cannot be audited after the fact, which is the whole argument for spending five and not six.

63. **The measurements, and what they are, with every basis named, and the note that a state file which reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.** **Block 0004 is 18,097 in ten chapters, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added, never on a concatenated stream. The volume is 77,778 in forty chapters. The manuscript is 1,367,032 in four hundred and forty chapters.** `about` 386 in 18,097, which is 21.33 per 1,000, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator. `the whole of` 24. `about four people` 1. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes 0, 0, 0, 0. `---` rules 0. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. **Ten money columns, ten footed, ten distinct totals at 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68 and 69 pence, none of them one of the Batch 0002 tens or the Batch 0003 tens. Fourteen printed speech-length figures, thirteen of them footed in the block's first pass and all fourteen footed now, none preceded by *about*, and the one that was not was Chapter 431's second figure at fifteen against a printed exchange of twenty-two, which the close writer should treat as the standing example of a figure attached to a two-speaker exchange.** The twelve barred tokens and the four barred numerals at zero across the ten new files, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` at zero, and across four hundred and forty files the barred kin-term at 2 and `hemp` at 70, `thirty-six` at 20, `fifty-two` at 102, `sixty-one` at 142 and `sixty-six` at 249, **so a close writer counts before it enforces.** **The longest maximal shared run against the four hundred and thirty prior files is 26 words and is the dateline frame, and the longest among the ten new is 18 words and is the volume's own three things in the same order, and exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more are 0 and 0.** **There is no target in this thread for any of these figures.**

64. **And what the successor must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the thirty above.** **Do not name the antagonist, in any chapter, in the close record, in any prompt, in any state file, or in the outline; Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894, and he is on the page in Blocks 3 and 4 and is not confronted and is not defeated and is not forgiven.** Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a second System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a tenth line of the song, do not un-say the ninth word, do not soften the leak, and do not have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not print a word about a roof, and do not mention the tile on a coping and schedule nothing for it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not answer the question from a road and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not repair the breach between the two men in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater, and the living hamlet's near-sixty and the erased eighty may not be printed together or compared on the page. Do not add a tenth asking of that bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a ninth mark's explanation, and do not add a tenth thing to a clerk's schedule of nine things in a yard. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not schedule the seed vault, the bell's device, a second oath-stone renewal, a cut, or Chapter 900. Do not re-spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic, do not point at the mason's mark, do not ask the boy to read his book out loud, and do not show that book to anybody. Do not put the three collections with no head on them in one column, print them together, or add their counts to each other. Do not make the three invented things a lie, a forgery, a fiction or anybody's work, do not have anybody correct any of them, and do not have the receipt convict anybody. Do not say who sent the four men, do not print a seal's office, and do not bring anybody in nine counties to anything. Do not have the protagonist conduct a transfer, get sheets back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works. Do not improve on, refuse, rank or make lose any of the four governances or the fifth answer of a room let by the day, and do not present the council as the answer to them. Do not appoint anybody to the empty building and do not read or explain the slate. Do not open either of the two unreadable sheets in that county and do not have anybody ask the man of about nineteen a third question. Do not give the protagonist's sheet away, lose it, or have it read, and do not have a clerk of about nineteen ask him for it. Do not reconcile the refusal figures, add any two of them, or add any of them to a figure of persons, and do not add about nine hundred sheets a month to about a hundred and four in a band. Do not add the two series of the room, and do not add the clerk's schedule of nine to the day-book's nine or to the nine under the fourth step. Do not spend a name without saying so with the arithmetic, and do not print a name that has not been budgeted. Do not print a figure attached to a speech the page does not print, do not print *about* before a figure attached to a speech the page prints, and do not print a money column that is not footed in pence on the page. Do not add two prices of the same thing in any register, including a notebook. Do not report a dateline without deriving the weekday from the day number and the day of month from the day number, separately, and do not certify both halves with a single figure, **because a check that returns one number for two derivations is one derivation, and the half that was already repaired is the half that comes back clean.**

---

## Threads Volume 09 Batch 0005 carries to Volume 10

**This section is written by the phase that wrote the close of Volume 09 and it is Volume 10's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan Volume 09 was written against and is binding, and the Volume 08 close record and `state/continuity.md` items 1085 to 1100 are the facts. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Thirty numbered threads, from sixty-five, and the last is what Volume 10 must not do. This section resolves nothing that Batches 0001 to 0005 left open, names the antagonist nowhere, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

65. **The frame Volume 10 inherits, and the two calendar rows the plan still has wrong.** **Volume 09 ran day 931, the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth YR 318, to day 1140, the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth YR 318, a Sunday and not a market day, two hundred and nine days elapsed and two hundred and ten inclusive, and the whole of it is YR 318, and the year does not turn inside it. The anchor for any continuation is day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, and the month starts inside YR 318 are 936, 966, 996, 1026, 1056, 1086 and 1116, and the first of the next year is Thawmonth 1 YR 319 at day 1236.** **`outline/volume-09.md` §17 gives day 965 as a Wednesday when it is a Sunday, and gives 1098 as a Saturday and a market day and 1099 as a Sunday when 1098 is a Sunday and not a market morning and 1099 is a Monday. All three were re-derived for the close and none was copied, and the table was not edited, and the correction is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1047, 1066, 1082 and 1085 and at `state/open-threads.md` 19 and 38. A Volume 10 writer derives the first day of the block and does not copy any of the three rows.**

66. **The loose council of nine, and what it is and what it decided, and what it is not.** **Nine people met eight times in a room over a store in a township of about nine hundred people, two days off a made road, in the county on the salt, about nine miles from a bar four miles west of a market town of about two thousand people and about five miles from that market town. The room is eleven paces by nineteen, with a chair on a platform at the top end and a hat on a shelf behind the door, both about a hundred and forty years old, a bolt on the inside that has not been drawn since a woman of about sixty-three decided against it, and a book on the shelf in which what is agreed in that room has been written for about a hundred and forty years.** The nine agreed, in seven words, that nothing about it goes on any sheet, and that each of the nine keeps whatever they write or do not write and none of the nine sends it to the other eight. **On the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth they decided that there is no ninth meeting and no tenth, and that if two of them ever stand in a room together again it will be because two of them turned up for something else, and that nobody is to arrange it and nobody is to take a room in advance.** **There is no crown on it, no office, no register, no head on anything, and the word that names the founding paper was not printed once in the whole of Volume 09 in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and Volume 10 inherits that at zero and the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule is still unanswered in prose.** The nine are reached by age and by trade: a woman of about forty-four at a kiln, a man of about forty-one at a boundary, a mason of about sixty-eight at an arch, a woman of about fifty with a board, a woman of about fifty-eight with a slate, a man of about thirty-three with a barrow, a man of about nineteen at a bar, a man of about twenty-two with a handcart, and a woman of about seventeen who carries glass, with a man of about thirty-four with a net who keeps the room's own list and is a return from Chapter 0412. **No name was spent on any of them and the volume's name budget closed at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent, so no name from Volume 09's budget is outstanding.**

67. **The ninth word, where it is on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, and what it may and may not do next.** **The ninth word is `Coldharbour`, a road nine miles long, and it is a name and not a practice, and it is out.** It was sung in a market in another county in a month in Greenmonth, day 957, and about forty people heard it and about nine of them heard it properly, and a man of about thirty has it on the back of his own hand under a bar of soap and nothing over it, and the three invented things that carry the same nine letters are in four counties and are being said by children. **On the nineteenth of Embermonth it was handed to a man of about twenty-two with a handcart, who holds no practice and is on no list in the county the bar is in or the county the kilns are in or on any county's own series, and who said that he is not keeping it and has nowhere to write it; on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth he said it again in front of eight people without being asked to repeat it, and said that he will say it again on a road in ten years. The man of about nineteen at the bar was asked on the same Sunday and said no, in twenty-nine words. A woman of about thirty-seven with a plate on a bracket over her door is in neither room and has not been told.** Nothing was recalled, nobody regretted it in a speech, no tenth line was added, and the twenty-word engine sentence was said once, in Chapter 407, and was not reprinted. **Volume 10 may not un-say it, may not soften the leak, may not add a line, may not have anybody regret it in a speech, and may not take it back out of the mouth of a man who is not keeping it.** A road is not a place and has nobody in it and cannot be entered on a form at all, and that is the whole of why the ninth line of the bench-board is still ruled and empty.

68. **The leaving, and what it did and did not cost, and what may not be made of it.** **On the twenty-eighth of Embermonth, a Monday, a man of about nineteen at a bar and a woman of about seventeen who carries glass asked a man of about thirty-two with a limp to leave a room, and he said in six words that he would be gone on Wednesday and did not argue, and on the thirtieth he left at about the eleventh hour and the room was given up at dusk and the woman of about sixty-three who keeps that room's book was not in it and has not been told.** The chapter sets out the argument he did not make and the room never heard, and the two who asked were right and nobody defended him. **The leaving is not a defeat and is not written as one, and the page does not explain it and does not admire it, and it is not the breach between the two men being repaired and the two may not be joined on the page.** He is the only person in nine counties who knows where nine of the eleven places are, he decided at the eleventh hour of a Sunday in a month in Embermonth not to make a list, and the reason is printed and is not a reason anybody would call decent, because a list of eleven people in nine places is a column. **Volume 10 inherits a man of thirty-two with a limp who is on no list in nine counties, who left a room he was asked to leave, and who did not write it down as a defeat.**

69. **The woman of about seventeen, her book, and the gate at the end of nine miles of road.** **On the twenty-fifth of Embermonth she said three things in a room of nine and was asked nothing: a road nine miles long about nine miles west of the room, a gate at the end of it for about four hundred years, and a woman on the far side of that gate who is her mother, from which she came to this side when she was nine and has not been down the far half since.** The identification of that road as a protected and unopened continuation is by an arithmetic and not by a crossing, and the arithmetic was made, identified and closed in Batches 0003 and 0004 and is not re-spent. **A book of about four hundred entries in four years, made by her out of sheets she buys and folds, with about nine marks in it in a hand that is not hers, has still never been shown to anybody and was not read out in the close and was not asked for, and the not asking is the ninth thing that book has never been shown for.** **Nothing was opened. The gate is exactly where it was on the twenty-fifth of Embermonth, nobody was sent, and no chapter of the close scheduled anything for it, and no figure of the far side of that gate has ever been printed on anything at all, and about thirty-four people live in a field and a lane at this end of the road and the two figures were not compared on any page.** **Volume 10 may not open the gate, may not send anybody to it, may not ask the boy to read his book out loud, may not show the book, and may not re-spend the arithmetic as a reveal, and the word that names the founding paper is not how the far side of that gate is described in any chapter.**

70. **The keeper of records, the four lines, and the question from a road.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane in a fen county about two hundred miles off, fourpence a year, a body corporate with a door, a number and the capacity to sue and to be sued, out of a deed of thirteen words.** A drawer, the second down on the left, about eleven inches deep, holds about four hundred returns and nothing else, and the returns have been going in since the seventh of a month in Longlight and are emptied into a bag with the letters for a cart that goes out on the fixed day and is back a fortnight later. **On the eleventh of Wolfmonth she wrote four lines on a sheet describing what that room holds, did not put a name at the foot of it, did not put the nine words into the round, and put the four lines into the bag with the letters, and she is not going to tell him and he is not going to hear.** The four lines describe about nine hundred sheets a month into nine counties, about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed Saturday, a press standing open with no date anybody will give, about four hundred returns of which about nine in ten were sent in because somebody told them to, a day-book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, and a register with seven questions and no findings. **A sheet with no name at the foot of it cannot be got at and cannot be enforced and cannot be corrected, and she is the keeper of records.** **The question is still in the drawer. Nine words, no letter, no seal, a place in that county on the back, from a man of about thirty-four about two days off a coast: *Is the first of the eight still done anywhere.* It went in on the fourteenth of a month in Longlight, day 979, a Sunday, and it has been there twenty-three weeks exactly and no day over.** The answer is nine miles from her door: a mason of about sixty-eight at a single arch over a beck, who has asked the far side of it about four hundred times, the last time of all on the seventh of a month in Rainmonth, to a stranger who did not carry it anywhere. **Nobody carried it in twenty-three weeks and nobody is going to, and the question is unanswered and is not answered with a figure and the close did not answer it and did not have her answer it.**

71. **The four unpaid things, still unpaid, and what the close did with them.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wanted somebody's trade against a place. The man of about forty-four who farms `Coldharbour` wanted the figure of four hundred and seventy to stand. The man of about thirty-three with a barrow wanted the sheet in his coat read. A mason of about sixty-eight wanted one sentence carried four miles.** None was done and none was refused and nobody said one word to the protagonist about any of them, and on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth all four were in a market in a market town of about two thousand people on the salt and the chapter did not gather them and no character in it remarked on any of them. **A drain arch was finished in a village in the first week of a month in Wolfmonth and a mason of about sixty-eight four miles off did not come and does not know, and neither of them has the other's name, and there is no form in this kingdom on which a man is entered as somebody who is free in a particular week.** The four are still unpaid and the close did not pay them and did not remark on them, and Volume 10 may not have a character remark on one and may not give the protagonist a scene about how hard they were.

72. **The bar four miles west of Marl, and its figures, on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth.** **Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, down on the other three, the man of about nineteen paid nine pence a day for three of the four with the Sunday unpaid, the start at day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, a Wednesday. On day 1140 it is three hundred and sixty-one days, which is fifty-one weeks and four days, a hundred and fifty-five paid days, a hundred and fifty-five twists of paper at nine pence, one thousand three hundred and ninety-five pence, twenty-nine marks and three pence, and the canon chain behind it is 170 on 949, 193 on 972, 228 on 1007, 259 on 1038 and 301 on 1080.** The bar is the only one of the three this volume touched and the three bars remain three objects. **The sheet under a stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven is a hundred and ninety-one days old and unopened, and the fold in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his is a hundred and forty-one days old and unopened, and the receipt in the inside of the boy's own coat is eighty-three days old and unread and he cannot read it.** **His four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth, which is two hundred and thirty-one days before the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and thirty-three weeks exactly, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it and about four of the eight are wrong by a day, and on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth one of the eight walked four miles to say nine words about it, and the boy looked at the middle of the street for about four seconds and picked his basket up, and the man who said it does not know whether he is one of the four who are right or one of the four who are wrong and there is nothing in that town or that county that would ever settle it.** Nobody asked the boy a second question, nobody read either sheet, and the close did not ask him a third question and did not have him read anything.

73. **The protagonist's sheet, the notebook, and what is in his coat that nobody has come near.** **A sheet nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and the name of a man of about thirty-four with a sheet against each of the three, and nothing else, written on the eleventh day of Harvestmonth in the eighth hour of an evening on a bench on a made road, in the inside pocket of a coat.** It is the only record in the world of when three things were entered on a form and by whom. **Nobody in the close came near it. It was not shown, not lost, not read, not asked for, and no clerk of about nineteen asked him for it, and he did not add a date and did not add a name.** A notebook, eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them, the eleventh line never used, no twelfth line, opened once in the close and left empty. **The separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at in Volume 09 and the count of the looks at it is not printed and is not looked at.** Two unreconciled numbers stand in the coat unreconciled. **A permanent limp in the right leg, a ferrier's stick he cannot lean on, he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight and is not going to, coming out of a chair takes him about four seconds longer than going into one, and a road-marked left palm that is warm at about the fourth hour and that he has never told anybody about.** Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent, the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and a sweep of the close's ten files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and **no chapter of Volume 09 says that the ledger does or does not move and Volume 10 may not either.** The System panel in Chapter 426 is spent and there is no second.

74. **The three invented things, and the one receipt, and what is true and cannot be taken back.** **`Coldharbour`, entered on a form on the twenty-second of Rainmonth, day 1017, by a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, from a man of about forty-four who farms about forty acres and said in seventeen words that it was a harbour once and it is his field now and both of those are true. `four hundred and seventy`, entered on the twenty-fourth, day 1019, from a day and a half of counting at a stile by about nine people, none of them on a roll. `salt carting`, entered on the twenty-fifth, day 1020, from two carriers asked in a yard.** They went out on the twenty-seventh in a band of about a hundred and four sheets, they are in four counties, and by day 1140 they are being said by children. **None of them is a lie, a forgery or a fiction, nobody corrected any of them, and the close did not remove any of them and did not make any of them anybody's work.** **A receipt, in a fair hand, accurate, left under a door of a building with a door off its sill in a town of about nine hundred people, saying what was taken and where and on which days and by whom, with a line underneath giving a place, a number of men and a trade in that order, and a name at the foot of it. It cannot be given back. The close printed the argument about it in a room on the eighteenth of Wolfmonth and nobody in that room agreed with it and nobody disagreed with it either.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet is also one of the four men with a crowbar and the volume has never said whether those two facts are connected and the close did not say it and Volume 10 may not.** Nobody was found, nobody was brought to anything, and the reason is on the page: a boy of seventeen worked out from three numbers that the four men did not know what was on the shelf.

75. **The two protected things that are not on a page, and everything else that did not move.** **The city is four hundred miles off on a shelf and Volume 09 sent it nothing, and its three protected numerals are at zero across fifty chapters including the close. The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled and nobody was sent.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and not scheduled and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it, and the word *failed* is not used about anything in the close. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. **About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list, and the register stands with seven entries all questions and no findings, and the day-book stands at nine entries of a thing nobody asked with a tenth not a gift, and the two villages are not back on a road and are not told why.** The corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on and its rate is not printed and nobody puts it in a column, and the reason the rate works is that nobody can check it. The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands and neither is adjourned, retold or closed. The bench under the fourth step is at nine and no tenth asking was put to it. Hushwater was not taken and neither figure was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled, and the crossing budget for Block 5 was none and none was made.

76. **The three collections with no head on them, and the rule about them.** **Four feet of board with about three hundred and seventy figures and no head, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns, and about four of them in the hand of the man who wrote the receipt, and a woman of about fifty who cannot read and matched that hand in about nine minutes on the nineteenth of a month in Fallowmonth and has not said it and is not going to. About four hundred entries in four years in a book a boy of seventeen made himself. Nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket.** **All three are the same kind of thing and none of them was read, shown or read out in the close, and they were not put in one column, not printed together and not added to one another, and the close printed no third collection's figure beside either of the other two's.** The first two stand at ten marks in common and the tenth is in a file two hundred miles off and nobody explained it in the close. **The want of the woman of about fifty is not restated, nobody thanked her, and the board is not read out and not taken off the wall and not improved on.** The wants of the woman of about twenty-nine and the woman of about thirty-nine are not restated either, are not thanked, are not converted and are not put in a room again, and are not made about the nine.

77. **The three bars, the three nines, and the two counts, all still separate.** **The kerb bar, the market bar, and the fourth-months bar four miles west of Marl, are three objects and the close did not merge them and touched only the third.** The count on the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is nine, the day-book is at nine entries, the register is at seven questions and no findings, and **the clerk's schedule of nine things in a yard behind a house of assizes is a different nine and is a list of what a court is answerable for, and the close did not add any of the three to another or to a figure of persons and did not put a tenth thing on any of them.** **About nine hundred sheets a month go out of a room over a weigh-house into nine counties and about a hundred and four go out in one band on a fixed Saturday in a band of a hundred and eight with four taken out of it, and the two are counts of two different series, are not added, and are not converted into one another; the close printed both figures and added neither.** The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and the close printed no fifth and quoted no close.

78. **The two men, and the word that is spent, and the span that is not a repair.** **The breach between the two men is open. Nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure and nobody did in the close. The word for what they are is printed nowhere in the close and the barred kin-term is at two across four hundred and fifty chapters and is spent.** **In the close they were about nine feet apart with a barrow between them and neither of them said a word, and a barrow with a wheel in it went up a street afterwards and did not stop, and that is the whole of it.** A man of twenty-five owns the crews and the yard four hundred yards up a street of working yards in a fen town of about four thousand, and the yard's rule is that nothing which belongs to a man goes in that yard's book and only a thing set, and a man of thirty-two with a limp lodges over a cooper's shop on the same street and has done for about three years. **The span built over nineteen mornings is a hand's width out of level and will be next spring, and it is not a repair, and the close did not report it as one, and neither of the two men has walked it with the other and is going to.** Chapter 409 is three sentences about a barrow wheel and is not a reconciliation, and a Volume 10 writer who reads it as one has spent Volume 09's only reconciliation.

79. **The room, the cart, the round, and the five governances, none of them improved on.** **A public lot in a township of about nine hundred people, a room over a store, a chair on a platform, a hat on a shelf, a list of about nine things over four generations, about nine people out of about nine hundred putting a name in every year, a book about a hundred and forty years old, a woman of about sixty-three who keeps it and takes what the room is taken for out of a box on the shelf and has never once put it in the book, and a bolt that has not been drawn in about nine years.** The four governances are hereditary custody, a rotating clerk, a public lot, and no gate at all, and the fifth answer is a room that is let by the day whose keeper goes home, and **none of the five was improved on, refused, ranked or made to lose in the close, and a loose council of nine with no crown, no office and no register was not presented as their answer.** A place with no gate is not a refusal. A boundary of two miles and nine stones in a fold thirty miles south is walked by one man of forty-one and a boy of nine in new boots does not have to. The month-boundary oath-stone is gone and no second renewal was scheduled.

80. **The name budget, closed.** **The budget for Volume 09 was five. Batch 0001 spent three — `Brenna Tull`, 44, `Saber Vahl`, 68, and `Ivo Serrat`, 17 — and Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 spent none, so the volume closed at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent and nothing after them.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the close's ten files returns `Tovan Kest` and `Mara Vey` and both are returns and not spends. **No name from Volume 09's budget is outstanding, and the eleven witnesses and the nine in the room were all reached by age and by trade, which is the whole of the volume's argument about names and a handle.**

81. **The measurements, and the note that a state file which reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.** **Batch 0005 is 20,292 in ten chapters, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added and recounted on the files by a second pass that did not inherit the first pass's numbers. The volume is 98,070 in fifty chapters. The manuscript is 1,387,324 in four hundred and fifty chapters.** `about` 453 in 20,292, which is 22.57 per 1,000, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator. `the whole of` 28. `about four people` 0. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes 0, 0, 0, 0. `---` rules 0. **Ten money columns, ten footed in pence on the page, ten distinct totals at 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89 pence, and 0 collisions with the fifty-one barred and spent totals. Sixteen printed speech-length figures, sixteen footed, none preceded by *about*, and the block adds nothing to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class.** The twelve barred tokens and the four barred numerals at 0 across the ten files, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` at 0. **The longest maximal shared run against the four hundred and forty prior files is 15 words and is a declared frame, and the longest among the ten new is 15 words and is a declared frame in kind, and the figure is 15 on all three bases the second pass ran it on, and the 23 and 28 an earlier certificate gave do not reproduce on any of them, and exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more are 0 and 0.** **The calendar, derived twice, returned 0 and 0 and a market claim of 0, as three numbers and not as one, over eleven dated references, and Chapter 0450's differently-worded dateline was derived by hand and foots as a twelfth. There is no target in this file for any of these figures and there is none in the prompt that produced them, and a figure recorded here is a fact about the world and not a quota.**

82. **The nine stale durations the close's own hand found, because a Volume 10 writer will make the same error and the instrument will not catch it.** **The boy's day count, his paid days, his pence and his marks were all four written at the day-1140 values of 361, 155, 1,395 and twenty-nine marks and three pence in Chapter 0448, which is set on day 1130, and all four were wrong for the day the chapter is on while being right for the day the volume ends on. The sheet under the stone, the boy's own receipt and the fold in the coat were all three written at their day-1140 ages in chapters set on day 1130 and on day 1099. A market 147 days back was called nine weeks back and is about five months. A receipt he had carried in his head for thirty-one days was called nine weeks old. A field he had stood in twenty-one days before was called nine weeks off.** **The method that survives is the one Chapter 0438 used: an interval of N days from a start day is taken as whole weeks of four up-days in seven with the Sundays taken out, and the remainder is taken from the weekday of the start day, and the check is that it reproduces the four prior canon figures exactly.** **A calendar check asks whether a date is right and not whether a figure is right for the day the chapter is set on, and a duration is the figure most likely to be right for a different day, and a Volume 10 writer derives every duration from the day number the chapter is on and not from the day the volume ends on.**

83. **The ninety-one lines the close's own hand changed, and what they were.** **Six stale durations across nine figures, four wrong speech-length figures of which one was certified and then found wrong against its own second turn, three figures about unprinted speeches cut, four occurrences of the barred numerals `fifty-two` and `sixty-one` re-voiced away, one writer's own working note left in a chapter file in the middle of a paragraph carrying a barred token with it, six self-references, four re-voicings for duplication, a woman of about thirty-four where the canon has a woman of about thirty-seven in two chapters, a count of meetings wrong in two chapters, a sentence that said eight of nine people said nothing about a receipt when five had spoken, two grammar slips, and one interval that was a fortnight and not a month.** **Not one of the ninety-one was visible to a comparison between two files, and the cheapest was a figure correct for a different day, and the fourth cheapest was a note in the middle of a paragraph, and the sixth was a state-file-shaped sentence inside a chapter. Nine defects were caught by hand in Batch 0002, eleven in Batch 0003 and a twelfth by a later pass, eighty-eight lines in Batch 0004 and one more after its review, and ninety-one in Batch 0005. And a second pass over the finished close, which is `state/continuity.md` 1101, found nineteen more across seven chapters in ten classes, and the cheapest of that pass is the same class as the cheapest of the first: a duration correct for a different day, in a drawer, at a bar, and on a board.**

84. **The one a certificate caught, and it has now happened three times in two volumes, so Volume 10 inherits the habit and not the number.** **Chapter 0445's second speech-length figure was certified at a hundred and nineteen against a printed exchange whose second turn alone was eighty-six, and the fix was not a new number but a new sentence around the figure, because a figure attached to a two-turn exchange is a figure about every printed line of it and the sentence around it is part of what is being certified.** Batch 0004's case was a block that wrote *fourteen printed figures and fourteen footed* about a set it had not all counted, and the one it had not counted was two paragraphs from the one it had. **Volume 09's own close made the case a third time and in the sharpest form: `state/continuity.md` 1097 certified fourteen printed speech-length figures where the pages print sixteen, certified that one chapter prints thirty-three and eighty-six which the pages print in neither printing, and 1099 certified a longest shared run of 23 and 28 words that reproduce as 15 and 15.** **A check that returns one figure for two derivations is one derivation, and a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose, and the second measurement is the one that goes stale, and a certificate is a last place a defect survives because everything downstream reads the certificate and not the chapters.**

85. **What the close of Volume 09 is, and what it is not, so that a Volume 10 writer does not inherit the wrong shape.** **It is a loose council of nine with no crown, no office and no register, whose first act was to agree that nothing about it goes on any sheet, and which decided on its eighth day that there is no ninth meeting and no tenth, and whose one fixed thing is a name in the mouth of a man who is not keeping it. It is a woman of seventeen who identified a protected continuation by an arithmetic and did not open it. It is a keeper of records who wrote four lines about her own room with no name at the foot and did not answer a question that has been waiting twenty-three weeks. It is a bar with a figure on it that a boy worked out on the back of his hand. It is a man's last page, and he is not in the room, and nobody is on a road.** **It is not a summary of what the volume meant, and it is not a reconciliation and not a reunion, and it is not a new office and not a new order and not a new mechanism and not a new enemy, and the word for what it is not is the word that names the founding paper and is at zero.**

86. **The word that names the founding paper, at zero, and the question that stays unanswered.** **It is not printed anywhere in Volume 09, in the outline, in any prompt or in any state file, in a document, in a quotation, in a character's mouth or in narration, and the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule is not answered in prose.** **A Volume 09 writer who could not approach a rule without naming it left the naming alone and the close is better for it. `outline/series.md`'s Volume 11 row is where the naming is allowed and a Volume 10 writer does not take it either, and the word remains at zero across the volume boundary until the volume whose row permits it.**

87. **The antagonist, unnamed, and the cooperation that was not revived.** **He is not named in any of the fifty chapters of Volume 09, in `outline/volume-09.md`, in any prompt or in any state file this volume wrote, and Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** **He is on the page in Blocks 3 and 4 and is not confronted, not defeated and not forgiven, and nothing in Block 5 revives the Volume 08 cooperation and nothing in Block 5 is a reconciliation.** Volume 10 inherits him unnamed and inherits the fact that the close printed a receipt in a fair hand that is accurate and cannot be given back and has a place, a figure of men and a trade on it in that order, and that the office which wrote it is the office with a column.

88. **The thing a reader can follow from this volume into the next one, in one paragraph, and it is not the antagonist.** `outline/series.md`'s Volume 10 row makes the Morrow Coast's tide road the chassis and the Crown's claim on it the pressure, and it is a different coast and a different road and a different claim, and Volume 09 may not schedule the tide road and may not mention a coast in any chapter except the standing four-months trade already on the page. **What a reader can follow is a man of thirty-two with a limp who is on no list in nine counties, who left a room he was asked to leave and did not write it down as a defeat, and a set of nine practices of which two have no keeper and one is not in a place on the page, and a woman of about seventeen with a book of numbers that has never been shown to anybody and a gate at the end of a nine-mile road that has not been opened, and three things that are true and a receipt that is accurate.**

89. **The volume's own plan, and the three rows in it that are one day out, and the two numbers a successor must return separately.** `outline/volume-09.md` is the authority for Volume 09 and this close did not edit it. **Its §17 table gives day 965 as a Wednesday when it is a Sunday, and gives 1098 as a Saturday and a market day and 1099 as a Sunday when 1098 is a Sunday and not a market morning and 1099 is a Monday.** Those three are recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1047, 1066, 1082 and 1085 and at `state/open-threads.md` 19, 38 and 65, and Chapter 0440 and Chapter 0441 print the right ones. **The correction is owed by whoever owns the plan and not by a writer and not by a repair pass, and the standing lesson is that a check which returns one number for two derivations returns the half it has just repaired, and that the day-of-month and the weekday are two derivations and not one.**

90. **And what Volume 10 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the twenty-five above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof, and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic as a reveal. Do not answer the question from a road, and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine. Do not repair the breach between the two men, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a tenth thing to any of the three nines, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not make any of the three invented things a lie or a forgery or correct any of them, and do not have the receipt convict the man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and do not say whether his inventing a place, a figure and a trade is connected to his being one of the four men with a crowbar. Do not improve on, refuse, rank or make lose any of the four governances or the fifth answer, and do not present the nine as their answer. Do not improve on, refuse, rank or make lose any of the nine practices, and do not give any of the three that have no keeper a keeper. Do not read out, show or add together the three collections with no head on them. Do not ask the boy of seventeen to read his book, point at the mason's mark, or explain the tenth mark. Do not date the press, ring the bell, renew an oath-stone, make or schedule a cut, or schedule the seed vault or Chapter 900. Do not have anybody find the bench-board. Do not schedule the tide road, mention a coast, or revive the Volume 08 cooperation. Do not spend a name that is not in the volume's budget, and Volume 09's budget is closed at 3 of 5. Do not restate the want of the woman of about fifty or thank her, and do not restate or thank either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine. Do not remark on the four unpaid things. And do not carry a figure forward from the last day of a volume into a chapter that is not set on the last day, because that is the error the close of Volume 09 made nine times and found by reading and not by measuring.**

91. **The second pass over the close, and the one thing Volume 10 inherits from it that is not a fact about the world.** **A phase that inherited the ten finished chapters and these five state files re-read all ten and re-ran every check off the files, and found nineteen lines across seven chapters in ten classes, and recorded it at `state/continuity.md` 1101. Three durations were correct for a different day: a question in a drawer printed at twenty-three weeks in a chapter set on day 1126 where it is a hundred and forty-seven days and twenty-one weeks, a boy at a bar printed at about three hundred and forty days in a chapter set on day 1113 where it is three hundred and thirty-four, and the matching of a hand dated to a month in which the receipt it is matched to had not been written. Two speech-length figures, one about a two-turn speech where the page now prints both the turn and the whole, and one sentence that said a number was printed nowhere in the same breath as printing it. One figure about an unprinted speech that was also wrong against the world, since the first of the eight is four words and was printed as nine. Three gender slips. Two month names that cannot be resolved to a day in this volume, both changed into a duration. Two sentences that did not parse, one contradiction of place, one grammar slip, and one stale count of meetings. All of these are repaired and none of them is a fact Volume 10 may rely on except the ones already carried above.** **What Volume 10 inherits from the pass is the habit and not the number, and the habit has a name by now: a certificate is a last place a defect survives, because a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale. Three of this volume's nineteen defects were in the state files and none was on a page, and one of them was a count of printed speech figures that was wrong by two, one was two figures the page never printed, and one was a duplication measurement that was wrong twice. A Volume 10 phase that inherits a certificate from this project should re-run the check and not read the number, and a Volume 10 phase that writes one should write what it counted and not what it intended, and a month name it cannot resolve to a day should become a duration.**

92. **The third pass over the close, what it found, and the one thing it adds to item 91, which is that a repair log is a certificate and is not a safer one.** **A phase that inherited the ten finished chapters and the five state files repaired the review findings on the second pass and changed three lines across three chapters and moved no dateline, no card, no plot beat, no ending, no money total and no name. One, Chapter 0450 printed the boy's start as *fifty-one weeks and four days and one day over a year*, and 361 days is four days short of a year, and the clause the second pass replaced was correct and is restored; every other figure in that sentence is right at 361 and stands, and the method at continuity 1093 was re-run for it and reproduces all six of its canon figures at days 949, 972, 1038, 1080, 1130 and 1140 exactly. Two, Chapter 0441's sentence about the room's prior occasion had been left by the second pass with no main clause, a present perfect in a past-perfect frame, and a reading of this as the second meeting of the nine on the first of the eight, and it now says the room had said a thing out loud on purpose once before this, which is the count continuity 1101 recorded as its own intent and had certified as done, so the certificate of a repair is the last place an unrepaired sentence still reads as whole. Three, Chapter 0444's *the ninth word and this one are in the same mouth in the same week* had no antecedent for either term and a week that is six days, and it now says a name that is not a practice went into that mouth on the nineteenth of this month and that speech came out of it on the twenty-fifth, which is six days, and the name is not printed, being the ninth word and out. The block is 20,303 words on the declared basis, the volume is 98,081 in fifty chapters and the manuscript is 1,387,335 in four hundred and fifty chapters, all three recounted on the chapter files.** **Nothing in this item is a fact about the world. What a Volume 10 phase inherits from it is one habit and it is the same habit item 91 describes and it now has a second half: re-run a check you inherit, and re-count the figure you are about to change, and never take the direction of a repair log's own sentence as evidence that the repair is whole, because the cheapest defect in three passes over one volume was a figure that was correct for a different day, the second time over, and it was introduced by a pass that was reading a certificate and not the page.**

## Threads the Volume 09 close hands to Volume 10

**This section is written by the phase that wrote the close record at `outline/volumes/volume-09-close.md`, and it is Volume 10's whole instruction. `outline/volume-09.md` is the plan Volume 09 was written against and is binding, and the twenty-eight threads under *Threads Volume 09 Batch 0005 carries to Volume 10*, items 65 to 92, are the facts, and `volume-09-close.md` §7 confirms, corrects and extends all twenty-eight of them. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after the close's own repairs and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. **Twenty-two numbered threads, from ninety-three, and the last is what Volume 10 must not do. The count is twenty-two and the count is the number of items from 93 to 114 inclusive, and an earlier printing of this line gave the Batch 0005 section's count instead of this section's, which is a close copying a thread count out of the section it is superseding and is the Volume 07 close's error in a new place.** This section resolves nothing that Batches 0001 to 0005 left open, names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term and no fifth unattributed statement, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

93. **The frame Volume 10 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Volume 09 ran day 931, the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth YR 318, to day 1140, the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth YR 318, a Sunday and not a market day, two hundred and nine days elapsed and two hundred and ten inclusive, and the whole of it is YR 318. **The anchor for any continuation is day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month; the month starts inside YR 318 are 936, 966, 996, 1026, 1056, 1086 and 1116; Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236; and the year turns 360 days after the volume's last day.** **`outline/volume-09.md` §17 gives day 965 as a Wednesday when it is a Sunday and gives 1098 as a Saturday and 1099 as a Sunday when 1098 is a Sunday and 1099 is a Monday; Chapters 0440 and 0441 print the right ones; the plan was not edited and the correction is owed by whoever owns it. Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and report them as two figures and never as one.** `state/continuity.md` 1104.

94. **The loose council of nine, and what it is and what it decided, and that it is not reachable.** Nine people met eight times in a room over a store in a township of about nine hundred people, and on the eighth day they agreed there would be no ninth meeting and no tenth, and that if two of them ever stand in a room together again it will be because two of them turned up for something else, and that nobody is to arrange it and nobody is to take a room in advance. **The first act was seven words long and was not written down: nothing about it goes on any sheet. There is no crown, no office, no register and no head on anything, and a name in a hat is not put to any of the nine's use, and the word that names the founding paper was not printed once in the whole of Volume 09 in any construction.** **The nine have no address and that is deliberate: a man of about forty-one refused to say where the nine stones of a boundary are in a room, and that refusal is the reason.** A successor that has them meet a ninth time has undone the volume's argument, and a successor that has them form an office, a register or a crown has undone it worse. `state/continuity.md` 1103, `volume-09-close.md` §4.

95. **The ninth word, where it is, and what it may and may not do next.** The ninth word is `Coldharbour`, a road nine miles long, and it is a name and not a practice, and it is out. It was sung in a market in another county in a month in Greenmonth, day 957, and about forty people heard it and about nine of them heard it properly, and a man of about thirty has it on the back of his own hand under a bar of soap and nothing over it. **On the nineteenth of Embermonth it was handed to a man of about twenty-two with a handcart, who holds no practice and is on no list in the county the bar is in or the county the kilns are in or on any county's own series, and who said he is not keeping it and has nowhere to write it; on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth he said it again in front of eight people without being asked to repeat it, and said he will say it again on a road in ten years. The man of about nineteen at the bar was asked on the same Sunday and said no, in twenty-nine words. A woman of about thirty-seven with a plate on a bracket over her door is in neither room and has not been told.** Nothing was recalled, nobody regretted it in a speech, no tenth line was added, and the twenty-word engine sentence was said once, in Chapter 407, and was not reprinted. **Volume 10 may not un-say it, may not soften the leak, may not add a line, and may not take it back out of the mouth of a man who is not keeping it. A road is not a place and has nobody in it and cannot be entered on a form at all, and that is the whole of why the ninth line of the bench-board is still ruled and empty.**

96. **The leaving, and what it did and did not cost, and what may not be made of it.** On the twenty-eighth of Embermonth, a Monday, a man of about nineteen at a bar and a woman of about seventeen who carries glass asked a man of about thirty-two with a limp to leave a room, and he said in six words that he would be gone on Wednesday and did not argue, and on the thirtieth he left at about the eleventh hour and the room was given up at dusk and the woman of about sixty-three who keeps that room's book was not in it and has not been told. **The chapter sets out the argument he did not make and the room never heard it: that he told four of the nine himself, that he connected eleven people in eleven places in nine counties in about four months, that not one of the eleven has ever been in a room with another of the eleven, that he is the only person in nine counties who can see the receipt and the board at once, and that a man in every room in nine counties at once is a man in a column.** The two who asked were right and nobody defended him. **The leaving is not a defeat and is not written as one, the page does not explain it and does not admire it, and it is not the breach between the two men being repaired and the two may not be joined on the page.** He is on no list in nine counties and he is not in the volume's last page.

97. **The woman of about seventeen, her book, and the gate at the end of nine miles of road.** On the twenty-fifth of Embermonth she said three things in a room of nine and was asked nothing: a road nine miles long about nine miles west of the room, a gate at the end of it for about four hundred years, and a woman on the far side of that gate who is her mother, from which she came to this side when she was nine and has not been down the far half since. **The identification of that road as a protected and unopened continuation is by an arithmetic and not by a crossing, and the arithmetic was made, identified and closed in Batches 0003 and 0004 and is not re-spent anywhere in the fifty chapters.** A book of about four hundred entries in four years, made by her out of sheets she buys and folds, with about nine marks in it in a hand that is not hers, **has still never been shown to anybody and was not read out and was not asked for, and the not asking is the ninth thing that book has never been shown for.** Nothing was opened, nobody was sent, and no figure of the far side of that gate has ever been printed on anything at all, and about thirty-four people live in a field and a lane at this end of the road and the two figures were not compared on any page. **Volume 10 may not open the gate, may not send anybody to it, may not ask the boy to read his book out loud, may not show the book, and may not re-spend the arithmetic as a reveal.**

98. **The keeper of records, the four lines, and the question from a road, and the two figures a successor must return separately.** Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane in a fen county about two hundred miles off, fourpence a year, a body corporate with a door, a number and the capacity to sue and to be sued, out of a deed of thirteen words. A drawer, the second down on the left, about eleven inches deep, holds about four hundred returns and nothing else. **On the eleventh of Wolfmonth she wrote four lines on a sheet describing what that room holds, did not put a name at the foot of it, did not put the nine words into the round, and put the four lines into the bag with the letters, and she is not going to tell him and he is not going to hear.** A sheet with no name at the foot of it cannot be got at and cannot be enforced and cannot be corrected, and she is the keeper of records. **The question is still in the drawer. Nine words, no letter, no seal, a place in that county on the back, from a man of about thirty-four about two days off a coast: *Is the first of the eight still done anywhere.* It went in on the fourteenth of a month in Longlight, day 979, a Sunday.
