At the day the chapter carrying it is set on, day 1126, it is a hundred and forty-seven days, which is twenty-one weeks exactly and no day over; at the volume's last day it is a hundred and sixty-one days, which is twenty-three weeks exactly.** Both figures are right on their own day and **a Volume 10 writer carries the one for the day it is writing, and this is the class of error that has been the cheapest defect in every pass over this volume.** The answer is nine miles from her door: a mason of about sixty-eight at a single arch over a beck, who has asked the far side of it about four hundred times, the last time on the seventh of a month in Rainmonth, to a stranger who did not carry the answer anywhere. **Nobody carried it in twenty-three weeks and nobody is going to.**

99. **The four unpaid things, still unpaid, and what the close did with them.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wanted somebody's trade against a place. The man of about forty-four who farms `Coldharbour` wanted the figure of four hundred and seventy to stand. The man of about thirty-three with a barrow wanted the sheet in his coat read. A mason of about sixty-eight wanted one sentence carried four miles. **None was done and none was refused and nobody said one word to the protagonist about any of them, and on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth all four were in a market in a market town of about two thousand people on the salt and the chapter did not gather them and no character in it and no sentence of narration remarked on any of them.** A drain arch was finished in a village in the first week of a month in Wolfmonth and a mason of about sixty-eight four miles off did not come and does not know, and neither of them has the other's name, and there is no form in this kingdom on which a man is entered as somebody who is free in a particular week. **Volume 10 may not have a character remark on one and may not give the protagonist a scene about how hard they were.**

100. **The bar four miles west of Marl, its figures, and the method a successor must use.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, down on the other three, the man of about nineteen paid nine pence a day for three of the four with the Sunday unpaid, the start at day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, a Wednesday. **At day 1140 it is 361 days, 51 weeks and four days, four days short of a full year, 204 up-days less 51 Sundays is 153, 155 paid days, 1,395 pence, twenty-nine marks and three pence. The canon chain behind it is 73 paid on 949, 83 on 972, 111 and 999 pence on 1038, 129 and 1,161 pence on 1080 and 151 and 1,359 pence on 1130, and the day counts at all seven printings are 170, 193, 228, 259, 301, 351 and 361.** The sheet under a stone is a hundred and ninety-one days old and unopened, the fold in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his is a hundred and forty-one days old and unopened, **and the same fold was thirty-nine days old at day 1038 and the close repaired two sites that said thirty-eight.** The receipt in the inside of the boy's own coat is eighty-three days old and unread and he cannot read it. **His four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth, which is 231 days before the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and 33 weeks exactly, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it and about four of the eight are wrong by a day, and on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth one of the eight walked four miles to say nine words about it, and the boy looked at the middle of the street for about four seconds and picked his basket up, and the man who said it does not know whether he is one of the four who are right or one of the four who are wrong and there is nothing in that town or that county that would ever settle it.** The bar is the only one of the three bars this volume touched and the three bars remain three objects.

101. **The protagonist's sheet, the notebook, and what is in his coat that nobody has come near.** A sheet nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and the name of a man of about thirty-four with a sheet against each of the three, and nothing else, written on the eleventh day of Harvestmonth in the eighth hour of an evening on a bench on a made road, in the inside pocket of a coat. **It is the only record in the world of when three things were entered on a form and by whom. Nobody in Volume 09 came near it. It was not shown, not lost, not read, not asked for, and no clerk of about nineteen asked him for it, and he did not add a date and did not add a name.** A notebook, eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them, the eleventh line never used, no twelfth line, opened once in Chapter 0446 and left empty. **The separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at in Volume 09 and the count of the looks at it is not printed and is not looked at.** Two unreconciled numbers stand in the coat unreconciled. **A permanent limp in the right leg, a ferrier's stick he cannot lean on, he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight and is not going to, coming out of a chair takes him about four seconds longer than going into one, and a road-marked left palm that is warm at about the fourth hour and that he has never told anybody about.** Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent, the qualification not earned and not claimed, and a sweep of all fifty chapters returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and **no chapter of Volume 09 says the ledger does or does not move and Volume 10 may not either.** The System panel in Chapter 426 is spent and there is no second.

102. **The three invented things, and the one receipt, and what is true and cannot be taken back.** `Coldharbour`, entered on the twenty-second of Rainmonth, day 1017, by a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, from a man of about forty-four who farms about forty acres and said in seventeen words that it was a harbour once and it is his field now and both of those are true. `four hundred and seventy`, entered on the twenty-fourth, day 1019, from a day and a half of counting at a stile by about nine people, none of them on a roll. `salt carting`, entered on the twenty-fifth, day 1020, from two carriers asked in a yard. **They went out on the twenty-seventh in a band of about a hundred and four sheets, they are in four counties, and by day 1140 they are being said by children. None of them is a lie, a forgery or a fiction, nobody corrected any of them, and the close did not remove any of them and did not make any of them anybody's work.** A receipt, in a fair hand, accurate, left under a door of a building with a door off its sill in a town of about nine hundred people, saying what was taken and where and on which days and by whom, with a line underneath giving a place, a number of men and a trade in that order, and a name at the foot of it. **It cannot be given back. The argument about it was printed in a room on the eighteenth of Wolfmonth and nobody in that room agreed with it and nobody disagreed with it either.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet is also one of the four men with a crowbar and the volume has never said whether those two facts are connected and the close did not say it and Volume 10 may not.** Nobody was found, nobody was brought to anything, and the reason is on the page: a boy of seventeen worked out from three numbers that the four men did not know what was on the shelf.

103. **The two protected things that are not on a page, and everything else that did not move.** The city is four hundred miles off on a shelf and Volume 09 sent it nothing, and its three protected numerals are at zero across fifty chapters including the close. The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled and nobody was sent. **The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled to reopen, and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it, and the word *failed* is not used about anything in Volume 09.** The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. **About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list, and the register stands with seven entries all questions and no findings, and the day-book stands at nine entries of a thing nobody asked with a tenth not a gift, and the two villages are not back on a road and are not told why.** The corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on and its rate is not printed and nobody puts it in a column, **and the reason the rate works is that nobody can check it.** The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands and neither is adjourned, retold or closed. The bench under the fourth step is at nine and no tenth asking was put to it. **Hushwater was not taken and neither figure was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed a second time, no cut was made and none is scheduled, and the crossing budget for Block 5 was none and none was made.**

104. **The three collections with no head on them, and the rule about them.** Four feet of board with about three hundred and seventy figures and no head, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns, and about four of them in the hand of the man who wrote the receipt, **and a woman of about fifty who cannot read matched that hand in about nine minutes on the nineteenth of a month in Fallowmonth and has not said it and is not going to.** About four hundred entries in four years in a book a boy of seventeen made himself. Nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket. **All three are the same kind of thing and none was read, shown or read out in Volume 09, and they were not put in one column, not printed together and not added to one another, and the close printed no third collection's figure beside either of the other two's.** The first two stand at ten marks in common and the tenth is in a file two hundred miles off and nobody explained it. **The want of the woman of about fifty is not restated, nobody thanked her, and the board is not read out and not taken off the wall and not improved on.** The wants of the woman of about twenty-nine and the woman of about thirty-nine are not restated either, are not thanked, are not converted and are not put in a room again, and are not made about the nine.

105. **The three bars, the three nines, and the two counts, all still separate.** The kerb bar, the market bar, and the fourth-months bar four miles west of Marl, are three objects and the close did not merge them and touched only the third. The count on the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is nine, the day-book is at nine entries, the register is at seven questions and no findings, and **the clerk's schedule of nine things in a yard behind a house of assizes is a different nine and is a list of what a court is answerable for, and the close did not add any of the three to another or to a figure of persons and did not put a tenth thing on any of them.** **About nine hundred sheets a month go out of a room over a weigh-house into nine counties and about a hundred and four go out in one band on a fixed Saturday, and the two are counts of two different series, are not added, and are not converted into one another; the close printed both figures and added neither.** The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and the close printed no fifth and quoted no close. **Extended by the close: the volume's money record is forty-four footed columns and not fifty, with two collisions — 70 pence twice inside Block 0002 and 30 pence at `chapter-0410.md` and `chapter-0441.md` — and a Volume 10 writer who expects ten columns a block will be short in its first block.** `state/continuity.md` 1107.

106. **The two men, and the word that is spent, and the span that is not a repair.** The breach between the two men is open. **Nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure and nobody did in the close, and the closest the two of them come to being in a room together is about nine feet with a barrow between them, and that is a distance and not a thing anybody has a word for that they would both use.** The word for what they are is printed nowhere in Volume 09 and **the barred kin-term is at two across four hundred and fifty chapters and is spent, and this thread prints no third.** A man of twenty-five owns the crews and the yard four hundred yards up a street of working yards in a fen town of about four thousand, and the yard's rule is that nothing which belongs to a man goes in that yard's book and only a thing set, and a man of thirty-two with a limp lodges over a cooper's shop on the same street and has done for about three years. **The span built over nineteen mornings is a hand's width out of level and will be next spring, and it is not a repair, and the close did not report it as one, and neither of the two men has walked it with the other and is going to. Chapter 0410 is a woman doing a piece of work he was asked to do and it is not a reconciliation, and a Volume 10 writer who reads it as one has spent Volume 09's only reconciliation.**

107. **The room, the cart, the round, and the five governances, none of them improved on.** A public lot in a township of about nine hundred people, a room over a store, a chair on a platform, a hat on a shelf, a list of about nine things over four generations, about nine people out of about nine hundred putting a name in every year, a book about a hundred and forty years old, a woman of about sixty-three who keeps it and takes what the room is taken for out of a box on the shelf and has never once put it in the book, and a bolt that has not been drawn in about nine years. **The four governances are hereditary custody, a rotating clerk, a public lot and no gate at all, and the fifth answer is a room that is let by the day whose keeper goes home, and none of the five was improved on, refused, ranked or made to lose in Volume 09, and a loose council of nine with no crown, no office and no register was not presented as their answer. A place with no gate is not a refusal.** A boundary of two miles and nine stones in a fold thirty miles south is walked by one man of forty-one and a boy of nine in new boots does not have to. The month-boundary oath-stone at day 966 was renewed and no second renewal is scheduled.

108. **The name budget, closed.** The budget for Volume 09 was five. Batch 0001 spent three — `Brenna Tull`, 44, `Saber Vahl`, 68, and `Ivo Serrat`, 17 — and Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 spent none, **so the volume closed at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent and nothing after them: the arithmetic is 3 + 0 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 3.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the fifty chapters returns `Brenna Tull` 1, `Saber Vahl` 1, `Ivo Serrat` 7, `Mara Vey` 4, `Aren Kest` 4, `Tovan Kest` 3, and two non-names that are sentence-initial, and every hit was read before the sweep was reported. **The eleven witnesses and the nine in the room were all reached by age and by trade, which is the whole of the volume's argument about names and a handle, and a name is the only asset in this manuscript that cannot be audited after the fact.** Volume 10 inherits a fresh budget and not this one.

109. **The measurements, and the note that a state file which reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.** **Batch 0005 is 20,303 in ten chapters, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added; the volume is 98,079 in fifty chapters after the close's own repairs and was 98,081 before them; the manuscript is 1,387,333 in four hundred and fifty chapters and was 1,387,335 before them; and the two words are two whitespace tokens the close's own repairs removed, both of them in Block 0001.** `about` **453 in 20,303, which is 22.85 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator, **and the live handoff printed 453 and 22.31 and both reproduce, and the close's first printing of this thread said 452 and 22.26 and was wrong.** The volume's `about` is **2,619 in 98,079, which is 26.70**, and it was 2,621 before the close's repairs, and the two removed tokens are the whole of the movement, and the close's first printing said 2,602 and 26.53 and said the numerator had not moved, and all three claims were wrong. Per block the `about` count is **676 / 614 / 490 / 386 / 453** at **29.47 / 31.05 / 28.87 / 21.33 / 22.31**, and the close's first printing carried 671 / 611 / 489 / 379 / 452 at 29.25 / 30.90 / 28.82 / 20.94 / 22.26, every one of the ten figures out. `the whole of` 28 in the block and 152 in the volume, per block **39 / 29 / 32 / 24 / 28**, and this one reproduces. `about four people` 0 in the block and **11** in the volume, per block **3 / 4 / 3 / 1 / 0**, and the close's first printing carried 2 / 4 / 3 / 1 / 0 and footed that row to 10 while Block 0001 is 3 and not 2. The seven filler words this project names — `held`, `steady`, `plain`, `counted`, `breathed`, `wrote`, `nodded` — stand at **149** in the volume at 23 / 0 / 2 / 47 / 0 / 77 / 0, per block 30 / 21 / 34 / 45 / 19, and the close's first printing carried 146 and a per-block row of 25 / 25 / 36 / 24 / 22 that sums to 132 and does not foot its own stated total. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes **0, 0, 0, 0** in Block 0005 and **0, 0, 0, 2** for the volume. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. **Curly-quotation balance 359 and 359, balancing in every one of the fifty files, and it was not balanced in two files before the close's repairs; the straight-quote count is 0 and was 272 in 26 files, all of them in Blocks 3 to 5, and the break fell exactly at the boundary between Batch 0002 and Batch 0003.** Ten money columns in Block 0005, ten footed, ten distinct totals at 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and **they reproduce the block's published ledger to the digit.** The twelve barred tokens at zero across the fifty files, and the four barred numerals at 1, 0, 0 and 0, **and the 1 is inside a correct money column at `chapter-0401.md`.** Manuscript-wide, `hemp` 70 in 40 files, `thirty-six` **20 in 13**, `fifty-two` **101 in 32 on the declared token basis and 102 written occurrences, the one being `fifty-two's` at `chapter-0230.md` line 55**, `sixty-one` 142 in 51, `sixty-six` 249 in 50. **The live handoff printed 20 and 102 for the second and the third and both were right, and the close's first printing corrected them to 17 and 100 and was wrong, so a Volume 10 writer counts before it enforces, and counts before it corrects, and the counts are here.** The barred kin-term is at 2 across 450 files and is spent. **The longest maximal shared run against the four hundred and fifty prior files is 76 words and is a bookend's location frame that stops mid-figure because the two chapters' day counts differ; the longest among the volume's own chapters across blocks is 28 words and is the volume's refrain; exact duplicate paragraphs touching Volume 09 are 0 and 0.** The calendar returned 0 and 0 and a market claim of 0 as three numbers over fifty datelines and one second date. **There is no target in this thread for any of these figures and none in the prompt that produced them, and a figure recorded here is a figure a successor may check and not a quota.**

110. **The habit the close inherits, which is not a number, and it has now happened five times in two volumes and the fifth time the close did it to itself.** **A certificate is a last place a defect survives, because a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.** Batch 0004 wrote *fourteen printed figures and fourteen footed* about a set it had not all counted. Volume 09's own close certified fourteen printed speech-length figures where the pages print sixteen, certified one chapter as printing two figures it printed in neither printing, and certified a longest shared run that does not reproduce. **And then the same close, holding the rule in one hand, broke it in the other: it took four figures from the live handoff that were correct — `thirty-six` at 20, `fifty-two` at 102, `about` at 453 and its rate at 22.31 — declared all four wrong, replaced them with 17, 100, 452 and 22.26, and in the same pass undercounted its own volume ledger by seventeen on `about`, by one on `about four people` and by three on the filler words, and the `about four people` row and the filler row each failed to foot against the total printed beside them.** A review of that phase counted every one of them off the chapter files and corrected the record, and the prose repairs and the calendar findings in the same phase stand, and this thread exists because the arithmetic did not.

**A phase that repairs from a certificate re-counts the figure the repair is about instead of taking the direction of the certificate's own sentence. A phase that writes a certificate re-counts every figure in it off the page rather than re-deriving any of them from the fact of its own repairs, because re-deriving is how Block 0001's two removed tokens ended up subtracted from Block 0005, which none of the repairs touched.** **And the third pass's own lesson is the sharper one: a pass that has corrected a right clause because it looks like the wrong one is worse than a pass that misses one, because the first is not recoverable by a reader who trusts the file.** `state/continuity.md` 1105, 1109, 1110, 1111.

111. **The instrument this close had to invent, and the three classes no instrument was pointed at.** The close's six bases are a per-file curly-quotation balance, clause-level Jaccard, eight- and twelve-word shingles, raw-token bidirectional extension, exact duplicate paragraphs, and hygiene. **Six of the seven repairs were invisible to every basis except the quotation-mark balance, and the raw-token basis, the only one that has ever found a real duplicated clause in this project, returned clean on all of them; the bold-parity basis, which found Volume 08's twin defect, returned clean on the twin's own first cousin.** The three classes are: **a mark that is uniform inside every scope a pass is given, and therefore invisible until it is read across a block boundary; a speech-length figure attached to a printed speech, where the basis is a list maintained per batch by the phase that wrote the speeches; and a duration printed against the day the volume ends rather than the day the chapter is set on, which a calendar check cannot see because it asks whether a date is right and not whether a figure is right for the day.** **A Volume 10 prompt should check the quotation mark, and should check it across block boundaries and not inside them, and should have somebody count the speeches and not a script.**

112. **What the close of Volume 09 is, and what it is not, and the promise the plan lost.** **It is a loose council of nine with no crown, no office and no register, whose first act was to agree in seven words that nothing about it goes on any sheet, and which decided on its eighth day that there is no ninth meeting and no tenth, and whose one fixed thing is a name in the mouth of a man who is not keeping it. It is a woman of seventeen who identified a protected continuation by an arithmetic and did not open it. It is a keeper of records who wrote four lines about her own room with no name at the foot and did not answer a question that has waited twenty-three weeks. It is a bar with a figure on it that a boy worked out on the back of his own hand. It is a man's last page, and he is not in the room, and nobody is on a road.** **It is not a summary of what the volume meant, and it is not a reconciliation and not a reunion, and it is not a new office and not a new order and not a new mechanism and not a new enemy, and the word for what it is not is the word that names the founding paper and is at zero.** **And one promise of the plan was not kept and could not have been: §14 required the protagonist to learn that the founding paper was fragmented on purpose and that a consent rule is a later edit, and §14's own second rule forbids the word that names that paper in a document, a quotation, a mouth and narration. The volume delivered a different and real knowing in Block 4 — that a receipt and a form cut about forty years ago are the same kind of thing, at `chapter-0437.md`, and he is not going to tell anybody — and lost the one the plan specified. That is a defect in the plan and not in the prose, and a Volume 10 writer reading §14 will otherwise spend a batch looking for a chapter that cannot be written.** `outline/volumes/volume-09-close.md` §5.

113. **The question the volume leaves open, which is the one to open on.** **It is not whether the receipt is wrong — the receipt is accurate — and it is not whether the three invented things are lies, because none of them is. It is what happens to nine counties when the honest instrument and the dishonest one are the same piece of paper.** A column needs a place and a figure and a trade. A road has none of the three. So the office invented all three in a fortnight, in the ordinary way, by asking people ordinary questions, and every one of the three is true, and about nine hundred people in four counties now hold a true sentence and will hold it after every person who entered it is dead. **The ninth people printed the argument about the receipt in a room on the eighteenth of Wolfmonth and not one of them agreed and not one of them disagreed, and nobody filled in the ninth line of the bench-board, and the ninth line is still ruled and empty, and a Volume 10 writer who fills it in has spent the volume's second volume-level fact.** §10 of the close record.

114. **And what Volume 10 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the twenty above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a second System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic as a reveal. Do not answer the question from a road, and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a tenth thing to any of the three nines, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure. Do not make any of the three invented things a lie or a forgery or correct any of them, do not have the receipt convict the man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and do not say whether his inventing a place, a figure and a trade is connected to his being one of the four men with a crowbar. Do not read the leaving as a defeat, do not read Chapter 0410 as a reconciliation, and do not read the council of nine as the answer to the four governances. Do not have a character remark on any of the four unpaid things. Do not merge the three collections or print their counts together. Do not schedule anything for the first of a month, the tide road, the press, the bell, the seed vault, or Chapter 900. And do not edit `outline/volume-09.md`; the three wrong rows in its §17 are owed by whoever owns the plan.**

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0001 carries to Volume 10 Batch 0002

**This section is written by the phase that planned Volume 10 and wrote its first block, and it is Batch 0002's whole instruction. `outline/volume-10.md` is the plan Volume 10 is written against and is binding, and `outline/volumes/volume-09-close.md` and `state/continuity.md` items 1113 to 1128 are the facts, and item 1128 is the second review of Batch 0001 and is the one to read for what the block is not. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. **Sixteen numbered threads, from 115, and the last is what Volume 10 Batch 0002 must not do.** This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

115. **The frame Batch 0002 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor. **Volume 10 runs day 1141 to day 1380, two hundred and forty days inclusive and two hundred and thirty-nine elapsed, eight months on the carried thirty-day rule; the five blocks are 1141 to 1188, 1189 to 1236, 1237 to 1284, 1285 to 1332 and 1333 to 1380, forty-eight days inclusive each.** Block 0002 runs **day 1189, the fourteenth of Hearthmonth YR 318, a Sunday and not a market day, to day 1236, the first of Thawmonth YR 319, a Friday and not a market day**, and **the year turns on the last day of that block and on no other day in the volume.** Month starts inside the volume are 1146, 1176, 1206, 1236, 1266, 1296, 1326 and 1356. **Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236. A writer who carries YR 318 through the block will have every date from the turn on be a year out and every one of them will foot against the others.** Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and report them as two figures and never as one. `state/continuity.md` 1113, 1115.

116. **The two dateline frames, and which of them a chapter is in, and the fact that the frame broke and came back inside Batch 0001.** A main-world dateline carries a month, a day of month and a weekday. **A coast dateline carries an ebb and an hour and carries none of the three, because nobody on that foreshore has said a day of the week in about nine years and nobody has said a month either; the year there is counted in springs and in the number of times the salt has been off.** Chapters 456 and 457 are in the coast frame; the other eight are in the main-world frame; the frame broke in the middle of the block and came back at Chapter 458. **The two frames are not convertible and no month name and no weekday is spoken in a room on that foreshore at all, and a yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no Block 0002 chapter may add any two of the three.** `state/continuity.md` 1114.

117. **The request, and what is still open about it, and what is not.** A folded letter with a brown seal, addressed to a keeper of records in a county about nine counties off, eleven days on a road. **What it asks for is a keeper for a connection between three roads that have never been joined, and the second side is a list of eleven items, every one of them a road somebody wants joined to a road somebody else is on.** The keeper of records did not answer it and did not fill a form in and sent for a man of thirty-two with a limp on the seventh hour of a Monday. **The letter is still on a bench in that room and the seal has not been broken open again and will not be, and the three port assemblies have not been told that a room refused, because nobody told them there was a room.** Batch 0002 may answer it, refuse it, let it go stale, or have it arrive twice, and the refusal of the coast in Chapter 457 is the refusal of a permanent connection and is not a refusal of the letter.

118. **The refusal, and the five grounds, and the one that is owed.** Nine people came out to a pan over about an hour in ones and twos, there is no chair, nobody kept a count, and they refused a permanent connection in about nine minutes on five grounds, none of them the same and none of them wrong. The man with the barrow refused in nine words: *he would rather be sent for than be found*. **A woman of about fifty-eight who sweeps a yard behind a house of assizes in a township of about nine hundred people sent nine words nine days back on a handcart — *tell him the yard will outlive all of us* — and nobody carried an answer and nobody is going to, and a woman of about thirty-four took the nine words and put them in the front of her shirt.** **The want of the woman of about fifty-one is owed in Block 0002 and is spoken once in the volume: she has never been asked what she wants in twenty-two years and she has said one thing about it out loud, to nobody, at an ebb, and nobody has acted on it and about four of the nine people who heard her have carried it toward the four.** A successor that has her asked what she wants by a room is spending the volume's second want; a successor that has the four ask the seven for something is spending the whole of the block.

119. **The three names, the budget, and the two slots that are unspent.** The budget for Volume 10 is five. **Batch 0001 spent three — `Marda Oakes`, 51; `Rusen Ball`, 29; `Halla Wray`, 63 — so the volume stands at 3 of 5 with two unspent, and Block 0002's whole share is those two.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns those three and nothing else. **A name is the only asset in this manuscript that cannot be audited after the fact, and the three that are spent are all working people and a fourth and a fifth should be too, and the first two volumes of this series learned the same lesson in the other direction.**

120. **The foreshore and the eleven houses, and the arithmetic nobody may simplify.** Two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand and broken shell, passable for about four hours out of twelve and about twice a day. **Eleven houses, about nine hundred people, four with a person in them who answers for it and seven with nobody in them, and the seven are the ones that are used.** The sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps, and the ninth is three black stones a storm put there in a month nobody living can name. **About nine cart-loads of shell a day off four boats go into the pans of four families; a wagon can go one way at the bottom of the water and cannot come back the same tide; a man on foot can go at any hour and gets his boots filled and says nothing about it.** A woman of about fifty-one keeps the count on a piece of driftwood with **twenty-two notches in the edge, one for each year, the twenty-second not cut, and it goes in when the water is off a mark that has not been off in twenty-two years.** **The notches are years and not tides and a writer who makes them tides breaks the object's whole reason for existing.** Twenty-two notches in six places in Batch 0001 and about forty in five of them, and the number was wrong the same way in all of them and no instrument in this project can see a count that is consistent.

121. **The wall, and the four heads, and the surveyor.** A wall of stone eleven paces long and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, laid in four days out of a barge, curing in wet sand, taking the tide twice a day, and about four inches of sand banking against its far side in four months. **A surveyor of about forty-four with a chain and a boy of about seventeen with a rod, and a piece of paper that came away with three heads filled in and a fourth head empty.** The fourth head wants the name of the person who will be answerable for the road. **Nobody filled it in and nobody is going to, and the surveyor's own remark that he was handed a figure of men by a man who did not have it was said once, to nobody, and has not been said again and will not be.** A man of about thirty-four with a sheet is on that sand, is not named, is not confronted, and **nothing in this volume says whether his being there and his being one of the four men with a crowbar are connected, and Volume 10 may not say it and Batch 0002 may not say it.**

122. **The accent, the memory, and the eleven lines of a notebook.** He said ten words in the accent of the far side of a salt-store in front of about six people on a foreshore and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow repeated them back correctly. **By the twenty-sixth day of Frostmonth he said a sentence in that accent to a woman of twenty-nine in a room over a weigh-house and she did not notice, and the narration says she has not noticed yet.** On the thirteenth day of Hearthmonth she said six words to him on a made road and then thirty-five, and he answered in the same accent in a sentence of twenty-three words that is printed on the page and carries no figure, and about nine people going past heard the two of them. **And that is the fourth time the beat plays and the first time he does not hear the difference, and the page dates it to five weeks earlier, which is 1188 less 1153.** The beat has now played four times in four shapes and it is a spent device: a woman says a line, it comes back in her accent, he hears both, and nobody says anything about it. **A fifth instance is a repetition and not a development, and a raw-token check returns clean on a repetition, so this is one of the two things in this handoff no instrument can check.** **A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line, and the word is the one for a blacked-in hearth in a courtyard about four hundred miles off and it is the right word for the wrong room, and he did not cross anything out and did not add a date and did not add a name.** **A kitchen he cannot place, in which about two of the details came from a courtyard; he got it wrong twice in Chapter 460 and got the window right and the door wrong and left it alone.** Nobody has said one word to him about any of it. **The mechanism is not explained, no rule is stated, no cut is made, the roadmark is not looked at, and no System panel is printed in this volume.**

123. **The crossing budget, spent, and what a successor may not spend.** About three attempted crossings a season at any stage. **Block 0001 spent the whole of it: nine days from the third of Frostmonth to the twelfth, four days from the nineteenth to the twenty-second, and two days from the twenty-fourth to the twenty-fifth, inside about four weeks. The three do not overlap, the protagonist is on the main-world foreshore in Chapters 456, 457 and 460, and he is not on the foreshore at all in Chapter 458, which is the chapter the second crossing runs through.** `outline/volume-10.md` §4 gives the volume three crossings in Block 1, none in Block 2, two in Block 3, one in Block 4 and one in Block 5, and **a Block 0002 writer should not read §4 as forbidding the crossing it forbids without also reading item 123, which is the honest way round.**

124. **The bar four miles west of Marl, untouched, and the four figures a successor may want and must derive.** **Block 0001 carries no figure for the bar and that is a decision and not an oversight.** The method at `state/continuity.md` 1109 was re-run for this block and reproduces all six of Volume 09's canon figures at days 949, 972, 1038, 1080, 1130 and 1140 exactly, and the day count at 1141 is **362 days, fifty-one weeks and five days, 204 up-days less 51 Sundays is 153, and the five-day remainder block begins on a Wednesday and adds two, which is 155 paid days, 1,395 pence and twenty-nine marks and three pence.** **None of those four figures is printed in Block 0001 and a successor that needs one derives it from day 779 and the method and not from this item.** The sheet under a stone, the fold in a coat that is not his and the receipt in the boy's own coat are all unopened and unread and Block 0001 does not mention any of them.

125. **What did not move, and the whole of the list, because the block is a new volume and a new volume is where this project loses facts.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The two villages are not back on a road. The corridor's rule is bad and its rate is not printed. The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The register is at seven questions and no findings and the day-book is at nine and the count under the fourth step is at nine. The three bars are three objects. Hushwater was not taken and neither figure was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled. **The nine who met eight times and then met no more are not contacted, not quoted, not sent for and not put in a room, and a name in a hat is not put to any of their use. The ninth word is not said in this block, the gate at the end of the nine-mile road is not opened and nobody is sent to it, the three invented things are not mentioned, the receipt is not mentioned, the four unpaid things are not mentioned, the woman of about fifty with the board is not thanked, the question in the drawer is not answered and its drawer is not opened, and the protagonist's own sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head is in his coat and is not shown, not lost, not read and not asked for.**

126. **And what Volume 10 Batch 0002 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the eleven above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend the arithmetic by which it was identified. Do not answer the question from a road, and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men, and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a tenth thing to any of the three nines, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure and do not print a rate for the tide road. Do not make the driftwood's notches into tides. Do not fill the fourth head of the form. Do not say whether the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and the four men with a crowbar are connected. Do not have the nine of Volume 09 meet, or be quoted, or be sent for, or be put in a room, and do not have a council of nine on the foreshore. Do not have the protagonist hold the middle, and do not have anybody thank the keeper of records, the woman of about fifty with a board, or the woman of about fifty-eight with a slate. Do not compare the nine-mile road with the tide road, and do not send anybody from one to the other. Do not play the accent beat a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times. Do not schedule anything for the first of a month, the tide road, the press, the bell, the seed vault, or Chapter 900. And do not edit `outline/volume-09.md`; the three wrong rows in its §17 are owed by whoever owns the plan.**

---

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0002 carries to Volume 10 Batch 0003

**This section is written by the phase that planned Volume 10 Batch 0002 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0003's whole instruction. `outline/volume-10.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` items 1129 to 1150 are the facts, and item 1149 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's repair passes and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Twenty-two threads, numbered from 131, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

131. **The frame Batch 0003 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor, and the chain was re-run for this block and reproduces every anchor the plan prints. **Block 0003 is days 1237 to 1284, the second of Thawmonth YR 319 to the nineteenth of Greenmonth YR 319, forty-eight days inclusive, and Block 0003's ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived from the day number and not read off a card or off this paragraph.** **The whole of Volume 09 and the whole of Volume 10 Batch 0001 are YR 318. Batch 0002 is YR 318 to the twenty-ninth of Goatmonth and then YR 319 from the first of Thawmonth. Every date in Block 0003 is YR 319, and the month starts inside Block 0003 are 1236, 1266 and 1296, of which 1236 is the last day of Batch 0002.** Day 1237 is a Saturday and a market morning. Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays, and **the volume carries sixty-eight of them at fourteen a block, and Block 0003's fourteen are 1240, 1242, 1247, 1249, 1254, 1256, 1261, 1263, 1268, 1270, 1275, 1277, 1282 and 1284, derived here once for the successor and not to be trusted as a printed list, because a printed list is a certificate and a certificate is the last place a defect survives.** Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and report them as two figures and never as one. `state/continuity.md` 1129.

132. **The six answers this volume has now spent, and the finding that is the volume's own, in full, because Batch 0003 is the block that has to live on top of it.** Five are wrong for a road and the sixth is not an answer to the question. **A boundary of nine stones is inherited because it is not being moved.** **A turn of a year ends on a day somebody else fixed about four months early.** **A hat is how a township says that it does not mind, and a name on a shelf can be found.** **A place with no gate is not a refusal, and nothing is not a keeping.** **A room that is let by the day whose keeper goes home works right up until somebody wants to know who holds it.** And the sixth, which nobody proposed: **that the only thing that has ever kept the tide road in use for about twenty-two years is eleven houses that somebody's job, and that nobody keeps it, and that the four of the eleven are where four people's work happens to stop and the seven are the way out of the weather because nobody is standing in them, and that not one person on that shore agreed to any of it.** `state/continuity.md` 1132, 1133.

133. **The wants, both spoken once, and neither may be restated.** **The woman of about fifty-one's want is in `chapter-0466.md` in a hundred and three words in two turns and it is that she wants to be asked for a day by somebody and told no, in the winter, and that she does not want a stone and does not want a head on anything, and that she wants the count of that shore to stay in her and in about nine heads of about nine people and nowhere else at all.** **The woman of sixty-three's want is in `chapter-0467.md` in ten words and it is that the road stay exactly as it is.** Neither woman was thanked. About four people on each shore are the kind of person who will carry both, and the four of the eleven were not on that sand and have not been told. **A Batch 0003 chapter that has either woman say a want again has spent the volume's second want, and a chapter that has anybody thank her for it has spent it differently.** `state/continuity.md` 1134.

134. **The four clauses about a wagon, and what became of them, and that they are not a toll and not a rate and not a keeper.** Whoever is nearest it gets it out, nobody is called for, nobody is paid, and nobody is asked afterwards. **The landward-end room carries them and has said them out loud once. The seaward-end room carries them and will say them at the next ebb. The port four days off does not carry them and nobody refused anything, because a weigh-house is a room where about nine hundred people are paid twice in seven days and a man in it cannot say a sentence that says *nobody is paid* without making three of the four clauses a lie.** No fixed day is fixed, no register is opened, no head is put on anything, no second meeting is scheduled, and there is no council. **Batch 0003 may not turn the four clauses into a rate and may not print a rate for the tide road and may not make anybody a keeper of them.** `state/continuity.md` 1131, 1135.

135. **The form's fourth head, and the two roads, and the man with the sheet, and the wall, and exactly where each stands.** **The fourth head is empty, nobody filled it in, nobody says that anybody has filled it in somewhere else, and the only time it is mentioned in Block 0002 it is mentioned as empty by a man of thirty-two with a limp who was asked whether he is needed and who offered to carry one sentence about it four days up a made road and was not taken up on.** **The wall is eleven paces long and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, took the tide twice a day on the first of Thawmonth YR 319 exactly as it has twice a day since the four days it took to lay, has a second footing course on it nine inches lower, and about four inches of sand banks against its far side in four months. No surveyor is on that sand in Block 0002 and nothing about the wall is scheduled.** **A man of about thirty-four with a sheet has been on that sand four times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and there is nothing in the four of them that says why he comes when he comes, and nobody has asked him and he has not said, and nothing in this volume says whether his being there and his being one of the four men with a crowbar in Volume 09 are connected.** **The nine-mile road and the tide road were not compared on a page in Block 0002, no one was sent from one to the other, the gate was not opened and nothing was scheduled for it, and the ninth word was not said once.** `state/continuity.md` 1144.

136. **The one sentence the man of thirty-two with a limp said on a page in Block 0002, and the fact that he is a witness in this block and not in it after it.** In `chapter-0469.md`, in three turns and a hundred and ninety words, asked whether he is needed: **if you want one sentence carried, then I am needed, and if you do not want it carried then I am not, and I have not asked which and I am not going to, and I have been asked to hold a thing four times in about seven months and I said yes to one of them and it is not this one, and a man who stands in the middle of three places is a man who can be sent for to all three, and this shore is not the middle of anywhere, it is the end of about nine hundred paces of hard sand.** **He conducted nothing, got nothing back, overrules nobody and is not the reason anything on that foreshore works. Nobody took up the offer in Block 0002 and Block 0003 may take it up or may not, and if it takes it up it is not his doing and it is not a fifth ask to hold a thing.** `state/continuity.md` 1136.

137. **The accent, and the memory, and the second loss of this volume, and where each stands.** **The accent is not played a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times and it was not played in Block 0002 at all: the only thing the man of thirty-two says on a page in this block is about whether he is needed, and not one of the hundred and ninety-one words is about his own voice.** No kitchen is placed or displaced in this block and nobody says one word to him about any of it. **A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line, a road-marked left palm warm at about the fourth hour, a sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head, and two unreconciled numbers in his coat are all untouched and unmentioned in Block 0002.** The eleven lines are Volume 09's and the eleventh line was used in Chapter 459 and there is still no twelfth line. `state/continuity.md` 1147.

138. **The driftwood, the eleven houses, and the arithmetic nobody may simplify.** **Two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand and broken shell, passable for about four hours out of every twelve and about twice a day, and for about eight hours out of every twelve the seaward half is under about two hundred paces of water.** Eleven houses, about nine hundred people, **four of the eleven with a person in them who answers for it and seven with nobody in them, and the seven are the ones that are used.** **A piece of driftwood about the length of a forearm and about two inches through, wedged in a driven post about five inches square, with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, the twenty-second not cut, and it goes in when the water goes off a mark that has not been off in twenty-two years, and the water did not go off that mark on the first of Thawmonth YR 319 and the twenty-second notch is still not cut.** About nine cart-loads of shell a day off four boats into four pans. The sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps and the ninth is three black stones a storm put there in a month nobody living can name. `state/continuity.md` 1130, 1143.

139. **The name budget with the arithmetic, and what is left.** The budget for Volume 10 is five. **Batch 0001 spent three, `Marda Oakes` 51, `Rusen Ball` 29 and `Halla Wray` 63. Batch 0002 spent one, `Tobin Kernick` 53, in `chapter-0468.md`, one occurrence. The arithmetic is 3 + 1 = 4 of 5 and one slot remains, and it has to serve Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 together.** **A capitalised two-word sweep over Batch 0002's ten files returns `Tobin Kernick` once and no other proper noun of two words, and a sweep over a manuscript that spends six more names across three blocks will find twenty-odd names of two words in a volume and every one of them is either a return or a place, and the three-assembly block did not want nine names and did not have them.** **Before spending the last slot, sweep the whole manuscript for the surname and not only the state files: `Cray` is spent at `chapter-0254.md` and this block rejected it, and `Quillon`, `Harrow`, `Cresswell`, `Penrose`, `Vosper`, `Karrick`, `Pentreath`, `Kernick`'s neighbours and six others were rejected on the same sweep.** `state/continuity.md` 1139.

140. **The money, read on the page, and the spent columns the next block must check against.** **Ten footed columns in Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62 pence, ten distinct totals, all footed in pence with the sum list on the page and the total after it.** Add them to the fifty-four already spent: **Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and Volume 10 Batch 0001 at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, and Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62, which is sixty-four columns and must not be sixty-four distinct figures.** **The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository; this phase checked against the sixty-four printed columns and did not re-read the forty, and a successor with the item in front of it can make the stronger check and should say which it made.** A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page. The four barred numerals are at zero across Batch 0002's ten files. `state/continuity.md` 1137.

141. **The speech-length figures and the rule, and the six that were wrong the first time.** **Batch 0002 prints eleven figures and all eleven foot, none preceded by *about*, on the declared basis of a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends, with a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span.** A speech printed as two quoted spans with an attribution between them is one turn and a figure attached to it is a figure about both printed lines; a quoted paragraph on its own with no attribution between it and the next is a second turn and a figure about the two is the sum of both. **Six of this block's eleven figures were wrong in a first printing and every one of them was wrong by drift after a sentence near it was rewritten: a hundred and forty-five that should have been a hundred and sixty-two, a hundred and twenty-two that should have been a hundred and nine, a hundred and seventy-four that was a hundred and sixty, a hundred and two that was a hundred and five, and ninety-five that was sixty-eight, and a hundred and five that was eighty-five because a span was counted and not a turn.** **A figure attached to a speech the page does not print is the defect this project has paid for most and Batch 0002 adds none to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and none to the protected unprinted figures.** `state/continuity.md` 1138, 1143.

142. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts about the world and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this thread for any of them.** **Block 0002 is 15,463 in ten chapters**, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added, and the per-chapter figures are **1,797 / 1,668 / 1,582 / 1,504 / 1,519 / 1,372 / 1,452 / 1,679 / 1,444 / 1,446**. **The manuscript is 1,419,702 in four hundred and seventy chapters and the four hundred and sixty before it are 1,404,239 on the same basis and are unchanged; the sum over the files is the authority and no concatenated stream was counted.** Volume 10 is 32,369 in twenty chapters. `about` is **480 in 15,463, which is 31.04 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-sensitive numerator, or **485 and 31.37** case-insensitive. `the whole of` is 25 and `about four people` is 7. `---` rules 0. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes **0, 0, 0 and 0**, and there is no zero-punctuation mandate in this volume. Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file. Curly quotation marks balancing in every file and on every line. **A raw-token sweep at eight words, headings stripped and bold datelines in, returns a longest shared run against the four hundred and sixty prior of eight words and it is the dateline frame, and 494 repeated eight-word shingles inside the block of which every one is dateline frame, money-column frame, house footing sentence, closing frame, speech-certification frame or a canon phrase of the foreshore, except one thirteen-word sentence that two women say to the same man and that Chapter 467 now explains on the page. Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, the ten new against the whole manuscript and against themselves: 0 and 0, which is the correct result for both.** `state/continuity.md` 1140, 1142.

143. **The barred tokens, counted before they were enforced, and the two manuscript figures that do not reproduce.** **Across Batch 0002's ten files, whole word, case-insensitive, every hit read: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` at zero, and so are `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six` and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` and `hundredweight`.** The barred kin-term is at **2** across four hundred and seventy chapters and is spent. **At manuscript scope on this phase's basis, which is a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends, whole word, case-insensitive: `hemp` 70, `thirty-six` 20, `fifty-two` 102, `sixty-one` 142, `sixty-six` 249, `failed` 21, `seam` 49, `roof` 99, `coping` 170, `melody` 2, `Culling` 11, `eastern` 40, `charter` 20, `Gatehouse` 3, `stage` 32, `ledger` 141, `qualification` 2, `system` 21, `panel` 2.** **Two of these do not reproduce the live handoff, `Gatehouse` at 3 against 2 and `brother` at 213 against 172, and this phase did not resolve the difference and changed no figure over it; count them again on your own basis rather than take either.** Count before enforcing: these are prohibitions written for two volumes against denominators of two and zero. `state/continuity.md` 1141.

144. **The three bars, the three nines, the two counts and the unattributed statement, all still separate.** **The kerb bar, the market bar and the fourth-months bar four miles west of Marl are three objects. Block 0002 touches none of them and carries no figure for the third, which is the second block in a row to make that decision and not an oversight.** The page under the fourth step is at nine and no tenth asking was put to it; the day-book is at nine; the register is at seven questions and no findings; the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine and none of the four is added to another or to a figure of persons. **About nine hundred sheets a month and about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day are printed once in Batch 0002, in `chapter-0464.md`, and are not added and are not converted into one another.** **The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and Batch 0002 prints no fifth.** `state/continuity.md` 1145.

145. **The nine who refused in Volume 09, and what may and may not be done with them, and the want of a woman of about fifty-eight.** **The nine are not contacted, not quoted, not sent for and not put in a room, there is no council of nine on that foreshore, and the word is not printed in Batch 0002.** The nine words from a yard are referred to once in Batch 0002, as a fact about a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow who took them off a handcart and put them in the front of her shirt and has said no once to a man of about twenty-nine who asked her whether she was going to answer them, **and nobody carried an answer and nobody is going to and the nine words are not printed again.** A successor that has any of the nine meet has undone the volume; a successor that has them form an office, a register or a crown has undone it worse. `state/continuity.md` 1146.

146. **What did not move in Batch 0002, and the whole of the list, because a new block is where this project loses facts.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent and the word *failed* is used of nothing in this volume. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list and the register is neither destroyed nor withdrawn. The two villages are not back on a road and are not told why. The corridor's rule is bad and is not improved on and its rate is not printed and nothing in Batch 0002 is a rate. The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands and neither is adjourned, retold or closed. The city is on a shelf four hundred miles off and its three protected numerals are at zero and it is sent nothing. The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled, **and no crossing was made in Batch 0002 and none is scheduled, which is what the plan's Block 2 budget allows.** Stage 4 is half-spent and the qualification is not earned and not claimed and a sweep of the ten new files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and no chapter says the ledger does or does not move, and a System panel is printed nowhere in this block and nowhere in this volume. The thirteen unlooked-at items are unlooked at and the Hushwater thread was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. `state/continuity.md` 1146.

147. **The two dateline frames and which chapters are in which, and the rule for a chapter set on the foreshore that carries a main-world date.** **Chapters 466 and 467 of Batch 0002 are in the coast frame** and carry an ebb and an hour and no month, no day of month and no weekday, and the ebb is written as a position in a man's own arrival because an ebb counted from a fixed point cannot be consistent across five weeks. **The other eight are in the main-world frame and four of those eight are set on or at the foreshore and carry a main-world date, which is the precedent `chapter-0458.md` sets.** **No month name and no weekday is spoken in a character's mouth anywhere in a chapter set on that foreshore, and every month and weekday token in those files is in the narrator's dateline or in the narrator's Section Five frame.** A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no chapter adds any two of the three. **A writer who needs a main-world day number for a coast chapter writes it in the state file and not in the dateline, and the two coast chapters of Batch 0002 are main-world days 1229 and 1231.** `state/continuity.md` 1130.

148. **The one name this volume spent and the want that came with it, in full, because a successor cannot infer a want from a name.** **`Tobin Kernick`, 53, has driven a salt wagon between the port four days up and the foreshore for nineteen years, goes down on the ebb, stands about two hours and comes back on the flood, and nobody sets him a time and nobody has ever set him one.** He stops at four of the eleven: a pan at the fourth house and a fire behind it that never goes out, a lease at the ninth, a widow with the last of a boat at the eleventh who buys four sacks and will not sell them back to him, and a house at the seventh that a man stands in and about four of them will not stop at. **His want, unrelated to anything else in the volume, is that if anybody ever comes down that sand and asks him to keep anything at all he will say no, because a man who cannot put a horse down is not a keeper of anything and never will be, and he sleeps in his wagon and has nowhere between the port and that shore to stable a horse.** There is a man two counties up who has been asking him about taking a house on that sand since about a year ago and he has said no about nine times, and about nine people on that shore have heard about that man and about two hundred have not and the rest have never been asked. `state/continuity.md` 1133.

149. **The defects this block's own hand found, and the two instruments that found them, and the standing lesson, which is now nineteen batches running.** **Twenty-two sites in ten classes across nine of the ten files, and only two of the ten classes were visible to any instrument this project owns.** **Six lines in six chapters had an odd number of bold markers, and six speech spans in five chapters had the closing curly quotation mark inside the bold rather than outside it or missing altogether, and the quotation balance found the second class in about a second across a block boundary and the bold-parity sweep could not see it because the bold was closed.** A speech figure counted a quoted span instead of a turn turned a correct one wrong in Chapter 466, and a drifted figure turned a correct one wrong in two more chapters. A sequencing break put a paragraph between a man saying a thing and the thing he said. **A boy who was about nine in Volume 09's last chapters was first printed about eleven in a chapter set a year and three quarters later, which is the cheapest defect in the block.** A man of about twenty-seven was placed at a meeting held before he was born. A band of about a hundred and four was first printed as a band of about nine hundred. Nine generations walked nine stones in one sentence and then walked them again. **And the retired reporting frame appeared eleven times in the four-word form *have said since that*, in nine new files and in one completed chapter of Batch 0001, and all eleven were removed, and the one in a prior batch's chapter was restored from git rather than edited, because a batch may not repair a prior batch's prose on a hunch.** A repair pass is not a safer thing to review than a batch, a script that edits prose is not a repair pass until a person has read the result, and a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale. `state/continuity.md` 1143, 1150.

150. **What Block 0003 inherits as a fact about the world, in one paragraph, and it is not a summary of the block.** Three port assemblies met once in about forty years and agreed on four clauses about a wagon in a channel, and two of the three will carry the clauses and the third cannot because it is a weigh-house, and they meet again on no fixed day. A woman of about fifty-one at the seaward end has been asked what she wants once in twenty-two years and answered in a hundred and three words, and a woman of sixty-three has been asked once in forty-one years and answered in ten words, and neither has been thanked. A man of about fifty-three who carts salt has said out loud that nobody keeps that road and that the whole of it is eleven houses that somebody's job, and the volume has four of five names spent with one slot left. A man of about thirty-two with a limp has been asked whether he is needed, has offered to carry one sentence about a wall four days up a made road, and nobody has taken him up on it. **The year turned on a Friday on two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand on the first of Thawmonth YR 319 and nothing on it knew, and the twenty-second notch is not cut.** `state/continuity.md` 1148.

151. **The prompt Batch 0003 is written against, and the three items of the plan that are binding on it.** **`outline/volume-10.md` §8 Block 3 is the midpoint reversal: the tide road proves that paths influence one another through trade and migration, that a price set on one shore is a wage on another, and that a disease and a word and a song all travel on a road that nobody owns, and no path remains untouched once it opens contact, and the volume's own argument against a keeper is the argument against contact.** §14 fixes the knowing: *he knows in Block 3 and not before, and the knowing is a chapter and not a paragraph, and it arrives as arithmetic and not as a revelation.* **§4 gives Block 0003 two crossings and the volume's season's budget is already spent, so a writer who reads §4 must read it with `state/continuity.md` 123 and know that the three crossings of Block 0001 used the whole of about three a season and that this is the honest way round.** And §13's name budget is five and one slot remains.

152. **And what Batch 0003 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the twenty-one above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend the arithmetic by which it was identified. Do not answer the question from a road, and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men, do not report the span as a repair, and do not put either of the two men on a page. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars and do not put a figure on the third one without deriving it from day 779 and the method at `state/continuity.md` 1109. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, an eighth register entry, or a tenth thing to any of the three nines, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure and do not print a rate for the tide road. Do not make the driftwood's notches into tides. Do not fill the fourth head of the form, and do not have a character say that anybody has filled it in somewhere else. Do not say whether the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and the four men with a crowbar are connected. Do not have the nine of Volume 09 meet, be quoted, be sent for, or be put in a room, and do not have a council on the foreshore. Do not have the protagonist hold the middle, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works. Do not have anybody thank the keeper of records, the woman of about fifty with a board, or the woman of about fifty-eight with a slate, and do not restate the want of either woman on the foreshore. Do not compare the nine-mile road with the tide road, and do not send anybody from one to the other. Do not play the accent beat a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times. Do not schedule anything for the tide road, the press, the bell, the seed vault, or Chapter 900. Do not spend the last of the five names without sweeping the whole manuscript for the surname first. Do not improve on a want, on the sixth answer, or on the four clauses, and do not turn any of the five wrong answers into a worse one than it is. And do not edit `outline/volume-09.md`; its three wrong rows in §17 are owed by whoever owns the plan.**

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0002 craft repair carries to Volume 10 Batch 0003

**These six threads were added by the craft repair pass on the block and they are carried to Batch 0003 with items 131 to 152 above. The highest number in this file is now 158, and item 158 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do on the strength of this pass.**

153. **The lead's name is on the page and it stays on the page.** `Aren` and `Kest` were both at zero across the twenty chapters of Volume 10 as Batch 0002 was first written, and the prompt that governed the block required him to be named. `chapter-0469.md` now carries him in the narration, in a speech attribution and in the closing frame. **Batch 0003 is the block in which Chapter 479 is his own arithmetic and he is told by somebody who is not a knight that he has been the reason a thing moved, and a chapter about a man's reckoning that does not print the man's name is a chapter about a noun phrase. Sweep the ten new files for `Aren` and for `Kest` and report both counts, and a zero is a failure and not a style.**

154. **The punctuation rule was wrong and was corrected, and the correction is the one thing in this pass a successor will most likely undo by accident.** The four hundred and seventy chapters before this pass had no question mark, no exclamation mark, no semicolon and no em dash, and a previous version of the Batch 0003 prompt called that the house convention. It is not. It was an accident across five volumes and about six hundred and twenty thousand words, it produced a chapter with no dialogue in it, and `workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §3 item 3 now says so in terms. **A question asked aloud takes a question mark, and a sentence that wants a semicolon or a dash takes one, and there is still no minimum and still no target.**

155. **The `about` rule is a reading rule and not a number, and both halves of it are binding.** The hedge stays on a figure the world is honestly vague about and comes off a figure a person in the room could have counted. **The figures that keep it: about nine hundred people, about nine hundred paces, about four hours out of twelve, about nine years, about twenty-two years, about nineteen years, about forty days of walking, about forty yards. The figures that lose it: nine people, four people, nine feet, four hundred yards, forty minutes, about a hundred and four, about nine hundred paces long when it is a measured length and about nine hundred paces when it is the shore's own estimate.** Block 0002 stands at about 16.2 per 1,000 on a whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, down from 31.04, and the count that matters is how many survivors are doing real work.

156. **The eleven speech-length figures foot, and if a repair shortens a speech the figure moves with it.** All eleven were recounted from the printed speech after this pass, with the attribution excluded and a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span. **Chapter 463 prints sixty-one and a hundred and sixty; Chapter 466 prints a hundred and three, of twenty-nine and seventy-four; Chapter 468 prints a hundred and seventy-four and a hundred and three; Chapter 469 prints a hundred and eight and a hundred and ninety; Chapter 462 prints twenty-eight and forty-four; Chapter 465 prints ninety-five; Chapter 467 prints one and ten.
