Seven of the eleven were re-footed in this pass, every one of them off by exactly the number of words the pass removed, and none of them was wrong when it was written. A repair pass that moves a figure it did not need to move is the same defect as one that misses a figure it did, and one such was caught here and put back.**

157. **Four volumes of the same drift are still unrepaired and are owed to the author, not to a batch.** The `about` rate is 26.5 per 1,000 in Volume 09, 21.2 in Volume 08, 20.9 in Volume 06 and 19.9 in Volume 07; the zero-punctuation run holds across roughly six hundred and twenty thousand words before Chapter 461; and Volume 10's first ten chapters still have `Aren` at zero and little dialogue. **A batch may not go and rewrite them, and this pass deliberately did not, so a successor that sees a Volume 09 page and wants to fix it is repeating a boundary violation and not finishing the job. The plan of record is the author's.**

158. **And what Batch 0003 must not do on the strength of this pass, and it is this thread and not a summary of the five above.** **Do not re-assert that the four hundred and seventy chapters establish a house convention of no punctuation, and do not set a question asked aloud with a full stop inside the quotation. Do not let the protagonist go through a chapter without his name on the page, and do not treat *a man of about thirty-two with a limp* as a name. Do not put the hedge back on a figure somebody in the room could have counted, and do not take the hedge off a figure the world is honestly vague about, and in particular do not harden about nine hundred people, about nine hundred paces, about four hours out of twelve, about nine years or about twenty-two years. Do not edit a speech-length figure without recounting the speech, and do not print a figure for a speech the page does not print. Do not repair a prior batch's prose on a hunch, and in particular do not touch `chapter-0460.md`, which belongs to Batch 0001 and still carries one instance of the retired reporting frame. Do not restore anything this pass removed from a fact about the world: the money totals stand at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62, the eleven houses and the four and the seven stand, the driftwood stands at twenty-two notches with the twenty-second not cut, the two wants are spoken once each and neither woman may be thanked, the sixth answer stands, the fourth head stands empty, and the antagonist is not named.**

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0003 carries to Volume 10 Batch 0004

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 10 Batch 0003 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0004's whole instruction. `outline/volume-10.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` items 1157 to 1172 are the facts, and item 172 is the list of what Batch 0004 must not do. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's last edit and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Twenty threads, numbered from 159, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

159. **The frame Batch 0004 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor. **Block 0004 is days 1285 to 1332, forty-eight inclusive, that is the twentieth of Mudmonth YR 319 to the seventh of Longlight YR 319, and the ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived from the day number and not read off a card or off this paragraph.** **Every date in Block 0004 is YR 319, and the year does not turn inside it. The month starts inside it are 1296 and 1326, and 1326 is the last of them, and Longlight is when boundary oath-stones are renewed, and the oath-stone of Volume 09's first of Longlight is gone and no second renewal is scheduled.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays, and the block's fourteen are derivable and **are not printed here, because a printed day list is a certificate and a certificate is the last place a defect survives.** **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third.** `state/continuity.md` 1157, and note that this block's own prompt gave a right day number and two wrong attributes of it, and the attributes were consistent with each other, and that is the class.

160. **The block's finding, in full, because Block 0004 is the block that has to act on it.** **A cart-load of shell is six pence in the only book in the trade and nine pence in the mouths of three ports; the difference is three pence; nine cart-loads a day on about two hundred days a year at three pence is five thousand four hundred pence a year; a widow at the eleventh house lives at twenty pence a day; two hundred and seventy days is nine months; and two hundred and seventy days at twenty pence a day is five thousand four hundred pence.** So a permanent connection is a fixed price, a fixed price is a book, a book is a person who answers for it, and that is all a keeper is, and the whole of what the fixed price would take out of that one household is nine months of her winter. **The same five thousand four hundred pence is the annual shortfall of a salt wagon that costs fifty-four pence a day and earns twenty-seven, and the block did not have anybody put the two copies of the number together and neither man has ever seen the other's.** A successor may not improve on the finding, may not round it, and may not have a character explain it in a sentence that is easier than the arithmetic. `state/continuity.md` 1159.

161. **The money, and the conversion, and the ten columns Block 0004 must not collide with.** **Ten footed columns in Batch 0003 at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96 pence, ten distinct totals, each footed in pence with the sum list printed before the total.** Add them to the sixty-four already spent: **Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and Volume 10 Batch 0001 at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62, and Batch 0003 at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96, which is seventy-four columns and must not be seventy-four distinct figures.** The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository; this phase checked against the sixty-four printed columns and did not re-read the forty, and a successor with the item in front of it can make the stronger check and should say which it made. A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page. **The four barred numerals are barred as figures as well as as words, and a speech-length certification is a figure, and a block that writes its speech figures after its prose will put a barred numeral on a page that a sweep for barred tokens does not find.** `state/continuity.md` 1160, 1161.

162. **The wagon, the sixth house, the lease, the widow, the four and the seven, and the whole of the instrument.** **A salt wagon costs fifty-four pence a day and earns twenty-seven and has done for nineteen years, and he is paid three sacks a trip and the ninth house has paid nine pence a sack for eleven years, and that conversion is made once on the page and the salt is not in a money column and the money is not in the salt.** He goes down on the ebb, stands about two hours and comes back on the flood. He stops at four of the eleven: a pan at the fourth house and a fire behind it that never goes out, a lease at the ninth, a widow with the last of a boat at the eleventh who buys four sacks and will not sell them back to him, and a house at the seventh that a man stands in and four of us will not stop at. **Eleven houses, four with a person in them who answers for it and seven with nobody in them, and the seven are the ones that are used, and four and seven are never added to eleven in a column.** Two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand and broken shell, passable for about four hours out of twelve and about twice a day, and for about eight hours out of twelve the seaward half is under about two hundred paces of water. **A piece of driftwood about the length of a forearm and two inches through, wedged in a driven post five inches square, with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, the twenty-second not cut, and it goes in when the water goes off a mark that has not been off in twenty-two years, and the water did not go off that mark on the first of Thawmonth YR 319 and did not go off it on the nineteenth of Mudmonth either.** About nine cart-loads of shell a day off four boats into four pans. The sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps and the ninth is three black stones a storm put there in a month nobody living can name. `state/continuity.md` 1158, 1168.

163. **The man of about thirty-four with the sheet, the second man, and the four people who saw, and what may and may not be said about any of it.** **He has been on that sand five times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and there is nothing in the five of them that says why he comes when he comes, and nobody has asked him and he has not said, and in nineteen days there was a sixth time available and this block did not take it.** **On the fifth time there was a second man, about twenty-eight, carrying nothing, with no chain and no rod and no sheet, who did not stand at the stone, who looked at the sand for about twenty minutes and then at the eleven houses one at a time from the landward end, and whom four people followed with their eyes without knowing they were doing it, and who was gone before anybody noticed him leave.** The only conversation of that tide was a jug of water with a broken handle and the man of about thirty-four did not offer the second man any. **Nothing in this volume says who sent the second man, and nothing in this volume says whether the man with the sheet and the four men with a crowbar are connected, and Block 0004 may not say it either and may not print a name on the second man.** A Block 0004 writer who needs a Crown office on that sand has four men who are not villains and doing what a road office does, and the plan is on the page as a wall, a deed and a figure of men in a yard. `state/continuity.md` 1169.

164. **The wall, exactly, and the price it has already caused, and neither is illegal in any way anybody has named.** **Eleven paces long and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, laid in four days out of a barge, with a second footing course nine inches lower, and about four inches of sand banked against its far side in four months, taking the tide twice a day exactly as it has since the four days it took to lay.** **The price it has already caused is nine weeks of the ninth house not buying, which is about a sixth of a wagon year, which is nine hundred pence, which is forty-five days of the winter of a woman at the eleventh house, and it was caused by a sentence of sixty-four words about a wall said four days up a made road to one man.** **The form's fourth head is empty, nobody filled it in, nobody says that anybody has filled it in somewhere else, and the one time it was mentioned in Block 0003 it was mentioned as empty, by a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow asking whether it is still empty.** Nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall. `state/continuity.md` 1169.

165. **The protagonist, in the state Block 0004 inherits him in.** **Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent; the qualification is not earned and not claimed.** A permanent limp in the right leg; a ferrier's stick he cannot lean on; he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight and is not going to. A road-marked left palm, warm at about the fourth hour, still untold. A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line. A sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket, not shown and not asked for. Two unreconciled numbers in his coat. **`Aren` 7 and `Kest` 7 across Batch 0003's ten files, in Chapters 472, 477, 479 and 480, and a block that spends a chapter on his own arithmetic and leaves him a noun phrase has undone the last repair pass.** He conducted nothing, got nothing back, overrules nobody, and is not the reason anything on that foreshore works; in Chapter 480 he carries one armful of wood and one woman thanks him for it. **The volume's season's budget of about three was spent in Block 0001 and Batch 0003 used none of the two crossings the outline allows it, which leaves the two available to Block 0004, and a writer who uses both must say so in its own state entry.** The accent is not played in Block 0003 and no kitchen is placed or displaced, and the accent must not be played a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times. `state/continuity.md` 1171.

166. **The two wants, the six answers, the four clauses, the three rooms, and the fever, and where each stands.** **Neither woman on that foreshore restated her want in Batch 0003 and neither was thanked, and neither want may be restated in Batch 0004.** The woman of about fifty-one wanted to be asked for a day and told no; the woman of sixty-three wanted the road to stay exactly as it is, and in this block she acted twice — she boiled water at the fourth house on a Saturday with four people down with a fever in the seventh, and she put a third armful of wood on her fire on the nineteenth of Mudmonth — and said her want nothing. **The six answers are as Chapters 468 and 469 left them and Batch 0003 improved on none of them.** **The fourth clause set is four clauses and not a rate and nobody has made a keeper of them; the three rooms have not met again; no fixed day is fixed; the port four days off still cannot say *nobody is paid* in a weigh-house.** **A fever came up the road in a wagon of shell and sat four days in the seventh of the eleven and was never reported to anybody, and the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow said out loud that a keeper would have given it an address in about nine days, and that she is not turning about, and that the reason is that a man four days up that road is going to want to put a page on it and she has seen what a page does to a number.** A fever is not a plot device and Batch 0004 may not resolve it, may not name its source, and may not use it to argue for a keeper. `state/continuity.md` 1159, 1170.

167. **The keeper of records, and the figure of about eleven hundred, and the two counts and the three counts.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles off the coast, fourpence a year, out of a deed of thirteen words.** **About nine hundred sheets a month go out of that room into nine counties, about a hundred and four go out in one band on a fixed day, and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month come in, and the three are not added and are not converted into one another and all three stand together on one page once in Chapter 478 and are not summed there.** Her register stands at seven questions and no findings, her day's book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, her drawer is about eleven inches deep and holds about four hundred returns and one of them holds a question of nine words that came off a road two days off a coast and that nobody is going to answer, and **her drawer is not opened in Batch 0003 and no eighth register entry and no tenth not-asking is added.** The press stands open with no date anybody will give and nobody shuts it. **About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list; she read the figure in about nine seconds and put the sheet back into a band at the fourth place from the cord and tied the cord and wrote the day on the outside of it, and said out loud that a figure is not a question and that if she turns that number into a question she has made one she has to answer. Nobody thanked her, and a successor may not thank her and may not thank her twice and may not restate her want and may not put her in a room with a man of thirty-two.** `state/continuity.md` 1168.

168. **The ninth word, and where it is now, and what has been spent.** **The ninth word is the name of a road nine miles long and it is out. In Batch 0003 it was said out loud nine times in a market town of about two thousand people on the fifth of Mudmonth, by nine people reached by age and by trade, between the fourth hour and the seventh hour, and not one of the nine wrote it down and nobody un-said it and nobody regretted it in a speech.** Nobody in that town could say where the word came from and nobody was sent to find out, and no one from the foreshore was in that town. **Saying it is not opening anything: the gate at the end of the nine-mile road is not opened, nobody is sent to it, nothing is scheduled for it, and the two roads were not compared on a page in Batch 0003 and are not to be compared on one in Block 0004.** The word is now in a market town, on a slate that was written on and rubbed out, in the mouth of a boy of about eleven who was sent for a pail, and in the memory of a man of about nineteen at a bar who was not asked. **Do not add a tenth line to the song, do not soften the leak, do not un-say it, and do not have anybody regret it in a speech.** `state/continuity.md` 1170.

169. **The nine who refused in Volume 09, and the words from a yard, and what may and may not be done with them.** **The nine are not contacted, not quoted, not sent for and not put in a room, there is no council of nine on that foreshore, and the word is not printed in Batch 0003.** The nine words from a yard that got no answer are not printed in Batch 0003 and are not referred to. A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow is now the volume's carrier of the wants and the answers and has been thanked for none of them; **she is not one of the nine and may not be turned into one of them, and she may not be given a name, and she may not be thanked.** The woman of about fifty with the board, the woman of about fifty-eight with a slate, the man of about nineteen at a bar, the man of about thirty-three with a barrow, the woman of about twenty-seven with a basket, the man of about fifty with a cart, the carter of about fifty and the man of about twenty-three who rows one of the four boats are all returns and are not new names. `state/continuity.md` 1170, 1162.

170. **The measurements and the three craft counts, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets. Every figure in this item is recounted after the review repair and item 178 is the repair; where the two disagree the recount in item 178 is the authority.** Block 0003 is 17,142 in ten chapters, counted file by file with the title line included, per chapter **1,874 / 1,848 / 1,676 / 1,658 / 1,737 / 1,547 / 1,699 / 1,646 / 1,627 / 1,830**; the four hundred and seventy before it **1,420,010**; the manuscript **1,437,152 in four hundred and eighty chapters**; Volume 10 **49,819 in thirty**. `about` **329 in 17,142, which is 19.19 per 1,000**, whole-word over whitespace, case-sensitive, after twenty-one hedges were cut from figures a person in the room could have counted, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register. `the whole of` 22 and `about four people` **0**. `---` rules 0. Question marks **9**, exclamation marks **3**, semicolons **2**, em dashes **1**, en dashes **0**, and the punctuation rule is corrected and is not a zero mandate. Per-line bold parity even on every line; curly quotation marks balancing in every file and on every line. **Exact duplicate paragraphs of twelve words or more: 0 against the whole manuscript and 0 against the block. Longest shared eight-word run against the four hundred and seventy prior: 8, the dateline frame; longest shared run between two chapters of the block: 8, the same frame.** `Aren` 7 and `Kest` 7. Quoted spans per chapter 13, 7, 9, 6, 6, 9, 13, 10, 15, 6. **Ten money columns, ten foots, ten distinct totals. Twenty-five printed speech-length figures, all footed from the printed speech, none preceded by *about*, and none attached to an unprinted speech. The barred tokens and the four barred numerals at 0 across the ten files.** `state/continuity.md` 1163 to 1166.

171. **And the three things this block learned that are not numbers and that Batch 0004 will meet on its first page.** **One: a check that only compares two places cannot see a novel, and this block passed all of them with two chapters reporting a conversation instead of printing one, and the fix in both cases was to print the speech and to move the figure onto what is printed.** **Two: a speech-length figure is a figure, and a barred numeral is barred in a figure as well as in a word, and a block that writes its speech figures after its prose will put a prohibited number on a page that a sweep for barred tokens does not find.** **Three: a prompt can carry a right day number and two wrong attributes of it, and the two wrong attributes can be consistent with each other, so a writer who checks the weekday checks the month too, and a writer who checks neither has every date in the block a month out and internally perfect.** `state/continuity.md` 1172, 1161, 1157.

172. **And what Volume 10 Batch 0004 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the thirteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech, and do not say the ninth word a second time. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend the arithmetic by which it was identified, and do not compare the nine-mile road with the tide road on a page. Do not answer the question from a road and do not have the keeper of records answer it. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars and do not print a figure for the bar four miles west of Marl. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, or an eighth register entry, and do not add any of the nines to a figure of persons. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure and do not print a rate for the tide road. Do not make the driftwood's notches into tides and do not give a foreshore figure two values. Do not fill the fourth head of the form and do not have a character say that anybody has filled it in somewhere else, and do not let anybody who is not a Crown officer put a name on anything. Do not say whether the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and the second man and the four men with a crowbar are connected. Do not have the nine of Volume 09 meet, be quoted, be sent for, or be put in a room. Do not have the protagonist hold the middle, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and do not have a chapter go on a whole volume of his name being absent from the page. Do not have anybody thank the keeper of records or the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, and do not restate either woman's want on the foreshore. Do not play the accent beat a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times. Do not schedule anything for the first of a month, the tide road, the press, the bell, the seed vault, the oath-stone, or Chapter 900, and do not make a cut. Do not resolve the fever, name its source, or use it to argue for a keeper. And do not edit `outline/volume-09.md`, whose three wrong rows are owed by whoever owns the plan.**

## Threads the Volume 10 Batch 0003 review repair carries to Volume 10 Batch 0004

**This section is written by the phase that repaired Volume 10 Batch 0003 after its review, it continues from item 172, and items 173 to 179 are the successor's whole instruction on the classes of defect the repair touched. Nothing in the section above is renumbered and nothing in it is withdrawn. `outline/volume-10.md` is the plan of record and this section does not amend it. It names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere and contains no chapter of new prose.**

173. **The retired reporting frame and its mirror, and the sweep that could only see one of them, which is the most expensive thing in this thread.** **The frame is *have said since that* and its forms *have worked out since that* and *have been saying since that*, and it is retired outright with a hard stop. Its mirror is the same construction with *then* in the place of *that*, and Batch 0003 wrote it twenty-six times across nine of its ten files while its own prompt forbade converting one form into another, and its own §7 sweep tested only the four-word form with *that* in it, returned zero, and certified a clean block.** The four shapes to write nothing of are *have said since then that*, *said since then that*, *has said since then that* and *have worked out since then*, and **a sweep for this frame must test both spellings, or the number it returns is a number about a spelling.** The retirement is forward-only: the four-word form stands at six hundred and seventy across the manuscript, at 13 in Volume 04, 272 in Volume 05, 132 in Volume 06, 142 in Volume 07, 110 in Volume 08 and 1 at `chapter-0460.md`, and none of them is repaired and none of them is in scope. **Report every scope with its number; a zero from ten files is a block-local zero.** `state/continuity.md` 1173 and 1174.

174. **A certificate written after an edit and not read against the paragraph, twice in one block.** Batch 0003's repair pass fixed the Chapter 473 exchange and one exchange in Chapter 479, wrote a state file saying *two chapters reported a conversation instead of printing one and both were replaced with the printed speech*, and left a full reported paragraph standing in §Three of Chapter 479, where the report carried five clauses and the speech that followed it carried one. **A block that repairs prose certifies the repair by reading the paragraph, and a successor inherits the certificate and not the paragraph, and so the certificate is the place a defect survives longest.** Chapter 479's §Three is now printed, in two turns of fifty and sixty-two. `state/continuity.md` 1175.

175. **Three figures of a person that no instrument in this project owns, and one of them is a duration.** **A duration has to survive from one chapter to another and to have a basis that is stated somewhere: a man of about twenty-nine came up a made road four days and arrived at the eleventh hour of the second of Thawmonth, and Chapter 472 on the seventh of Thawmonth now says five days in that town, and four days of travel and four days in a town are not the same four days. Chapter 477 on the fifth of Mudmonth now says he is on his ninth day back in it after nineteen days out of it, and those two figures foot.** **A figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person is a defect whether or not two people were meant, and Chapter 477's man of about thirty-three with a barrow now has a boy sitting up in him and is not a woman of about thirty-four.** **And a total implied rather than stated is a defect even when the arithmetic is right: Chapter 478's third turn is the rest of the hundred and thirty-two, and it is written that way because sixty-one is a barred numeral.** `state/continuity.md` 1176, 1177 and 1178.

176. **A speech figure that is one turn's total printed in another turn's slot, and the block that shipped one and certified it clean.** **Chapter 473 certified sixty-three words in two turns of six and fifty-four in two spans of nine and forty-five, and six plus fifty-four is sixty, and the second turn is nine and fifty-four, which is sixty-three, and the exchange is sixty-nine. It now reads sixty-nine, six and sixty-three, nine and fifty-four.** Twenty-four figures became twenty-five when Chapter 479's §Three was printed and footed, **and all twenty-five now foot against the printed speech, turn by turn, with the attribution excluded and a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span.** `state/continuity.md` 1179 and item 1161.

177. **The day number is right and the two attributes can both be wrong, and the chapter can be right and the prompt wrong.** **`workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §2 gave day 1284 as the nineteenth of Greenmonth and as a Monday and not a market day, and 1284 is the nineteenth of Mudmonth and a Thursday and a market day; the two wrong attributes agreed with each other, because a Monday is not a market day, so a writer who believed the weekday believed the month and a writer who believed the month would have footed every date in the block and been wrong everywhere at once. `chapter-0480.md` printed it correctly. The prompt has been corrected in place, in bold, with the derivation.** **A successor may inherit a prompt figure and it may be wrong, and the prompt's own rule governs: the figure is the error and not the count, and the day is derived twice from day 930 and the two halves are reported separately.** `state/continuity.md` 1180.

178. **The headline word count in both prompts did not reproduce, and the count moves every time a sentence is repaired.** **Batch 0003's §0 printed 1,419,702 and the count is 1,420,010; Batch 0004's §0 printed 1,437,036 and the count as Batch 0003 left it was 1,437,142 and after this repair is 1,437,152. Both are now the count, and each line names the figure it replaced.** The block is **17,142** in ten chapters, per chapter **1,874 / 1,848 / 1,676 / 1,658 / 1,737 / 1,547 / 1,699 / 1,646 / 1,627 / 1,830**, and `about` is **329 in 17,142, which is 19.19 per 1,000**, and the figure this file's own predecessor carried, 334 in 17,132, did not reproduce either; a recount of the files as the block left them returns 332. **A figure that is wrong in a state file is not a rounding error in the book, it is a certificate that has gone stale, and the recount is the authority.** `state/continuity.md` 1180 and item 1163.

179. **And what Volume 10 Batch 0004 must not do in the light of this repair, in one short list, because item 172 is the long one and this is the short one that came out of reading a block after it shipped.** **Do not write the retired frame in either of its spellings, and do not write a near-miss of it. Do not report a conversation instead of printing one, and do not certify a repair without reading the paragraph it is in. Do not let a figure of a person stand next to another figure of a person that a reader cannot tell from it. Do not let a duration of a man's stay in a town be different in two chapters without a departure on the page. Do not print a speech figure that is a turn's total in an exchange's slot. Do not print a total that a reader would have to subtract, unless the total is a barred numeral, and then say that it is a barred numeral. Do not trust a prompt's day number, a prompt's week number or a prompt's headline count, because all three of this block's did not reproduce and one of them is now corrected in place. Do not report a block-local zero as a manuscript zero.** Everything in item 172 stands and none of it is replaced by this, and the plot of this block is not touched by any of it.

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0004 carries to Volume 10 Batch 0005

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 10 Batch 0004 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0005's whole instruction. `outline/volume-10.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` items 1183 to 1206 are the facts, and item 1206 is the list of what Batch 0005 must not do. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's last edit and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Twenty-four threads, numbered from 180, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

180. **The frame Batch 0005 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, and the chain was re-run independently of the prompt and of every printed list and reproduces every anchor the plan prints. **Block 0005 is days 1333 to 1380, forty-eight inclusive, that is the eighth of Longlight YR 319 to the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth YR 319, forty-seven elapsed, and it is the last block of the volume, and the ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived from the day number and not read off a card or off this paragraph.** **Every date in Block 0005 is YR 319, and the year does not turn inside it and did not turn after day 1236.** The month starts inside it are 1356 and nothing after, and 1356 is the first of Rainmonth. **The last day of the volume is day 1380, the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth YR 319, and that is a Tuesday and not a market day, and 1380 is not negotiable and a chapter that falls on it must carry that date.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays, and the volume's last block carries fourteen of them, and **they are not printed here, because a printed day list is a certificate and a certificate is the last place a defect survives.** Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third. `state/continuity.md` 1183.

181. **The two dateline frames, and which of them the last block can afford.** **Chapters 484 and 487 of Batch 0004 are in the coast frame and carry an ebb and an hour and no month, no day of month and no weekday; the other eight are in the main-world frame and six of those eight are set on the foreshore.** A coast-frame chapter's main-world day number is written in the state file and not in the dateline, and Batch 0004's two are **1300 and 1318**. **No month name and no weekday is spoken in a character's mouth anywhere in a chapter set on that foreshore**, and a sweep of all ten of Batch 0004's quoted spans for the twelve month names and the seven weekday names returns one hit in a first printing and zero now. A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and Batch 0004 prints all three in Chapter 487 and adds none of them, and no chapter of the last block may add any two of the three either. `state/continuity.md` 1184.

182. **The wall, exactly, and the four hundred yards, and what is not illegal about it.** **Eleven paces long and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end, laid in four days out of a barge, with a second footing course nine inches lower, and about four inches of sand banked against its far side, and it has taken the water over it twice a day every day since the four days it took to lay.** **In Chapter 485 a surveyor of about forty-four ran a chain from the landward face of the wall to the door of the eleventh of eleven houses and it came to four hundred yards, and four hundred yards is the only distance between the wall and any house that anybody has ever measured.** The prompt for Batch 0004 printed *about four hundred yards* and the pages print four hundred yards without the hedge, because the surveyor chains it. **The wall is not a boundary, not a toll, not a gate, not a rate and not a keeper, it has not stopped a cart in two months, and no man in nine counties has named a way it is against anything, and the surveyor could have named four ways of it in about four minutes the day he ran it and named none of them.** **The wall stays. The volume does not say it is void and does not say it is upheld and nobody wins the argument about it on a page.** `state/continuity.md` 1185, 1203.

183. **The two documents, the fourth head, and the only person in nine counties who has cut the other three.** **A deed is a foot and a half long, folded in three, with one ruled signature at the foot and nothing else. A form is the same size with four heads cut into it, of which three are filled in — a place, a figure of men, and a trade — and the fourth is a line for the name of whoever is going to answer for the road between the two ends, and it has been empty since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth.** **In `chapter-0482.md` a copyist of about thirty-one says out loud that the fourth head ought not to be on the form, and is right about it, and a clerk of about forty-seven of twenty-two years' standing says that he has cut three of the four heads on about nine hundred forms and has never cut that one and is not going to start now, and they agree in nothing.** **Nobody has filled the fourth head, nobody has said that anybody has filled it in anywhere else, and nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall in the about four months since it was made on a sand in about a hundred and ninety words in three turns.** A character may still say the fourth head ought not to exist and be right and not be agreed with. Nobody who is not a Crown officer puts a name on anything. `state/continuity.md` 1185, 1194.

184. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet, the sixth time, and the answer that is neither a conspiracy and not nothing.** **He has been on that sand six times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and the sixth was the seventeenth of Greenmonth YR 319.** On the fifth there was a second man, about twenty-eight, carrying nothing, who did not stand at the stone, looked at the eleven houses one at a time, and was followed with the eyes by four people; **there was no second man on the sixth and nobody looked for him.** **The question nobody asked him was asked in seven words by a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow and it was why he comes when he comes, and his answer is that he is sent, that a man in a room with a seal decides how often the place is looked at, that he has never been told who decided it and is never going to ask, and that the only thing about his six visits that is his own is that he has not found out whether the fourth head wants a person or wants a word.** He writes four things on a sheet with no head on it and dates it at the top. **Nothing in Volume 10 says whose plan the wall is and nothing in the last block may say it, and nothing names him, and nothing says whether he and the four men with a crowbar are connected, and he is a return and not a name.** `state/continuity.md` 1186.

185. **The chain, and the boy of about seventeen with a rod, and what a chain is for.** **A chain answers one question, which is how far it is from the ring at this end to the ring at the other end along whatever the two men holding it walk over, and a chain run straight across one of the nine places the sand turns comes up with a longer answer than the ground under it.** **The boy of about seventeen with a rod asks what a chain is for three times across Chapters 481, 484 and 485 and is told three times by three people, and the last telling is that a chain is a stick you lay on the ground to see how big the ground is, and the ground is there either way.** A chain and a rod are not rented for money on that shore and neither is in a money column. **The boy keeps his own column in Chapter 484, the first he has ever kept, on the back of a rod-measuring sheet with a pencil stub out of a shed, and he is not one of the seven who refused a permanent connection and he is not one of the nine and he is not a council and Batch 0005 may not give him a name unless it spends the last slot.** `state/continuity.md` 1187.

186. **The sand, the eleven houses, and the driftwood, and the arithmetic nobody may simplify.** **Two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand and broken shell, passable for about four hours out of every twelve and about twice a day, and for about eight hours out of every twelve the seaward half is under about two hundred paces of water.** Eleven houses, about nine hundred people, **four of the eleven with a person in them who answers for it and seven with nobody in them, and the seven are the ones that are used, and four and seven are never added to eleven in a column.** The four are the pan and the fire at the fourth house, the lease at the ninth, the widow with the last of a boat at the eleventh, and a man standing in the doorway of the seventh whom four of the wagon men will not stop at. **A piece of driftwood about the length of a forearm and two inches through, wedged in a driven post five inches square, with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, the twenty-second not cut, and it goes in when the water goes off a mark that has not been off in twenty-two years, and the water did not go off that mark on the first of a month in this year and did not go off it on the seventeenth day of Greenmonth either.** About nine cart-loads of shell a day off four boats into four pans. The sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps and the ninth is three black stones a storm put there in a month nobody now living was born in. `state/continuity.md` 1190.

187. **The ninth house, the nine weeks, and the three figures that belong to them, none of which the last block may re-derive or round.** **The ninth house did not buy for nine weeks and started again on the twenty-eighth of Mudmonth YR 319, buying three sacks at nine pence a sack, the price it has paid for eleven years.** **A wagon year is about two hundred days and twenty-seven pence a day the wrong way, which is five thousand four hundred pence a year; a sixth of that is nine hundred pence; and nine hundred pence is forty-five days of the winter of a woman at the eleventh house at twenty pence a day.** A man with nine hundred acres inland said out loud that he did not stop buying because of the man with the sheet but because a wall appeared, and the man of about fifty-three said the figure out loud to nobody and counted three sacks and stopped before the third, and **the two of them have an arrangement about nine weeks old that is not on a docket with a nail on it.** **Three sacks at nine pence a sack is not converted into money anywhere in Batch 0004, because that conversion was made once in Chapter 473, and the two prices of shell are never added in any register including a notebook.** `state/continuity.md` 1188, 1195.

188. **The two seasonal costs, and the rule that they are never added, and the one new figure in them.** **A season on that sand is the days the water is off long enough and a wagon year is about two hundred days.** A widow of about seventy at the eleventh house lives at **twenty pence a day**, and about two hundred days at twenty pence a day is **four thousand pence**, and that is the cost of a season to that household in money and it is a new figure and it is not five thousand four hundred pence, which is nine months of her winter and the cost of a permanent connection. **The cost of a season to the household at the ninth house is two carts up the made road, four days of a boy's legs each way, and one question asked out loud in front of nine people, and the man who paid it said himself that it is not pence, that he cannot give it to anybody in pence, and that if anybody puts it in a column he will take the column out.** The two sit on the same stretch of sand in Chapter 488 and are not added, and **no rate in nine counties converts a boy's two legs into pence on that foreshore, and the corridor's rate is a rate nobody can check and this is not it, and no rate is printed for the tide road anywhere in this volume.** `state/continuity.md` 1189, 1203.

189. **The two instruments, and that they do not touch, and that this is the whole of the volume's finding.** **Four of the eleven houses are somebody's job and eleven paces of stone is at a hundred paces from the landward end, and in Chapter 487 both are on the same sand at the same hour with about nine hundred people crossing both, and the only conversation between them is about a barrow.** **Four of eleven, seven of eleven, eleven paces of stone and about nine hundred people are four figures and are never added to one another in any order, in any column, or in any sentence that is trying to find out what moves.** Nobody made a list, there was no meeting, and there is no room on that sand. **And the wall is not illegal in any way anybody has named, and the four houses are not a keeper, and the two are four hundred yards apart and no paper joins them, and that distance is not a gap in anything.** `state/continuity.md` 1190, 1185.

190. **The two wants, the sixth answer, the four clauses, the three rooms, and the fever, and where each stands after Batch 0004.** **Neither woman on that foreshore restated her want in Batch 0004 and neither was thanked, and neither want may be restated in Batch 0005.** The woman of about fifty-one is referred to and is not on a page; the woman of sixty-three acted twice more, in Chapters 486 and 487, and said her want nothing. **The six answers are exactly as Chapters 468 and 469 left them and Batch 0004 improved on none of them**, and the sixth, that eleven houses are somebody's job, is the instrument the block put beside a wall and is on the page in Chapters 485, 487 and 490. **The four clauses about a wagon are four clauses and not a rate, the three rooms have not met again, no fixed day is fixed, and the port four days off still cannot say *nobody is paid* in a weigh-house.** **A fever is not resolved, its source is not named, it is not used to argue for a keeper, and no character catches anything; four people sat in the seventh of the eleven in Batch 0003 and four of them are still not named and Batch 0005 may not name them.** `state/continuity.md` 1191.

191. **The keeper of records, and the three counts, and what did not move.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles off the coast, fourpence a year, out of a deed of thirteen words, is not on a page of Batch 0004 and no chapter of it is set in her room.** Her register stands at seven questions and no findings, her day's book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, and her drawer about eleven inches deep is not opened. **About nine hundred sheets a month, about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day, and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three series and are not added and are not converted into one another.** About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. Nobody thanked her and Batch 0004 did not thank her twice and did not restate her want and did not put her in a room with a man of thirty-two. **The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent and the word *failed* is used of nothing in this volume. The hearing stands. The quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The press was not dated and nobody shut it. The bell was not rung. No oath-stone was renewed and none is scheduled. No cut was made and none is scheduled. Nothing was opened in Batch 0004 and nothing is scheduled to be.** `state/continuity.md` 1192, 1194.

192. **The protagonist, in the state the close inherits him in.** **Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent; the qualification is not earned and not claimed.** A permanent limp in the right leg; a ferrier's stick he cannot lean on; he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight and is not going to, and getting up is about four seconds longer than sitting down. A road-marked left palm, warm at about the fourth hour, still untold. A notebook with eleven lines and a word on the eleventh and no twelfth line. A sheet of nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket, not shown and not asked for. Two unreconciled numbers in his coat. **`Aren` 11 and `Kest` 11 across Batch 0004's ten files, in seven chapters, and he is at zero in two of them by design.** He conducted nothing, got nothing back, overrules nobody and is not the reason anything works. **He conducted no crossing in this block and the volume has now spent three crossings of about three, all of them in Block 0001, and the outline's one crossing for Block 4 was not used, so Batch 0005 has none either and a successor that wants a crossing is overrunning a season that is already spent.** The accent is not played in Batch 0004 and no kitchen is placed or displaced, and the second loss of this volume is still a person losing an illusion about his own voice and it has not yet been spent. `state/continuity.md` 1193.

193. **The two men, and the two bars, and the three nines, and the ninth word, and what the close may not do with any of them.** The breach between the two men is open and is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure in this block, the word for what the two of them are is printed nowhere in it, and **the barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and ninety chapters and is spent and Batch 0004 prints no third of it.** The span built over nineteen mornings is not reported as a repair. **The three bars are three objects and Batch 0004 touches none of them and prints no figure for the bar four miles west of Marl.** The count on the page under the fourth step is at nine, the day-book is at nine, the register is at seven questions and no findings, and the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine, and none of the four is added to another or to a figure of persons. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent. **The ninth word is out in a market town and was said nine times in Chapter 477 and was not said a second time anywhere in Batch 0004, and the gate at the end of the nine-mile road is not opened and nobody is sent to it and the two roads are not compared on a page.** Hushwater was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. `state/continuity.md` 1194.

194. **The money, and the seventy printed columns, and the weaker check, and the mark.** **Ten footed columns in Batch 0004 at 91, 118, 76, 134, 85, 109, 143, 94, 127 and 100 pence, ten distinct totals, ten distinct owners, each column added in pence with the sum list printed before the total.** Add them to the seventy already printed: **Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and Volume 10 Batch 0001 at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, Batch 0002 at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62, and Batch 0003 at 98, 106, 54, 87, 105, 72, 103, 122, 82 and 96, which is seventy printed figures and must not be seventy distinct ones.** **The prompt for this block said seventy-four and printed seventy, and the forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository; Batch 0004 checked against the seventy and did not re-read the forty, and Batch 0005 has the item in front of it and should make the stronger check and say which it made.** A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page. **The four barred numerals are barred as figures as well as as words, and a speech-length certification is a figure, and Batch 0004 printed three of them into certifications before the sweep found them and recast two of them as their printed span sums.** `state/continuity.md` 1195, 1196.

195. **The speech-length figures and the rule, and the seven that were wrong the first time.** **Batch 0004 prints twenty certifications and all twenty foot, none preceded by *about*, on the declared basis of a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends, with a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span, and a speech printed as two quoted spans with an attribution between them counted once and the figure attached to it being a figure about both printed lines.** The full list is at `state/continuity.md` 1196 and a successor may take it from there. **Seven of the twenty were wrong in a first printing and every one of them was wrong by drift after the `about` sweep cut a hedge out of a printed speech, and one of the seven was fixed by changing the prose instead of the figure because the figure was right and the cut hedge was in the wrong place.** A figure attached to a speech the page does not print is the defect this project has paid for most, and **Batch 0004 adds none to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and none to the list of protected unprinted figures, and no chapter of Batch 0004 reports a conversation instead of printing one.** `state/continuity.md` 1196.

196. **The name budget with the arithmetic, and what is left, and what the close must not spend it on.** **The budget for Volume 10 is five. Batch 0001 spent three, `Marda Oakes` 51, `Rusen Ball` 29 and `Halla Wray` 63. Batch 0002 spent one, `Tobin Kernick` 53. Batch 0003 spent none. Batch 0004 spent none. The arithmetic is 3 + 1 + 0 + 0 = 4 of 5, so the last slot is untouched and Batch 0005 has it whole and may spend one name or none.** **A capitalised two-word sweep over Batch 0004's ten files returns `Aren Kest` and nothing else**, and every other hit is a sentence opening across a bold marker or a section heading and each was read. `Cray` was last swept in Batch 0003 and returns `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0254.md`; **sweep it again rather than take it from a state file, because a name is the only asset in this manuscript that cannot be audited after the fact.** **The last slot is not for a surveyor, a clerk, a copyist, a boy with a rod, a man with a crowbar, a man with a shovel, a man with a board, or a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, and it is not for either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine.** `state/continuity.md` 1197.

197. **The three wants, and the four women who may not be thanked, and the room that may not be built.** The woman of about fifty-one wants to be asked for a day and told no, and is `Marda Oakes`. The woman of sixty-three wants the road to stay exactly as it is, and is `Halla Wray`. The woman of about thirty-four with a barrow is the volume's carrier of the wants and the answers and has been thanked for none of them, **and she is not one of the nine who refused in Volume 09, may not be turned into one, may not be given a name, may not be thanked, and may not be put in a room.** A woman of about thirty-one is a copyist in a room over a chandler's shop in Chapter 482 and said one true sentence and was not thanked. **A woman of about twenty-nine in a room over a weigh-house is not one of the two women of about twenty-nine and about thirty-nine and a successor must not turn her into one. The woman of about fifty-eight with a slate wrote the word *yard* on a slate in Batch 0003 and rubbed out the rest, and she has no name and no want in this volume and gets none in the close.** `state/continuity.md` 1191, 1192.

198. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this thread for any of them.** **Batch 0004 is 20,366 in ten chapters, as the files stand after the review repair, and it was 20,357 as the block left it**, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included and the ten added, and the per-chapter figures are **1,965 / 2,326 / 1,897 / 2,160 / 2,075 / 1,922 / 2,224 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880**; the four hundred and eighty chapters before it **1,437,152**; **the manuscript 1,457,518 in four hundred and ninety chapters**; Volume 10 **70,185 in forty**. `about` **418 in 20,366, which is 20.52 per 1,000**, whole-word over whitespace, case-sensitive, after seventy hedges were cut from figures a person in the room could have counted, and the survivors are the age house form and the honest-uncertainty register. `the whole of` 15 and `about four people` **0**. `---` rules 0. Question marks **4**, exclamation marks **4**, semicolons **6**, em dashes **1**, en dashes **0**, and the punctuation rule is corrected and is not a zero mandate. Per-line bold parity even on every line; curly quotation marks balancing in every file and on every line. **Exact duplicate paragraphs of twelve words or more: 0 for the ten new files against the whole manuscript and 0 against the block, and the manuscript figure this thread once gave as 0 is 33 groups across Volumes 01 to 07 and item 204 is the whole of it. Longest shared eight-word run against the four hundred and eighty prior: 46, the driftwood sentence in `chapter-0487.md` that stands whole in `chapter-0480.md`, and it was reported here as 58 and named the dateline frame, and the true figure before the repair was 104 and it was a money column in `chapter-0485.md`; longest shared run among the ten new files: 33, the money-column frame in `chapter-0489.md`, and it was reported here as 29 and the true figure before the repair was 65.** `Aren` 11 and `Kest` 11. Quoted spans per chapter 6, 12, 13, 9, 5, 10, 6, 7, 5 and 5. **Ten money columns, ten foots, ten distinct totals. Twenty printed speech-length figures, all footed from the printed speech, none preceded by *about*, and none attached to an unprinted speech. The barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the barred kin-term at 0 across the ten files, and the retired reporting frame at 0 across nine forms and not the six that were swept, and the block as first printed carried four instances of the frame in the three forms that were not.** `state/continuity.md` 1198, 1199, 1200, 1201.

199. **And the three things this block learned that are not numbers and that Batch 0005 will meet on its first page.** **One: a hedge is a correct edit and a speech figure is a second measurement of the prose, and the second measurement is the one that goes stale, so cutting seventy of them moved seven printed figures and nobody looked at the figures.** **Two: a barred numeral is barred in a figure and not only in a word, and this block printed three of them into certifications, one of which was a turn total that could only be recast as the sum of its own printed spans, and that is the fourth block running in which a certification has printed a number the manuscript does not print.** **Three: a prompt can carry two attributes of a day that cannot both be true of any day in the block's range, and it did — a Thursday and the nineteenth of a month, and there is no such day between 1285 and 1332 — and it can carry a count of how many columns are spent that is four larger than the list it prints, and both of those figures were wrong and the derivation and the printed list govern.** `state/continuity.md` 1204, 1183.

200. **And what Batch 0005 inherits as a fact about the world, in one paragraph, and it is not a summary of the block.** A wall of eleven paces at a hundred paces from the landward end took the tide over it twice a day every day since the four days it took to lay, and it is four hundred yards from the eleventh of eleven houses and no paper joins them. A chain is a stick you lay on the ground to see how big the ground is. A form with three heads filled in and a fourth head empty has been on a table in a town four days off a coast for four months beside a deed that has been signed by nobody, and the only person in nine counties who has cut the other three says the fourth ought not to be there and is right and is not agreed with. A man of about thirty-four with a sheet has been on that sand six times and does not know who sends him and is not going to ask. The ninth house bought three sacks at nine pence a sack for the first time in nine weeks and nobody said a figure out loud. A widow of about seventy lives at twenty pence a day and a season is about two hundred days and that is four thousand pence, and the house next door along paid two carts and a boy's legs for the same about two hundred days and will not put it in a column. Four of the eleven houses are standing in and nine hundred people went across both them and the wall on one tide and stopped on neither. On the first of Longlight there was nothing on two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand to swear, and the twenty-second notch in a piece of driftwood is not cut, and a boundary oath-stone four miles off a made road is missing and nobody was sent to look for it. **A man of about thirty-four with a limp is named on seven of the last ten pages, and he is the reason nothing on that sand works, and he has not been thanked, and about four hundred yards of sand in between a wall and a house is not a gap in anything.**

201. **And what Batch 0005 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the twenty-one above.** **Do not name the antagonist and do not say whose plan the wall is.** Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, have anybody regret it in a speech, or say the ninth word a second time. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or compare that road with the tide road on a page. Do not resolve the shut road four hundred miles off, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and do not answer the offer with a figure. Do not repair the breach between the two men and do not report the span as a repair. Do not reopen the city, the valley, the press's opening day, the roadmark, the mechanism, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years. Do not merge the three bars and do not print a figure for the bar four miles west of Marl. Do not take Hushwater and do not print its two figures. Do not add a tenth asking of the bench, a tenth not-asking, or an eighth register entry. Do not print the corridor's rate as a figure and do not print a rate for the tide road. Do not resolve the fever, name its source, use it to argue for a keeper, or have a character catch anything. Do not renew an oath-stone and do not schedule a second swearing. Do not fill the fourth head, in speech or on paper, and do not have anybody accept the offer of one sentence about the wall. Do not name the surveyor, the boy with the rod, the copyist, the clerk of the works, the man with the crowbar, the man with the shovel, the man with a sledge, the man with a barrow, the man with a board, or the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow. Do not make any of the four men with a crowbar, a shovel, a sledge and a barrow a villain, a knave or a liar, and do not have one of them discover that he has been wrong about something and go away. Do not vary four hundred yards, or four thousand pence, or any of the ten figures Batch 0003 added to the trade. Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and do not have him laid a chain he is a party to. Do not conduct a crossing. Do not write the retired reporting frame in either of its spellings. Do not report a conversation instead of printing one. Do not cut a hedge out of a printed speech and leave the figure standing. Do not trust a prompt's day number, its count of columns, or its headline word count. **And do not end the volume on a summary, on a wall winning, on a wall coming down, or on a man standing on a road.**

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0004 review repair carries to Volume 10 Batch 0005

**This section is written after a reviewer read Batch 0004's ten chapters and its five state files, and it continues from 201. Items 180 to 201 are not renumbered and not withdrawn, and item 198 carries the block's measurements as the block left them and its corrected figures are here. `state/continuity.md` items 1207 to 1212 are the same pass at length. Together with items 180 to 201 these six threads are Volume 10 Batch 0005's whole instruction, and item 201 is still the list of what it may not do.**

202. **The three false claims the block's certificate carried, and the three true ones, and a rule that is worth more to Batch 0005 than any of the six.** **A certificate can overclaim in three ways and this block did all three: it reported a number about ten files as a number about the manuscript, it reported a number from one basis as a number from another, and it named a frame as the longest run when the longest run was a thing the frame was declared not to cover.** The corrected figures are at `state/continuity.md` 1209 and the true ones are: the block is **20,366** and the manuscript is **1,457,518 in four hundred and ninety chapters**; the longest shared run against the four hundred and eighty prior is **forty-six words on the declared eight-word basis and fifty-five on the looser six-word basis**; the longest run between two chapters of the block is **thirty-three and fifty-two**; exact duplicate paragraphs are **0 for the block and 33 in the manuscript**; `about` is **418 at 20.52 per 1,000**. **And the rule: a number a writer computes about his own block is the number most likely to be wrong, because it is the only number in the file nobody else has computed. Report the basis, report the scope, and when a number is zero, open the file the zero is about and read a paragraph of it before the number is written down.**

203. **A declared frame is not a licence to copy, and the money column is the frame that will be copied.** Batch 0004's own prompt handed Batch 0005 a licence: §7 item 4 told it to declare the money-column frame rather than re-voice it, and `chapter-0485.md` was holding a hundred and twenty-one words of `chapter-0475.md`'s column word for word when that prompt was written. **Four of the block's ten columns were re-voiced in the repair and a re-voice may not move a price, a term, a sum-list entry or a total.** **Batch 0005 writes ten columns and re-voices them, and a column is a person writing down what they spent in their own order, and the check is that the longest run any column makes against any earlier column is under about twenty words.** The dateline frame and the house footing sentence may be reused, because they are the sound of the book, and the driftwood sentence may be restated, because it is a fact with one value; none of the three is a list somebody copied.

204. **The inherited duplicate paragraphs, thirty-three of them, and this is a whole-volume job and not a repair pass.** **Across the manuscript, thirty-three paragraphs of twelve words or more stand whole in two or more different chapter files, in sixty-seven chapter instances, at 12 in Volume 01, 7 in Volume 02, 18 in Volume 03, 20 in Volume 04, 4 in Volume 05, 4 in Volume 06 and 2 in Volume 07; three further files repeat a paragraph inside themselves, at `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0037.md`, `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0052.md` and `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0113.md`.** The largest cluster is six paragraphs of a letter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0148.md` standing whole in `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0153.md`; the next is a writ in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0025.md` standing whole in `chapter-0026.md`. **Batch 0005 does not repair any of them. They are inherited, they are four hundred and ninety chapters back, and a writer who spends a block's whole budget on Volume 03 has not written the close of Volume 10.** **And the same bare zero is printed in about twenty-four places in the four ledgers, in earlier blocks' entries, two of which are honest about it and print the sweep's own manuscript total beside it; a reader of any of them should take the manuscript figure as 33 and not as the zero that is printed there.** Whoever takes this on takes it as its own pass, with its own readings, and it is not to be repaired in a repair pass on any other block.

205. **The retired frame, in nine forms and not six, and the sentence that is the trap.** The six the block's prompt named are the four-word form with *that* and the mirror with *then*; the three it did not name are the two comma forms and the bare *said since that*. **A comma in front of the adverbial does not make it a different frame**, which is why a reviewer reading the pages found four of them in a block that had certified the frame at zero in both spellings, and the fix was to re-voice four clauses in Chapters 484, 486 and 487 and not to argue about the reading. **Batch 0005's sweep runs the nine forms and reports nine numbers, and a sweep that tests six and reports "both spellings" has tested neither of the forms that were in the pages.**

206. **What the repair did and did not touch, so that Batch 0005 does not find a changed block and think it inherited a different one.** It touched six chapter files and five state files and one successor prompt. **Four money columns re-voiced at identical totals, two dateline clauses re-voiced at identical figures, four clauses re-voiced off the retired frame, and no quoted speech anywhere, so all twenty printed speech-length certifications still foot against the speech they are attached to and none was recounted.** No date moved, no total moved, no figure of a person moved, no name was spent, the fourth head is still empty, the offer is still untaken, the fever is still unresolved, the wall still stays, and the volume's three crossings are still three spent.

207. **`state/phase-ledger.json` does not describe this project, and it is not a writer's file.** It reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap`, `status: planned`, `attempts: 0` and `actualModel: null` at Chapter 490 with Volume 10 Block 0005 pending, and only two commits in the repository's history have ever touched it. **No writer, no batch and no review repair may edit it, and this thread records the discrepancy so that the controller can act on it and so that no later writer reads a stale phase out of it.** The same goes for `scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json` and `outline/volume-09.md`.

## Threads Volume 10 Batch 0005 carries to Volume 11, and Volume 10 is closed at Chapter 500

**Items 208 to 218, continuing from 207. Volume 10 ran to Chapter 500, day 1380, the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth YR 319, and this section is the whole instruction a Volume 11 writer inherits from the last block of the last completed volume. Nothing in it is withdrawn and nothing in it is a suggestion.**

208. **The First Witness is not named, and the mechanism is not named, and the Gatehouse's oldest protocol is not printed, and the word about a roof and the word about the tile on a coping are both at zero across five hundred chapters.** `outline/series.md`'s Volume 11 row makes the answer a public inquiry into that protocol and **this volume may not print the word, may not print the name of the mechanism, and may not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping, and the last of those three is Chapter 900's.** A sweep of the ten files of the last block returns zero for all of them.

209. **The word that names the founding paper is still not printed, in five hundred chapters, in this volume's outline, in any prompt and in any state file, and the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule is still unanswered in prose.** **The coast's own sheet carries a word in a fourth head and that word is the coast's name for the road and is not a keeper and is not a person, and the Crown's form has a fourth head that is empty and stays empty.** A Volume 11 writer may not print the name, and may not have anybody say it aloud, and may not have it arrive on a form.

210. **The wall is legal in form and has no signature, and the volume did not say it is void and did not say it is upheld, and that stays as it is.** It is eleven paces of stone with a second footing course nine inches below the top of it, at a hundred paces from the landward end of two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand, and it is four hundred yards from the door of the eleventh of eleven houses, and **no man in nine counties has named a way it is against anything, and a surveyor of about forty-four could have named four ways of it in about four minutes and named none of them.** Nobody wins the argument about it on a page.

211. **The Morrow Coast Commons exists and is not a council of nine and is not modelled on one, and it has no keeper, no crown, no office, no register of persons and no fixed day.** **Its only rule is about the tide, is thirty-four words long, and is not on the sheet; the sheet is a sheet on a stone at the landward end and will be a sheet on a stone in about four months, and if the water takes it the thing does not stop.** Eleven houses are in it, four of eleven and seven of eleven are never added to eleven in a column, and about eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list and nothing has put them on anything.

212. **Five paths are open and none of them has a keeper, and the five holders have never met.** The man of about thirty-four from the port four days off, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow, the mason of about sixty-eight at a single arch over a beck nine miles off a yard of four lime kilns, the woman of about fifty at a roadhouse nine miles on from the kilns, and the woman of about fifty-one at a post with twenty-two notches cut in a piece of driftwood. **About four of the five have heard of none of the other four, the answer to the arch is still nine miles off from the room where it would be wanted, and the twenty-second notch is not cut and the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years.**

213. **Aren Kest keeps two unreconciled counts of his own life and the difference is three, and he has stopped trying to say his days out loud.** Thirty-one nights against five roads and thirty-four on a chalk board over a yard stair in a fen town, and **the habits of a full ordinary year in Merefen no longer fit his days, and he has a stable set of skills and an unstable sense of daily chronology, and the chapter that says so is Chapter 500 and it is the last chapter of the volume.** A Volume 11 writer may not resolve the difference of three and may not say which of his days belong to Merefen.

214. **The childhood room has now been placed twice, in Chapter 459 and again in Chapter 500, and he was not sure of it either time.** The three objects are a chipped bowl, a hook with a piece of horse-brass on it and a stool, and the window in that kitchen does not shut and never did. A Volume 11 writer may not place it a third time and may not say that he is sure of it.

215. **The accent is spent as a device and it was used once in this volume's last block, deliberately, in a salt-store.** He said his no in the accent of that shore and knew he was saying it in that accent, and about four of nine people in that room heard the voice and about five did not. **It has not been played in any other chapter of the volume and a Volume 11 writer may not play it as a reveal.**

216. **The inherited arithmetic defect in `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0488.md` line 53, recorded and not repaired, and it is a Volume 11 reader's item rather than a Volume 11 writer's.** The printed sum list has thirty entries against an item list of twenty-nine prices, the extra entry being a second *four*, the items foot at ninety, and the printed total says ninety-four. **Ninety-four is a canon figure in this volume's own series of spent columns, so the page stands as it was delivered and the defect is a whole-volume job like item 204 and not a repair pass.**

217. **The thirty-three duplicate paragraphs across the manuscript are inherited and are a whole-volume job.** None of the thirty-three involves a chapter of Volume 10's last block, and three further files repeat a paragraph inside themselves.

218. **What Volume 11 may not do, in one list, and it is the list `outline/volume-10.md` and this volume's own threads add up to.** Do not print the word that names the founding paper. Do not fill the form's fourth head and do not have anybody say anybody has filled it in. Do not name the First Witness or the mechanism. Do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off and do not schedule it and send nobody. Do not take up the offer of one sentence about the wall. Do not put the about eleven hundred people on anything. Do not put the two villages back on a road. Do not print a rate for the corridor's rule or for the tide road. Do not strike the hearing or the quarantine instrument. Do not take the city off the shelf and do not print its three protected numerals. Do not reach the valley. Do not add a figure to the register's seven questions, the day's book's nine, or the nine under the fourth step, and do not add the bench's nine to any of them. Do not add about nine hundred people on the sand to about nine hundred people paid twice in seven days. Do not add about nine hundred sheets a month to about a hundred and four in a band to about four hundred and nine parish rolls. Do not convert thirty-one into thirty-four.
