Do not add four thousand pence to five thousand four hundred. Do not say the wall is void or say it is good. Do not turn the driftwood's notches into tides. Do not convene the nine who refused. Do not restate the want of the woman of about fifty-one or the want of the woman of twenty-nine in the room two hundred miles off, and do not thank either of them. Do not resolve the fever. Do not take the Hushwater thread and do not print either of its two figures. Do not renew the missing oath-stone. Do not conduct a crossing, and the volume's season's three were spent whole in Block 0001. Do not spend the last of the five names on a surveyor, a clerk, a copyist, a boy with a rod, a man with a crowbar, a shovel, a sledge or a barrow, a man with a board, or a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow. Do not name the antagonist. Do not edit any chapter of Volumes 01 to 09. Do not write `state/phase-ledger.json`.

## Threads Volume 11 Batch 0001 carries to Volume 11 Batch 0002

**Items 219 to 232, continuing from 218. Volume 11 opened on day 1381, the twenty-sixth of Rainmonth YR 319, and this block ran to day 1434, the nineteenth of Fallowmonth YR 319, and this section is the whole instruction Batch 0002 inherits. Nothing in it is withdrawn and nothing in it is a suggestion. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

219. **The return, its four heads, and the fourth head's state.** A return asks for **a place, a figure of people, a trade, and a fourth head that is ruled and empty**, and it says at the bottom in the same ink **Nobody is to be sent to find out.** The fourth head is empty on every return that has come back in nine counties. **Batch 0002 may not fill it, may not have anybody say anybody has filled it in, and a character may say the fourth head ought not to be there and may be right and may not be agreed with.** The coast's own sheet holds a word in a fourth head on a different document in a different county, and **any chapter that puts the two near each other must say on the page that they are two documents.** The word that names the founding paper is still not printed and may not be printed in Batch 0002; the outline fixes it as a Block 0003 event and **a Batch 0002 chapter that prints it has spent the series' central mystery in a chapter about a form.**

220. **The first distributed archive and its two nails, and what it is not.** A second set of four ruled heads in a second county, **with no figure, no place, no trade and no name on any of the four**, and the fourth head on the second set ruled **a sixteenth of an inch narrower** than on the first so that a word put in one will not fit in the other, and a man in four years with nine counties of forms can tell which county a word was written in without being told. **Neither nail is a copy of a return and neither is an institution and neither has a name and neither has a keeper, and a copy of a return is still refused.** Batch 0002 may not convert this into a council, a body, a register, a roll or a meeting, may not give it a fixed day, and may not have either woman thanked for it.

221. **Gault Prentice, about fifty-one, in a grey coat, and what he is not.** He arranged the inquiry, walked up a lane on foot, said his own name once, and **did not say whose plan it is and did not say who he answers to.** He is right about the danger a controlled disclosure is built against and the proof is the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel who copied a return out of a room on the first of a month in the year before this one in about nine minutes at no charge. **He is not the antagonist, is not a knave, is not corrupt, and a chapter that makes him a villain has spent the volume's only face and it is a good one.** He has taken the chain of office off and put it on a table and said the thing he has made is worse than he thought, and **he has not got a name to put on a form and has said so in the room.** His want, said once: to be able to say that nobody was hurt who was not told first. Batch 0002 may not restate it and may not thank him.

222. **Nesta Braith, twenty-six, the copyist, and the rule that governs her.** She has said **once, in another county, that the fourth head ought not to be there**, and she was not asked in that room and answered anyway, and **she has decided that being right is not a reason to say a thing twice.** In Chapter 510 she refuses to say it a third time and says what she is going to do with the head instead, **which is the sixteenth of an inch.** **Batch 0002 may not have her say it again, may not have anybody agree with her out loud, and may not turn her into an instrument: a character in a room may be right and be alone in the room and that is the whole of her.**

223. **The five returns, the four counties, and the cut-out line.** Nine counties asked, five answered in eleven days, **four whole with the fourth head ruled and empty and one with the fourth head cut out clean with a knife and a quarter inch of the paper taken away.** The four that came back whole are not the same paper and the four heads are the same word cut by four different knives. **The man who cut that line out is not a villain, has never been asked, and does not know that a form of his is on a table four days up a made road. Batch 0002 may not convict him, may not have him explain himself, may not make him a liar, and may not make the cut a forgery.** And **the cut is not the same act as an empty head and the two must not be used as one on a page.**

224. **About four hundred people at the end of a made lane, on a return, and the count is of bodies and not of residents.** The lane is about a mile and a half from the hired room and ends at eleven houses and a chapel. **The figure of about four hundred on the return is the people who were standing in the lane on the twenty-fourth day of Harvestmonth, and about four hundred people live at the end of it, and the two are about four hundred and are not the same about four hundred and are never added.** A man of about seventy in the fourth house was in his bed and nine women were at the well and none of them lives there. **They have not been told, and Batch 0002 may not tell them in Block 0002 unless the outline buys that, and may not resolve the man of about forty-one's one sentence, and may not let the woman of about thirty-four's argument that the sentence should have gone under the second head succeed without saying on the page what it cost.**

225. **The woman of about twenty-seven at the eleventh of eleven houses, and what she is not.** She walked up the lane with a copy of the return and is not angry, and **she is not a keeper, not a claimant, not on any sheet, and not a symbol, and the man of about twenty-four who walked the lane is not her agent.** **Batch 0002 may not thank her, may not give her a want, may not have her come back to the room without a reason the page gives, and may not make her the volume's protagonist of the coast.** She is twenty-seven and the keeper of records is twenty-nine and they never stand in the same room in this block, and **a Volume 11 writer must not put a woman of about twenty-nine and a woman of about twenty-seven four lines apart in one exchange, because that is a figure of a person next to a figure of a person and it is the defect no instrument in this project owns.**

226. **The nine words, the four sentences, and the offer that was made and not taken up.** **Controlled disclosure is another kind of the same silence**, and the four sentences that follow are about what would have to be done to the sheet. The **second** sentence of the four proposes making two sets of four heads, one in each county, with nobody in either town knowing the other exists including the people who keep the rooms, **and Mara Vey says she has not decided to do it and has not said no to it**, and the third sentence says that if she did it she would tell the woman of about thirty-one with a pen in about four months and before anybody else. **The offer is on the table and is unanswered and Batch 0002 may not answer it, may not schedule its resolution, and may not have the four names at the top of a sheet written down.**

227. **The question the next block inherits, stated as the outline states it.** **What does an office do about a return it cannot read, and about the four roads that are not roads, without asking the people who live on them, and without putting a person in the fourth head?** The block's answer so far is that it cannot, that the only instrument found in ten chapters is a woman in a room two hundred miles off who sent four ruled lines and fourteen words, and **that the proof of the fourth head is the mechanism that spreads it, because the only way to find out whether the fourth head is in every form in nine counties is to ask nine counties for their forms, and the asking is a form going out.**

228. **The four roads that are not roads, and that nobody may name.** Two returns from a coast four days off carry the same word under the first head, the same about nine hundred under the second, four trades under the third, and an empty fourth, and **nobody in that room can say whether the word is a place or a name.** **The four roads that are not roads are a fact in `outline/volume-11.md` and are not on any page of this block and may not be named, counted, located, or nearly named in Batch 0002, and no chapter may put a figure against one of them.** And **the twenty-second notch is not cut, the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years, and nobody has been sent to that coast by anybody in this volume.**

229. **The antagonist, still unnamed, and the inquiry is not his on the page.** He is not named in any of the ten chapters, in `outline/volume-11.md`, in any prompt, or in any state file this block wrote, and **Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** In this block he is on the page only as a plan, **and a plan is a room, two clerks, a chairman with a chain of office, a form with a fourth head, and a panic, and the block never says whose and no chapter of Batch 0002 may say whose.** His name was printed six times in Volumes 01 and 02 and has been printed nowhere in the five hundred and eight chapters since.

230. **The inherited arithmetic defect in the Volume 10 close's certificate, found by recount in this block and recorded and not repaired.** `state/current.md` at the Volume 10 close certified the manuscript at **1,413,316 in five hundred chapters** and its own two scopes at **1,387,333 and 96,168**, and the two scopes make 1,483,501. **The live figure is the recount and is in `state/current.md`, and the difference is 70,185, and a Volume 11 Batch 0002 writer who cites a manuscript total must cite the recount and not the certificate.** It is at `state/continuity.md` 1241. This is the fifth time this project has recorded rather than fixed, and the class is item 1130's month rule: **a thing that was not checked against the thing it is a total of.**

231. **The money columns and the frame, and the standing rule for the next block.** **The ten totals of Block 0001 are 74, 80, 86, 90, 95, 101, 107, 110, 116 and 123 pence, and the block spent nine of its ten columns on one item list before the repair pass and the longest run any column made against another was two hundred and five words, which is the largest single defect this project has found in its own hand and was found by reading and not by measuring.** The rule that follows: **a money column is a person writing down what they spent in their own order, and re-order and re-word the items and never the prices, and hold the longest run any column makes against any earlier column under about thirty words, and the ten of this block now stand at thirty-one words column against column inside the block and thirty-four against the prior manuscript, and the longest run anything in the block makes against anything else in it is thirty-three words and is a sentence in a cost paragraph and not a column.** The ninety spent columns are named in the Volume 10 Batch 0005 prompt and the forty recurring totals in item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository and are the weaker check.

## Threads Volume 11 Batch 0002 carries to Volume 11 Batch 0003

**Items 233 to 248, continuing from 232. Volume 11 Block 0001 ran day 1381 to 1434 and Block 0002 ran day 1438 to 1488, both YR 319, and this section is the whole instruction Batch 0003 inherits. Nothing in it is withdrawn and nothing in it is a suggestion. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

233. **The return, its four heads, and the fourth head's state after two blocks.** A return asks for **a place, a figure of people, a trade, and a fourth head that is ruled and empty**, and it says at the bottom in the same ink **Nobody is to be sent to find out.** The fourth head is empty on every return that has come back in nine counties **except one.** **In Chapter 518 a return came up a made road on a Sunday with one word in the fourth head, and it is the first return in nine counties in about four years with a word there, and a woman of about fifty-three who takes in washing said out loud that she was told to fill it in and that she has wanted it stopped since the day a clerk in her county cut the head out of a form, and both of those are true.** She was not thanked, not asked to explain and not convicted, and the chapter says on the page that **the rule that the fourth head stays empty was never the office's to keep.** **Batch 0003 may not fill the Crown's form's fourth head, may not have an office fill any head on any form, may not turn Chapter 518 into a conviction or a scandal, may not have anybody say the woman was wrong, and may not print a second filled fourth head without a reason that is a person and not an office.** The word that names the founding paper is still not printed and **is a Block 0003 event**, and the coast's own sheet holds a word in a fourth head on a different document in a different county, and any chapter that puts the two near each other must say on the page that they are two documents.

234. **The two words for one lane, and the finding, and what nobody did.** A made lane about a mile and a half from the hired room, ending at eleven houses and a chapel, has two words on it. **At the rail of a common at the landward end about four people call it the Green Lane; at the eleventh of eleven houses six people call it the Long Lane; both are people who live at the end of it and both are right.** The form asks for the name of the road as the people who live at the end of it call it, and got the word the man of about forty-one with a cart already had. **The finding is that a place has more names than a form has lines, and a form that asks for one of them gets whichever the person filling it in already knew.** **Batch 0003 may not resolve it, may not turn it into a boundary or a dispute, may not schedule choosing, may not have the room adopt either word, and may not have a character tell the woman of about twenty-seven that she was right to be angry about a line.** The woman of about twenty-seven was thanked by nobody and is not on any sheet.

235. **A copy of a form, and the fact that nobody regulates one.** **A woman of about thirty-four copied a return for herself in about nine minutes at a table in her own kitchen with a borrowed straightedge, and the copying is bad: the third head is under the second one because the sheet she copied from had a crease in it, and nobody told her to rule the fourth head and she ruled it.** It came out of a bundle in a saddler's shop and into about four hundred kitchens. **The finding is that a copy is the only form nobody has ever thought to regulate, and that about four hundred people at the end of that lane have each got one and about nine of them have compared them and the nine do not agree about a place.** **Batch 0003 may not regulate copying, may not make copying an offence, may not make it a right, may not have anybody thanked for it, and may not have the about four hundred copies counted as a list.** Two copies of one return are now in two counties and both are right.

236. **The fifth line, the two of them, and what it is not.** **A clerk of about forty-six with grey in her hair ruled a fifth line on one of the second set of four ruled heads because the book in her county had five columns and the sheet had four, and Nesta Braith took it off in about four minutes with the knife she rules with and said one thing about it.** **A second fifth line was ruled in the fifth week of the block on a different sheet, went out in the Monday bundle with nobody ordering it and nobody cutting it, and is empty.** **A fifth line is not a fourth head and a line that is ruled and then taken out again is a third thing, and there is no head anywhere in nine counties for a third thing. Batch 0003 may not fill either fifth line, may not say anybody has, may not rule a sixth, and may not make the fifth line a sign or a code or a mark anybody recognises.**

237. **The two sets of four ruled heads, the sixteenth of an inch, and the offer that is still unanswered.** **Set one is in this county and set two is in the county two hundred miles off, and the fourth line on set two is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower than the three above it, on purpose, so that a word put in one will not fit in the other and a man in four years with nine counties of forms can tell which county a word was written in without being told.** Neither is a copy of a return, neither is an institution, neither has a name, a keeper, a register or a fixed day. **The offer of two sets of four heads is on the table, unanswered, and Batch 0003 may not answer it, may not schedule its resolution, and may not have the four names at the top of a sheet written down.** The Monday cart goes up the made road and comes back on the Sunday and a bundle of nine returns goes in each way.

238. **Gault Prentice, about fifty-one, in a grey coat, and the letter that is in his coat.** **He offered to send the four names of the four readers to the nine counties so that a road may complain to a person, said out loud in nine printed sentences that it is worse than the offer he made before, and said he would rather be found than be right.** The room did not agree with him and about four of the nine people in it think he is right. **The four names are not on the page and the letter went back into his coat with them still off it, and a thing that was not done is not a rule and is not a refusal and has no head on it.** **He is not the antagonist, is not a knave and is not corrupt, and a chapter that makes him one has spent the volume's only face. Batch 0003 may not restate his want, may not thank him, may not have him send the letter, and may not have him answer the question he did not ask in Chapter 503.** He said the second cost of the block out loud in Chapter 519, about a room with a thing in it that the room does not have.

239. **Nesta Braith, twenty-six, the copyist, and the two things she has now done.** **She has said once, in another county, that the fourth head ought not to be there, and she has decided that being right is not a reason to say a thing twice, and in Chapter 512 she took a line off a sheet with the knife she rules with and in Chapter 515 she copied a word in nine minutes instead of four and said out loud that her hand had decided before she did.** **Batch 0003 may not have her say it again, may not have anybody agree with her out loud, may not have her refuse a third time in public, and may not turn her into an instrument.** **She is the only person in nine counties who has ruled a fifth line off a sheet and she is not thanked for it and is not on a list.**

240. **The three returns from the coast, and the two documents, and the twenty-second notch.** **Three returns have come from a coast four days up a made road and a day off a boat, in one hand and three inks, with the same word under the first head, the same about nine hundred under the second, the same four trades under the third and an empty fourth head on all three, and the three dates the clerk entered are the day the boat came and are nine days apart and none of them is the day anything happened.** **Nobody on that coast writes a month or a day and has not for about nine years, and no month name and no weekday may be spoken in a character's mouth on that sand in any chapter of Batch 0003.** About four people read the word as a place and about four say it is a name, and **Batch 0003 may not decide which is right.** A Crown form's empty fourth head and a sheet on a flat stone with a word in a fourth head are two documents in two counties and the two may not be printed as one thing without saying so on the page. **The twenty-second notch is not cut, the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years, and nobody has been sent to that coast by anybody in this volume.**

241. **The clerk of about thirty-four, the clerk of about twenty-two, and the clerk of about forty-six with grey in her hair.** The first walked a made lane about a mile and a half on a market Saturday and brought back two words and an hour, and he ruled nothing between them and put no head over either. The second keeps a book of about two hundred leaves with about nine of them written on, was asked for it at about the fourth hour, and said no in nine words, and nobody argued with him. The third came up a made lane with a bundle on a Sunday, slept in the town, and ruled a fifth line on a Monday morning and said out loud that the book was short and not the sheet that was long. **None of the three is a keeper, none is on a list, none is a villain, and the two books are not reconciled and the clerk's nine leaves are not added to the day's book at nine, to the count under a fourth step at nine, or to the bench's nine in about nineteen years.**

242. **The refusal, and that nobody argued with it.** **The first refusal in this volume that nobody argued with is a man of about twenty-two saying no in nine words to a man of about fifty-eight in a hired room, and the second cost of the block was spoken out loud by a man of about fifty-one in a grey coat and not by the man of thirty-two with a limp.** Nobody was thanked. **Batch 0003 may not have anybody argue with that refusal, may not have it withdrawn, may not have the book opened, and may not have the nine leaves entered in any register in nine counties.**

243. **The three figures of carts and the bare leaves.** **A man of about twenty-nine who keeps an inn has kept a tally for about six years on two sticks, one for the Saturday market and one for the Thursday, and the Saturday figure is six hundred and eight and the Thursday figure is five hundred and seventy-one, and the two are two counts of two different mornings and are never added to each other or to about four hundred at the end of a lane or to about nine hundred on a coast.** He asked what the number was for and the answer on the page is that the room does not know. **Where a return carries two numbers the room rules a bare leaf with no head over it and puts both on it, and there are three such leaves in six weeks.** A bare leaf has no keeper, no head, no register and no fixed day, **and Batch 0003 may not give it one, and may not add a leaf to a book of carts with a head on it, and may not let a bare leaf become a list of four hundred people.**

244. **The man of thirty-two with a limp, and what he is now.** `Aren` and `Kest` are at 11 and 11 across the ten files of the block, **in all ten chapters.** **He was asked one question in the ten chapters, in Chapter 520, where a man of about fifty-eight asks him what the room is for and he answers and then declines to be the man who will make two documents meet; the earlier version of this item said he was asked no question in any of them and the page contradicts it and the claim is struck. He conducted nothing, got nothing back, overrules nobody and is the reason none of it works**, and he is a person a room can write down and is not a keeper and is not on a list. He has read about four hundred and nine returns in two days and is not going to read another set in this block's successor. **His answer in Chapter 510 is not to be answered again, and no chapter of Batch 0003 may put him in front of a room and have him answer a question about a form.** The breach between the two men is not repaired and **Batch 0003 may not repair it, may not put either of them in a room with a third person in it, and may not print the word for what they are, which is at two across five hundred and ten chapters and is spent.**

245. **The antagonist, still unnamed, and the inquiry is still not his on the page.** He is not named in any of the twenty chapters of this volume so far, in `outline/volume-11.md`, in any prompt, or in any state file this block wrote, **and Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** **In this block he is on the page only as a plan, and a plan is a room, two clerks, a chairman with a badge, a form with a fourth head, a letter that was not sent and a panic that nobody caused, and the volume never says whose and no chapter of Batch 0003 may say whose.** **No new office, no new council, no new order, no new mechanism and no new enemy, and the word *council* is at zero and stays there.**

246. **What did not move in this block, in one list.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it, and the word *failed* is not used about anything. The offer of one sentence about the wall is unanswered and nobody has taken it up. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The two villages are not back on a road. The corridor's rule is bad and its rate is not printed and nobody has put it in a column. The hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed. The quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The register of the room over the weigh-house is at seven questions and no findings, the day's book at nine entries, the count under the fourth step at nine, and the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine, and none of the four is added to another. The three bars are three objects and this block touches none of them. Hushwater was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, and nothing was opened. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four. **A System panel is printed nowhere in this block and nowhere in this volume, and a sweep of the ten new files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*.**

247. **The measurements of this block, and what they are for.** **Block 0002 is 20,938 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,525,382 in five hundred and twenty chapters, and the five hundred and ten before this block are 1,504,444 on the same basis, and all three are the recount after the review repair; this item first printed 20,738 and 1,525,182 and `state/current.md` first printed 20,733 and 1,525,177, and three numbers for one measurement in three files written in one commit is a measurement nobody took.** Ten footed money columns at 81, 93, 102, 115, 124, 129, 133, 140, 149 and 153 pence, all ten distinct, none colliding with the hundred printed columns already spent, **and the check was made against those hundred and not against the forty recurring totals named in item 36, which are printed nowhere in this repository, and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** Thirty printed speech-length figures, per chapter 4 / 3 / 3 / 3 / 3 / 2 / 4 / 2 / 4 / 2. `about` 553 in 20,938 at 26.41 per 1,000 and `the whole of` 8, and the 26.47 in `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` was a fourth number for it and is struck. Punctuation 17 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 4 semicolons, 0 dashes, 0 rules. Bold parity even, curly quotation marks 187 and 187 across the block and 23 / 22 / 11 / 16 / 24 / 15 / 25 / 16 / 24 / 11 per file. The barred tokens at 0, the four barred numerals at 0, the kin-term at 0, and a self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns at 0. **`Aren` at 11 and `Kest` at 11.** Duplication on a raw-token eight-word shingle with punctuation retained and a file never compared against itself: 41 words against the prior manuscript and 35 inside the block, both frames and neither a money column, and exact duplicate paragraphs 0 and 0 against a manuscript figure of thirty-three. **The name budget stands at 2 of 5 with three slots unspent.** `state/current.md` is the handoff and it is rewritten whole.

248. **What Volume 11 Batch 0003 must not do, in one list, and it is this block's list and not a summary of the twenty-eight above.** **Do not print the word that names the founding paper before Block 0003 and, in Block 0003, print it once and only where a copy of a copy of a paper nobody can date puts it in front of a person who has to decide something, and not in a document that a clerk is filling in.** Do not name the First Witness and do not name the mechanism and do not name the Gatehouse's oldest protocol. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. **Do not fill the Crown's form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not have a second filled fourth head without a person and a reason, and do not turn the woman of about fifty-three into a witness, a villain, a symbol or a subject.** Do not resolve the two words for one lane and do not schedule choosing. Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right. Do not fill either fifth line and do not rule a sixth. Do not answer the offer of two sets of four heads and do not write the four names on a sheet. Do not send the letter and do not have Gault Prentice restate his want. Do not decide whether the word under a first head is a place or a name. Do not say a month name or a weekday in a character's mouth on that sand. Do not cut the twenty-second notch and do not send anybody to that coast. Do not conduct a crossing and do not schedule one. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word. Do not name the antagonist and do not say whose plan the inquiry is. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not reopen or schedule the shut road and send nobody. Do not take up the offer of one sentence about the wall. Do not put the about eleven hundred on anything. Do not put the two villages back on a road. Do not print a rate for the corridor's rule or for the tide road. Do not strike the hearing or the quarantine instrument and do not adjourn, refuse, retell or close the hearing. Do not take the city off the shelf and do not print its three protected numerals. Do not reach the valley. Do not add a figure to the register's seven questions, the day's book's nine, or the nine under the fourth step, and do not add the bench's nine to any of them, and do not add the clerk's nine leaves to any of them. Do not add about nine hundred sheets a month to about a hundred and four in a band to about four hundred and nine parish rolls, and do not derive one of the three from another. Do not add about four hundred people at the end of a made lane to about two thousand people in a market town or to about nine hundred people on a coast, and do not add the two about four hundreds to each other. Do not add the two figures of carts to each other. Do not reconcile the two books and do not merge the three series. Do not have anybody argue with the clerk of about twenty-two's refusal and do not have the nine leaves entered anywhere. Do not have a bare leaf become a list. Do not have the protagonist answer a question about a form, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works. Do not put the two men in a room together. Do not end the block on a summary, on a resolution, and not on a person agreeing.

249. **The Sunday the room was open on, and the floor that has not been washed.** The room over a saddler's is shut on Sundays, and the one exception is a Sunday the cart for a weigh-house two hundred miles off comes back on, because that cart goes on Mondays. **Chapters 510 and 518 are both Sundays the room was open and Chapter 518 is the only one where about nine people were in it instead of about four.** On that Sunday the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key said out loud at the door, before she turned it, that a Sunday the cart comes back is the one Sunday the room is open and that anybody who came in on it would be washing the floor of that room afterwards and nobody else. About nine people came in, nobody argued with her about it, **and the floor has not been washed.** A rule a room keeps and a rule a person pays to break are two different objects and the second one is not on any form in nine counties. **Batch 0003 may not wash the floor, may not have anybody wash it, may not open the room on another Sunday, and may not have anybody say that it ought to be washed.** **Batch 0003 may set a chapter on a Sunday and may not do so without the cart, because a canon rule broken for a reason is a scene and a canon rule broken for no reason is a hole.**

250. **The block was a certificate and not a scene, and Batch 0003 may not be one.** All ten chapters of Block 0002 run `One` to `Five` in the same order and twenty-eight exchanges end in a bold paragraph of one shape that quotes the word counts of the speech printed two lines above it. **The instrumentation is on the page and `state/current.md` already holds every one of those figures, and a chapter that ends on a measurement ends on the wrong thing.** **No chapter of Batch 0003 may print a speech-length certification by default. Where a figure is printed anyway it must foot from the speech as printed, and the total must be read against the itemisation printed beside it in the same sentence, and one figure in Batch 0002 did not.** A figure inside a character's mouth about that character is a different object and is allowed. **And no chapter of Batch 0003 may close on a recap paragraph of the chapter that just happened, at most four of the ten may close on one, and the last page may not.**

251. **The room splits down the middle and nobody moves, and Batch 0003 must not.** *About four of the nine people in that room thought X and about five thought Y* stands sixteen and twenty-six times across Block 0002, and not one person in ten chapters changes an action because of a disagreement. **Cap the device at one use per chapter in Batch 0003, and wherever it appears somebody in the room must do something different afterwards because of it.** A tally that reports a room's opinion is a measurement and not a scene, and a scene is somebody doing something a reader did not expect. **And Block 0002's closing refrain, *the tide decides twice a day and is not paid*, closes eight of its ten chapters and the block's last chapter, which makes it a refrain and not a fact: it may appear at most twice in Batch 0003 and never on the last page, and the last page ends on the hearing standing.**

252. **The money columns are padding and Batch 0003's are short.** About three thousand words of Block 0002, roughly a seventh of the block, are ten cost columns. Every one of them was built as a distinct item list before it was written, every one foots exactly, and every price in about two hundred and fifty items is one of seven values — one, two, three, four, five, eight and nine pence — and no column buys the same thing twice without a second of it, so one column buys three brooms and four lots of boiler's coal and four lots of straw and another buys five beds and three pots of tea and three buckets of tar. **The arithmetic is right and the padding is the defect, and the repair is in the cards and not in the columns: every money column in Batch 0003 is eight to twelve items, the price range is wider than those seven values, and the column still foots exactly as before and is still verified verbatim against its own chapter page.** **The longest run any column makes against any other column in Block 0002 is four words and it is the tail of a list, and that is the only thing about the columns that is worth carrying forward.**

253. **What Volume 11 Batch 0004 must not do, in one list.** Inherit all of the above and add: **do not reintroduce an in-page speech-length certification as a default; do not let a chapter close on a measurement; do not use the four-and-five tally more than once in a chapter; do not close a chapter on the tide refrain or close the block on it; do not write a cost column of more than twelve items; do not print a claim about what a person said, did or was asked without going to the page and reading the evidence; do not break a rule of the room or of the county without a person, a reason and a price on the page; and do not write a number into a state file that was carried forward from another state file.** **The last of those is the one this project has now paid for twice in one block, and it is the cheapest to stop and the hardest to notice.**

## Threads Volume 11 Batch 0003 carries to Volume 11 Batch 0004, and these are the successor's whole instruction

254. **The founding paper is named, and the name is a single ordinary word, and the block's rule about it is now spent.** **The word that names the founding paper was printed once in Chapter 523, out loud, by a man of about fifty-eight with a chain of office, in a hired room over a saddler's, on the twenty-first of Wolfmonth, YR 319, and the printing is in the chapter and nowhere else in the block.** It is not in a document a clerk is filling in, not in a quotation from an archive, and not in any narration before the mouth that says it. **The word is not written in this thread, in `state/current.md`, in this section, in any prompt, or in any chapter of Batch 0004, and a Volume 11 Batch 0004 writer who needs it will have to go to `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0523.md` and read it there, and that is the only place in the repository where it is written.** `outline/series.md`'s question about who first wrote the original consent rule is still unanswered in prose, the hand that made the second cut is still not identified, and the name of the First Witness and the name of the mechanism are still not printed and are still not printed by Batch 0004.

255. **The oldest thing in the works, and the three nine-word sentences, and what each of them is.** A refusal in a hand about a hundred and eighty years old, on a sheet of shop-wrapping paper, under a fifth line with no head over it: *Nothing shuts but two who did not propose it.* A margin instruction in a hand about a hundred and forty years old on a copy of that sheet: *Leave the last line blank until you are told*, and nobody may say whose it is. A selection in a hand about a hundred and forty years old, written over a stroke through the same line: *A thing once asked is a thing twice agreed.* **All three are nine words and all three were counted from the sentence as printed, and the first printing of the refusal was fourteen words under a claim of nine and was recast before the block closed.** The refusal is the oldest datable writing in nine counties; the margin instruction is the one nobody may attribute; the selection is the thing that has been going out into nine counties ever since.

256. **The second cut, and the four things that are not a finding, and the sentence on the page.** **Nobody in nine counties can say who made the second cut, and no chapter of Batch 0003 identifies him, and Batch 0004 may not identify him.** A county is not a man, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet said that sentence in the room in Chapter 524 and said it again in Chapter 530 and asked for it to be minuted as the third time, **and it is the third time because he said it in Chapter 514 and the woman of about thirty-four said it in Chapter 505, and a first printing of this item said he had said it in that room for the first time in this volume and it is wrong.** The copyist of about twenty-six said out loud, unasked, that the hand is about a hundred and forty years old, that a man who writes a thing needing no name has never been asked for one, and therefore that he is **dead, was not a keeper, is not a villain, and has no name** — and those four things are written on a bare leaf with no head over it and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud that the four things are not a finding. **A selection is a refusal with the two witnesses taken out, and a thing with two witnesses taken out is a thing one person can do alone on a Tuesday.** Batch 0004 may not say who, may not make him a villain, may not make him a keeper, may not make the cut a forgery, and may not answer the series row's question in prose.

257. **The fifth line under a form with four heads, and the three other fifth lines, and the difference between all of them.** **A fifth line is a fifth line.** In this block: a fifth line ruled under a form of about a hundred and eighty years ago with a stroke through it, on a sheet in a room in a market town; a fifth line, where there is one, cut into a stone on a coast, about a sixteenth of an inch narrower than on paper; a fifth line in the second set of four ruled heads that a clerk of about twenty-six cut off in about four minutes in Fallowmonth; and a second fifth line that went out in a Monday bundle and is empty. **A fifth line is not a fourth head, a struck-out line is not an empty line and is not a filled one, and a copy of a form is not a form once somebody has added a line to it.** Batch 0004 may not fill any fifth line, may not rule a sixth, may not say anybody has, may not make a fifth line a sign or a code or a mark anybody recognises, and may not put the stroke in a hand.

258. **The two documents in one room for the first time, and what may not be done with them.** A stone a foot and a half long with four heads cut into it and a word in the fourth was carried up the made road off a boat and in a man's arms, in an oiled-paper parcel, and was put beside a Crown form in a hired room. **They are two documents in two counties, one of them a foot and a half long on a flat stone at the landward end of a coast four days off and the other a sheet off a press in a market town, and nobody in that room has the right to say which one is older, and any chapter of Batch 0004 that puts them near each other says so on the page again.** The stone is on no longer a flat stone and nobody was sent to fetch it; the four heads are the same four words and the fifth, where there is one, is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower on the stone. **The Crown form's fourth head stays empty: nobody in Batch 0004 may fill it, nobody may say an office has filled it, and no chapter of Batch 0004 fills any head on any form.**

259. **The three words for one lane, and that nobody may resolve them.** **The Green Lane** at the rail of the common, used by about four people who live at the end of it. **The Long Lane** at the eleventh of eleven houses, used by six, and said by the woman of about twenty-seven who is thanked by nobody and is on no sheet. **The Chapel Way**, given by a man of about seventy in the fourth house, and carried on a copy of a return made in a kitchen at the end of that lane, in a hand that is not the man in the cart's. **A form does not ask a place its name; it asks a person for the name the person has, and a place that has three names has three people.** Batch 0004 may not choose between the three, may not schedule choosing, may not have the room adopt one, may not turn the lane into a boundary or a dispute, and may not have a character tell the woman of about twenty-seven that she was right to be angry about a line. **The about four hundred who live at the end of that lane and the about four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth are the same figure and are not the same figure and are never added.**

260. **The four lines with a question nobody asked, and where they are.** A sheet that is not a form, with four heads cut with a knife, a word in the fourth head, and four lines under it: *You have nine hundred and about four trades and one word. Who is in the room when you answer? Nobody asked us. We are asking you. A road that answers a question about itself is a road that has been found, and we know that, because one of us wrote it to you and not to anybody else.* **Nobody in nine counties asked that question and the room did not answer it, and Batch 0004 may not answer it, may not treat it as a source, may not say who wrote it, and may not turn *one of us* into a person.** The four lines went up the made road on the Monday under a return and through about nine hands in a saddler's shop, and nobody has told the man in the shop what was in it.

261. **The coast's rules, which are the strictest in the volume.** **No month name and no weekday is spoken by anybody from that sand in any chapter of Batch 0004.** Nobody on that coast writes a month or a day and has not for about nine years. The twenty-second notch in a piece of driftwood at the seaward end of that sand is not cut, the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years, and **the notches are years and are not tides.** **No chapter of Batch 0004 may put the wall and the driftwood on the same page**, and the wall is on no page of Batch 0003 at all, and Batch 0004 may not take up the offer of one sentence about it, may not say the wall is void, and may not say it is good. **About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list and Batch 0004 may not put them on anything.** Nobody has been sent to that coast and Batch 0004 may not send anybody and may not schedule a sending.

262. **The protagonist after Chapter 528, and what he may and may not be asked.** **He is a person with his own name in the back of a book of carts, beside the man of about fifty-eight's own, and that book goes up a made road in a Monday bundle, and the whole of what he bought was that a book with two names in it is a record anybody can be shown.** He is on the page in nine of Batch 0003's ten chapters. He was asked nothing about a form, nothing about a rule and nothing about the fourth head in Chapter 528, **and two questions were put to him in front of the other eight, the first by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow and the second, about his own eighth sentence, by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and a first printing of this item said one and it is wrong.** **Batch 0004 may not have him answer a question about a form, may not have him conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and may not print a paragraph accounting for any silence of his.** He is in Chapter 527 and says nothing there and no chapter of Batch 0003 explains it. His stage is Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent, and the qualification is not earned and not claimed; **no crossing is conducted in this volume, none is scheduled, and a System panel is printed nowhere in it.**

263. **The two unreconciled counts and the difference of three, which did not move.** Thirty-one nights against five roads, thirty-four on a chalk board over a yard stair in a fen town, and the difference of three has not been found, and he did not look for it in Chapter 528 and the notebook in his coat has eleven lines and no twelfth line and did not come out of the coat that day. **The leaf with a seventh line that says a person is not looked at in this block and Batch 0004 may not look at it.** Batch 0004 may not convert thirty-one into thirty-four, may not resolve the difference of three, may not say which of his days belong to Merefen, and may not place the childhood room a third time.

264. **The hearing, the register, the day's book, the bench, the clerk's nine leaves, and the four nines and the five nines.** **The hearing is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and the last clause of the block is that the hearing stands.** The register of the room over the weigh-house is at seven questions and no findings, the day's book is at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, the count under the fourth step of an outside stair is at nine, the bench has been asked nine things in about nineteen years, and the clerk of about twenty-two's book has about nine of about two hundred leaves written on, and **none of the five nines is added to another and none of them is added to a figure of persons.** The register's seventh question is still the one nobody is going to answer and nobody is going to open the drawer. About nine hundred sheets a month, about a hundred and four in one band and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three counts of three different series and are not added and none is derived from another. **Batch 0004 may not move any of the five nines, may not add a tenth asking of the bench, may not destroy the register, may not withdraw it, and may not repeal the quarantine or dissolve the instrument.**

265. **The answer the block's room did not give, and which is now the block's second question for the volume.** A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked, out loud, in front of eight people, whether a rule is still a rule when one man changed it in a hand, and **the room did not answer it and the question is not answered in prose.** The man of about fifty-eight gave two sentences that are ways of not answering and stopped. **Batch 0004 may not answer it, may not have anybody argue with the asking, may not make the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel a villain or a crank, and may not let him be thanked for asking.** Chapter 527's last beat is a copyist saying that a line is not a rule and not a finding and is a thing a woman with a knife and a straightedge and no head over her table draws, and **she will not say the same thing a second time in nine counties and Batch 0004 may not have her say it again, may not have anybody agree with her out loud, and may not turn her into an instrument.**

266. **The decision in the room's book, and what it is not.** In Chapter 530 a man of about fifty-eight ruled a head over a line in the room's book that says a rule was changed by one man in a hand and that nobody knows who and nobody is going to find out. **That line is a finding about the room's own limits and is not a finding about a road, and about four of the nine people in that room think writing it down is right and about five think it is a way of not doing the other thing, and the room did not vote.** The head cut into the wood over that line is not a head on any form and is not the fourth head and has no name. **Batch 0004 may not treat that line as a rule about a road, may not have any character act on it as though it were, may not expand it, and may not let it be read back as an answer to the question Chapter 527 asked.**

267. **The four hundred unregulated copies of a return, and the two of them that now disagree three ways.** A copy is a thing nobody regulates and about four hundred people at the end of a made lane each have one and about nine have compared them. **One of those copies now carries the Chapel Way under its first head, and its third head sits under its second one because the sheet it was copied from had a crease in it.** A form goes out and comes back; a copy stays where it was written. **Batch 0004 may not regulate copying, may not make copying an offence, may not make it a right, may not thank anybody for it, and may not count the about four hundred copies as a list.**

268. **Gault Prentice, and what he has now said in that room three times, and what he may not say a fourth time.** He said in Chapter 513 that he would rather be found than be right. He said in Chapter 530 that a room which has just worked out that it cannot find a man, and has said so out loud, and has then ruled a head over the saying of it, is a room that has made a finding about its own limits and dressed it as a finding about the world, and that rooms get read as roads by people who are not in them, and that he has spent about four months building a thing that was supposed to be small and has found out again that the smallness was in the room. **Batch 0004 may not have him say *I would rather be found than be right* again, may not restate his want, may not thank him, may not make him a villain or a knave, and may not let him send the letter with the four names on it, which is still in his coat with the four names still off it.**

269. **The four names and the two sets of four heads, both still unanswered.** A man of about fifty-eight has a letter in his coat offering to send the four names of the four readers to nine counties, and **the four names are not written on anything in this county and the offer is still on the table and is still unanswered.** The offer of two sets of four ruled heads, one in each county, with the fourth line in the second set ruled a sixteenth of an inch narrower so a word put in one will not fit in the other, **is still unanswered and is not answered in Block 0003 and may not be answered in Batch 0004, and Batch 0004 may not have the four names written on a sheet.** The set-one and set-two heads are not a copy of a return, not an institution, not a council, and have no keeper, no register and no fixed day, and neither set may become one in Batch 0004.

270. **What Volume 11 Batch 0003 changed, in one list, and none of it was a plan and none of it was a repair.** A sheet of shop-wrapping paper with a fifth line under it is on a table in a market town. A refusal has been read out loud and a word has been said out loud once. A strip of paper two inches wide with a second nine-word sentence on it is on a table and goes into a Monday bundle. A stone a foot and a half long has come off a flat stone at a coast four days off and is in a room, in oiled paper. Four lines with a question nobody asked have gone through about nine hands in a saddler's shop. A made lane has three names and a copy of a return carries the third. A man of thirty-two with a limp has said in nine sentences that the room's record is a record of his own command failure, and has had his own name written in the back of a book of carts beside another man's. A line is in the room's book with a day and an hour against it. **The hearing stands.**

271. **The measurements of Batch 0003, and what they are for.** **20,718 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, per chapter 2,574 / 1,905 / 1,990 / 2,057 / 2,091 / 1,834 / 1,801 / 2,241 / 2,031 / 2,194, and every one of those figures recounted after the last prose edit of the repair pass rather than carried forward. The manuscript is 1,546,100 in five hundred and thirty chapters, and the 1,525,382 for the five hundred and twenty before this block reproduces the figure at the Batch 0002 close exactly.** Ten footed money columns at 92, 99, 111, 117, 125, 137, 145, 151, 158 and 165 pence, ten distinct totals, ten distinct owners, 102 items in columns of 8 to 12, prices from 2 to 31 pence across 21 distinct values, **the longest run any column makes against any other column in the block is zero words**, and none of the ten collides with the hundred and ten spent columns, the forty recurring totals of item 36 being printed nowhere in this repository and not re-read. **No speech-length figure is printed anywhere in the block.** The calendar, derived twice from day 930: **0 / 0 / 0 mismatches** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, and five market mornings, and **no chapter falls on a Sunday.** Punctuation **13 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 4 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules.** `about` **548 in 20,718 at 26.45 per 1,000** on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator, and **542 at 26.16** on a lower-case-only one, and both are given because the case convention is the whole difference between them and because Batch 0002's 26.41 is this block's figure on the first convention and not a fall in the register, `the whole of` 5, and **0 instances of *about* in front of a figure somebody in the scene could have counted.** The barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the kin-term at **0**; a self-reference sweep at **0** at block scope; the eleven bare-noun self-references are at **202 at manuscript scope, every one in Volumes 01 to 07 and 09 and none in Volume 08 or Volume 10**, and the four-word reporting-frame family is a separate count at **1,608, all in Volumes 04 to 08 and 10**. **`Aren` 20 and `Kest` 20, in nine of the ten chapters, reported separately.** Duplication on a raw-token eight-word shingle with punctuation retained: **32 words against the prior manuscript and 27 inside the block, and the 32 is a dateline frame shared with `chapter-0505.md`, and a first printing of this certification said thirty-eight and the count does not reproduce on any of the four normalisation variants tried, the first a dateline frame and the second the sentence at the foot of a return; exact duplicate paragraphs 0 and 0 against a manuscript figure of thirty.** **The name budget stands at 2 of 5 and this block spent none, and the reason is that the person who said the word is a person an inquiry can call and is not a keeper and a chapter about a document does not need a handle.**

272. **What Volume 11 Batch 0004 must not do, in one list, and it is this block's list and not a summary of the eighteen above.** **Do not print the word that names the founding paper a second time; it is written once, in `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0523.md`, and Batch 0004 may refer to it and may not write it.** Do not identify the hand that made the second cut and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not name the First Witness, do not name the mechanism, do not name the Gatehouse's oldest protocol, and do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping. Do not fill the Crown form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not fill either fifth line, do not rule a sixth, and do not have anybody say a second person has filled a fourth head. Do not turn the woman of about fifty-three into a witness, a villain, a symbol or a subject, and do not thank her. Do not resolve the three words for one lane, do not schedule choosing, and do not let the room adopt one of them. Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right and do not count the copies as a list. Do not answer the offer of two sets of four heads and do not write the four names on a sheet and do not send the letter. Do not say a month name or a weekday in a character's mouth on that sand, do not cut the twenty-second notch, do not turn the notches into tides, and do not send anybody to that coast. Do not put the wall and the driftwood on the same page, do not take up the offer of one sentence about the wall, and do not say the wall is void or that it is good. Do not put the about eleven hundred on anything, do not put the two villages back on a road, and do not print a rate for the corridor's rule or for the tide road. Do not strike the hearing or the quarantine instrument, do not adjourn, refuse, retell or close the hearing, and do not add a tenth asking of the bench or an eighth register entry or a tenth thing to any of the five nines, and do not add any of the nines to another or to a figure of persons. Do not merge the three series of the room over the weigh-house and do not add about four hundred at the end of a made lane to about two thousand in this town or to about nine hundred on that sand, and do not add the two about four hundreds to each other. Do not resolve the difference of three and do not look at the leaf with a seventh line that says a person. Do not have the protagonist answer a question about a form, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence. Do not put the two men in a room together and do not print the word for what they are. Do not have the copyist of about twenty-six say a thing she has said, do not have anybody agree with her out loud, and do not turn her into an instrument. Do not have Gault Prentice restate his want or repeat *I would rather be found than be right*, and do not thank him. Do not answer the question Chapter 527 asked, and do not let the line in the room's book be read back as a rule about a road. Do not name the antagonist and do not say whose plan the inquiry is. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not conduct a crossing and do not schedule one, and do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not schedule it, and send nobody. Do not take the city off the shelf and do not print its three protected numerals, and do not reach the valley. Do not resolve the fever, do not take the Hushwater thread, do not print either of its two figures, do not renew the missing oath-stone, do not place the childhood room a third time, and do not play the accent as a reveal. Do not end a chapter on a measurement, do not close more than four chapters of ten on a recap paragraph, do not close the block on one, and do not close a chapter or the block on the tide. Do not write a number into a state file that was carried forward from another state file, **and do not edit any chapter of Volumes 01 to 10, and do not write `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md` or `outline/ending.md`.**

273. **The three defects this block's own hand found after its first certification, which are the three a successor will repeat if it is not told, and they are the same three this project has now paid for in three blocks running.** **First, a claim about a person is not a measurement and no instrument in this repository can read it.** Chapter 527's closing paragraph set out, in one long sentence, what seven people had said in that room in the order they had said it, and four of its seven claims were false against the chapter's own page; a sweep counts tokens and a claim has to be read. **Second, a figure checked against another figure instead of against the prose it describes.** The block's first certification printed a longest shared run of thirty-eight words against the prior manuscript, and the true figure on its own declared basis is thirty-two, and the thirty-eight does not reproduce on any of the four normalisation variants tried. **Third, a figure whose basis was not recorded is a figure nobody can check including its author**: the block's `about` figure was reported at 26.12 and the true figure on a case-insensitive numerator is 26.45, and the two differ by nothing but a convention the first printing did not name, and 26.41 is Batch 0002's number. **A successor that inherits any figure from a state file without re-counting it on the chapter files will inherit one of these three, and the standing instruction is that each file is counted on its own and the ten are added.**

274. **What Volume 11 Batch 0004 must not do, restated as the four hard prohibitions with the reason for each, because the reason is what stops a writer doing the thing by accident.** **Do not print the word that names the founding paper, because it is spent and `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0523.md` is the only place it is written.** Do not identify the hand that made the second cut or answer the series row's question, because the volume's finding is that nobody is going to find out and a writer who finds out has spent the volume's argument in a chapter about a document. **Do not fill the Crown form's fourth head, either fifth line, or any head on any form, because a head is a shape and not an order and a line somebody has been through is not an empty line and is not a filled one.** Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, be the reason a thing works, or answer a question about a form, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence, because being answerable is what this volume has taken from him and a paragraph explaining his absence is the instrumentation on the page. **To those four, the rest of item 272, and the three of item 273.**

## Threads Volume 11 Batch 0004 carries to Volume 11 Batch 0005

**This section is the successor's whole instruction. A phase reading it should not need a second reading of `outline/volume-11.md` or `state/current.md` to find out what is true, and the numbers at the foot are the numbers Batch 0005 inherits whether it re-counts them or not.**

275. **The record, and what is on it, and where it is.** A head ruled at the top of a set of four pages, a hand cut with a knife and a straightedge, and a name written in the head by the man who made the record. Page one is a command: a crossing, a district and forty men, a term of about a day and a night, no purpose given, no question asked, about nine people let over and not sent back. Page two is a word: *none*, from an account that came up a made road on a cart with the forms, and *none* is printed at the head of every form in nine counties in the die with the four heads. Page three is a copy of a piece of paper not in this county, with a sign made with a pen pressed hard and a day written above it in his own hand of about six years ago, and the sign is not explained by anybody and the record does not say what it does. Page four is a list of five things he does not know and will not be asked about in a room by anybody with a badge. **The record is four pages and a head, it is true, it is complete against his own knowledge and not otherwise, it has been read out loud once to about four hundred people, and a copy of its first page is on the board in a saddler's shop. Batch 0005 may not add a page to it, may not fill the head under it, may not have anybody fill it, and may not say what the sign on page three is for.**

276. **The head, and what it is and is not.** There is no head in nine counties for a record of a man's own wrongdoing and the room ruled one on the tenth of Hearthmonth, which is the second head the man of about fifty-eight has ruled in that room in eleven years. **A head is a shape and not an order; a head is not a fourth head; a head is not on a form; nothing is ruled under it and there is nothing under it to put an answer in; and the whole of what it is for is that a record with a head over it can be shown to a person and a record without one can only be produced by the man who made it.** Batch 0005 may not rule a line under it, may not rule a second head on it, and may not turn it into a form.

277. **The printed line at the head of the form, and the die, and the three things that are not that line.** In letters smaller than the four heads, cut in the die with them, and set in the same sort as the line at the foot that says that nobody is to be sent to find out: *Fever in this parish: none since the last return.* **A printed line is not a ruled line, a printed line is not a fifth line, and a fifth line is not a fourth head.** The other three fifth lines stand where they stood: the one ruled under a sheet of about a hundred and eighty years ago with a stroke through it; the one, where there is one, cut into a stone on a coast about a sixteenth of an inch narrower; the one ruled off a sheet in Fallowmonth and taken off in about four minutes; and the one going out in a Monday bundle that nobody ordered and nobody cut. **Batch 0005 may not fill any fifth line, may not rule a sixth, may not make the printed line say anything else, and may not say who cut the die.**

278. **The crossing, and the sign, and what nobody learned.** His mother made a crossing on a day he wrote down six years ago and a little, and he was not there and has not asked her, and he read the page out loud in a hired room over a saddler's and it is the only page of the four he read himself. **Nothing in the block was a crossing, nothing was conducted, nothing was cut, no mechanism is explained, no word about a roadmark is printed, and the woman of about forty-four says on the page that she could not tell what made the sign and neither could anybody in the room.** A man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said out loud that if the sign opens a road then this is the first document in nine counties with a door in it, and that he does not know whether it does, and that that is the only reason he is letting it stay in a public record. **Batch 0005 may not say what the sign does, may not say why anybody can cross, may not look at the roadmark, and may not have anybody in nine counties find out.**

279. **The story in the market, and the panic, and what it is made of.** A story that a record says where four roads are, out of a town of about two thousand people, inside a fortnight, with **no four roads in the record and no four roads in that room and nobody in that room who has ever been able to say how many there are.** The four comes from the four heads counted by a person in a hurry. A person came up the lane and asked the man of about fifty-eight whether the room was going to shut the roads and he said no, and the person asked then what the room is for, and he did not answer. Nobody was sent to find out, no order was made and no head was filled in on any form. **The whole of what is on the table in this county is a room, two clerks, a chairman with a chain of office that is a badge, a form with four heads on it and the fourth of them empty, and a panic, and no chapter of Volume 11 may say whose any of it is.** The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel said the people in the street are right that a road anybody can find out about can be shut, and that finding is not to be taken back.

280. **The two readings of a man who published his own mistake, and that they are not a contradiction.** About four hundred people who heard the record read out say he is the reason the rest of it stands up, and about four hundred say he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it in the first place, and **the two sets do not overlap and the block does not choose between them and does not have to.** He was asked in Chapter 539 whether he was going to correct them and he said no, and that was the whole of the answer, and he has not corrected one of them, and **no chapter of Batch 0005 may print the reason he is silent for him, may not print a paragraph accounting for a silence of his, and may not have him answer a question about a form.**

281. **The two questions the room has now asked twice and has not answered, and the two it must not answer in prose.** A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked, in Frostmonth and again in Goatmonth, whether a rule is still a rule when one man changed it in a hand; a woman answered it the second time and the answer was wrong and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet gave the reason and was right and nobody agreed with him out loud. The question is not answered in prose. A room in another county sent four lines up a made road asking who is in the room when you answer, and the room did not answer it, and **Batch 0005 may not answer it, may not treat it as a source, may not say who wrote it, and may not turn *one of us* into a person.** Neither is anybody to be thanked for asking and neither is anybody to be a villain.

282. **The lane, and the name, and the copies, and the fact that a lane learns from a person and not from a sheet.** The three words are still three and nobody was asked which is true, nothing is scheduled about choosing, the room has adopted none, and a man of about thirty-four with a sheet has said in writing in that lane and to nobody that he is not going to be the clerk who chooses. **One of about four hundred unregulated copies carries the Chapel Way under its first head and its third head sits under its second one because the sheet it was copied from had a crease in it, and nobody has sent anybody to find out who wrote it.** About nine people at the end of that lane have compared their copies and the nine do not agree about a place. **Batch 0005 may not regulate copying, may not make it an offence or a right, may not thank anybody for it, may not count the copies as a list, may not resolve the three words, and may not turn the lane into a boundary or a dispute.** A man of about fifty-three with a barrow read one page out loud at a rail of a common and what arrived at the eleventh of eleven houses was a man's name, and a woman of about twenty-seven asked what it is for and nobody in that lane could tell her.

283. **Hester Ollett, and what she is and is not.** Forty-one, reads out loud at the chapel at the end of a made lane and at the market cross on Thursdays, asked on a Monday whether she would read a man's record of itself and said she would read anything anybody asked her to read, and then asked what she was paid before she said yes, and was paid. **She is not a clerk, not a copyist, not a keeper, not on a sheet, not a villain, not a symbol and not a keeper of the record, and nobody thanked her and nobody argued with her.** Batch 0005 may not give her a want, may not thank her, may not make her the instrument of a reading, and may not have her read a second thing aloud.

284. **The man of about thirty-two with a limp, and what he is now.** He has his own name in a head he asked for, at the top of a record in public, in front of about four hundred people, and about four hundred people have found him, and being found is the thing he bought in Chapter 528 and he did not know it would be this. He is on the page in eight of the ten chapters of Block 0004 under his own name and he is not in the lane chapter and he is not in the panic chapter, and in Chapter 535 he is in the street outside the door the reader is inside. **He conducts nothing, gets nothing back, overrules nobody, answers no question about a form, and is the reason none of it works. Thirty-one nights still stand against five roads and thirty-four on a chalk board over a yard stair two hundred miles off, the difference of three has not been found, and the notebook has eleven lines and no twelfth line and the leaf with a seventh line that says a person is not looked at.** Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent; the qualification is not earned and not claimed; no crossing is conducted in this volume and none is scheduled; a System panel is printed nowhere in this volume.

285. **What did not move, in one list.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it. The offer of one sentence about the wall is unanswered and nobody has taken it up, and no chapter of this block put the wall and the driftwood on the same page. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The two villages are not back on a road. The hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed. The quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The register of the room over the weigh-house is at seven questions and no findings, the day's book at nine entries, the count under the fourth step at nine, the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine, and the clerk of about twenty-two's book has about nine of about two hundred leaves written on, and **none of the five nines is added to another and none is added to a figure of persons.** About nine hundred sheets a month, about a hundred and four in one band and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are three counts of three series and none is derived from another. The three bars are three objects and this block touched none of them. Hushwater was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, nothing was opened, and the floor of the room over a saddler's has not been washed. The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four. The four heads are four heads and the fourth is empty. The twenty-second notch is not cut. The antagonist is not named.

286. **The measurements, with every basis named, so that a successor inherits figures it can check and not figures it has to trust.** **17,766 words in ten chapters, per chapter 2,062 / 1,904 / 1,836 / 1,669 / 1,849 / 1,838 / 1,654 / 1,668 / 1,439 / 1,847, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added; the five hundred and thirty prior chapters recount to 1,546,100 on the same basis and reproduce the Batch 0003 close exactly, and the manuscript is 1,563,866 in five hundred and forty chapters.** Ten footed money columns at **101, 114, 103, 126, 146, 150, 163, 167, 175 and 182 pence**, 110 items in columns of 10 to 12, 26 distinct prices from 1 to 41, a mark at forty-eight pence in every conversion, every column checked item for item, listed price for listed price, sum for sum, total for total and conversion for conversion against its own page and again after the last prose edit, none of the ten colliding with any of the ninety-three distinct numeric totals printed in the five hundred and thirty prior chapters, and the longest run one column makes against another column in the block **7 words**, being *a pot of tea at five, a* in Chapter 531 against Chapter 537, and against a money column in the prior chapters **16 words**, in Chapter 539 against `chapter-0530.md`. **Both of those figures were 17 and 20 in the first printing and neither reproduced, and the canon phrase this item named as the 20-word run is not in a money column at all: it stands in the tail sentences after a column, and its own longest shared run is the 20 words in Chapter 537 against `chapter-0526.md`.** No speech-length figure is printed in the block; a sweep of the same shape returns thirteen *N words* matches and all thirteen are figures about a document, a short utterance or an idiom. Duplication, raw token, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight-word shingles, a file never compared with itself: **24 words against the prior manuscript and it is the chapter-opening cost-column frame shared with `chapter-0527.md` by `chapter-0536.md`, ending only where 0527 prints the eleventh column he has kept and 0536 prints the twelfth, and 20 words within the block and it is the bold dateline frame shared between Chapters 539 and 540**, and exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more **0 and 0**, against a manuscript-scope figure of 30 in 61 chapter instances. **The 24 is a correction made in this review repair and the 20 it replaced was real but was the wrong run: the block had carried 44 curly apostrophes into a volume whose first thirty chapters carry none, which held the cost-column frame apart from the chapters behind it word by word.
