The apostrophes were folded to straight and the two measurements now agree, which is the only reason this item can name one figure for one basis.** Punctuation **10 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 2 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules**. `about` **406 in 17,766 at 22.85 per 1,000 case-insensitive and 401 at 22.57 lower-case-only**, and `the whole of` 15, and **zero instances of *about* in front of a figure somebody in the scene could have counted.** Curly quotation marks **111 and 111** across the block and **25 / 18 / 16 / 13 / 4 / 9 / 11 / 11 / 2 / 2** per file, straight quotes 0, bold parity even on every line of every file, and doubled words, space-before-punctuation, double blank lines and lines without a terminal mark all **0**. Calendar derived twice from day 930, which is the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday: **0 / 0 / 0 mismatches** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, and **seven market mornings**, and **no chapter falls on a Sunday.** The chapter days are **1545, 1549, 1552, 1557, 1564, 1569, 1573, 1578, 1585 and 1596**, and the year is **YR 319 in nine of the ten and turns on the tenth.** Barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the kin-term at **0** at block scope, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` `hundredweight` at **0** at volume scope. A self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns at **0** at block scope; the bare-noun family at **194 at manuscript scope** against an inherited 202, the eight unaccounted for recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1290 and not explained away; the four-word reporting-frame family a separate count at **1,608**, all of it in Volumes 04 to 08 and 10. **`Aren` 22 and `Kest` 22, reported separately.** Name budget **2 + 0 + 0 + 1 = 3 of 5**, two slots remain. **And two of the block's own figures did not survive verification and were corrected on the page and not in this file: the two money totals that collided with spent columns were cleared by adding pence to the column and not by moving the number, and the block's duplication figures were 35 words against the prior manuscript and 44 words inside the block before the repair and are 20 and 20 after it.**

287. **What this block's own hand found after its first printing, in fourteen findings and no plot, because the class is the one this project has now paid for in four consecutive blocks.** **A claim about a person is not a measurement, and twelve of the fourteen were that and none of them was visible to a sweep.** Chapter 532 claimed a printed sentence was nine words and it was eleven. Chapter 535 claimed a printed notice was nine words and it was eighteen, and was recast to nine and re-counted. Chapter 534 said the man of about thirty-four with a sheet had read the first two pages aloud, on a page that prints a woman of about forty-four reading the first and him reading the second. Chapter 533 said a man had had eleven years of looking at a crossing, on a page whose command is six years ago and a little. Chapter 536 said a speech was four sentences and the page prints three, and said a man had been down nine counties in six weeks when the page's own dates make it a fortnight and a bit, and claimed nine sentences of nobody knowing that are not printed. Chapter 537 said the clerk of about thirty-four had come down the lane on the Thursday, and then said he had not, on the same page. Chapter 538 gave two intervals that the page's own dates contradict. Chapter 540 said a man had not been in that room for nine days, eleven being the figure the dates make. **Then six more were found by reading the block a second time against itself: Chapter 534's dateline and its body each claimed a single question on a page that prints three; Chapter 537 said the man of about thirty-four had been asked nothing by anybody on a page that prints him asking the woman at the rail; Chapter 535 named five counts of persons and called them four; Chapters 535, 536 and 539 each paired the wrong two counts as the same figure and not the same figure; Chapter 536's column ordinal was one too high and Chapter 538's was two too low; and Chapter 538 called a fortnight last week.** Two money totals collided with spent totals and were cleared by adding pence to the column and not by moving the number. Two claimed figures of persons were cut because a person in the scene could have counted them. One name was spent and it is the reader and not a keeper. **And the two findings that would have shipped are duplication: the block's own certification claimed a longest shared run of 8 words against the five hundred and thirty prior chapters and 8 inside the block, and the true figures on the declared basis are 20 and 20, and before the repair there was a 44-word run between the money columns of Chapters 534 and 539 and a 35-word run from Chapter 537's dateline into Chapter 526's.**

288. **The two inherited defects this block's verification found and did not repair, because both sit in chapters that belong to an earlier batch, and Batch 0005 must not repeat either.** **The column ordinals of the man of about thirty-four with a sheet**: Chapter 516 prints his as the ninth he has kept in eleven years and Chapter 524 prints it as the eleventh, and no column of his is printed between them, so one of those two is wrong and this block could not tell which, and Chapter 532 prints the thirteenth and is consistent with 524. **`outline/volume-11.md` §17 prints day 1626 as the first of Mudmonth YR 320 on a Thursday, and the chain gives it a Wednesday**: day 930 is the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday, every month is carried at thirty days, day 1596 is a Monday, and 1596 + 30 is a Wednesday. The same chain gives day 1650 a Saturday, which is what the volume's own opening paragraph says, and a Thursday at 1626 would give a Sunday at 1650. **Batch 0005 derives both halves from day 930 and does not copy the 1626 row.**

289. **The standing prohibitions, in one list, and they carry forward to every batch after this one and not only to Batch 0005.** **Do not print the word that names the founding paper; it is spent, it is written once in `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0523.md`, and Batch 0005 may refer to it and may not write it. Do not identify the hand that made the second cut and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not name the First Witness, do not name the mechanism, do not name the Gatehouse's oldest protocol, and do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping. Do not fill the Crown form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not fill either fifth line, do not rule a sixth, and do not have anybody say a second person has filled a fourth head. Do not rule a line under the head at the top of the record and do not add a page to the record. Do not explain the mechanism, print a System panel, name the works under the abbey, or say what the sign on the third page does or why anybody can cross, and do not look at the roadmark or at the leaf with a seventh line that says a person. Do not say whose plan the inquiry is, do not name the antagonist, and do not add an enemy, an office, a council, an order, a mechanism or a person with a want that is about this book. Do not have the protagonist answer a question about a form, conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, or be the reason a thing works, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence, and do not correct the people who have said he is the reason the rest of the record stands up, and do not decide which of the two readings of him is right. Do not answer the question the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked twice, do not answer the four lines from another county, and do not let either asker be thanked or made a villain or a crank. Do not shut any road, schedule the reopening of the shut road, or send anybody to find out, and do not use the word *failed* about anything. Do not resolve the three words for one lane, do not schedule choosing, and do not let the room adopt one. Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right. **Do not put the about four hundred that live at the end of a made lane and the about four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth into any other pair: the pair that is the same figure and is not the same figure is those two, the about four hundred who came to the chandler's is a third count and is in neither pair, and a money column that names it has to say so.** Do not print a figure of persons in a column without the count of the counts beside it, and do not call five counts four. Do not carry a column ordinal forward without reading the two chapters either side of it. Do not count the two-sets occasions in that room. Do not call a fortnight a week. Do not say a person was asked nothing on a page that prints him asking somebody. Do not say a page prints one question when it prints three. Do not let a dateline's clause order run into a chapter in another block, and **a declared frame is not a licence to copy it: the block behind this one ran thirty-five words into Chapter 526's dateline and forty-four words between two of its own money columns, and a declared frame is still prose and still has to be re-worded.** Do not print a speech-length figure that is not footed on the page. Do not add a pence to a money column and print the old total; add the pence and print the new one. Do not give Hester Ollett a want, thank her, or have her read a second thing aloud. Do not print a total that collides with a spent column; check all ninety-three and not only the hundred-and-over range. Do not copy the printed weekday for day 1626 out of `outline/volume-11.md`.


290. **The eight findings of this phase's second pass over Batch 0004, which the successor inherits as the class it will repeat.** Chapter 540 counted four people in a room whose own page puts five there and gave that room its cost column, and the chairman's speech is built on the difference. Chapter 540 attributed a written refusal to a man whose lane chapter prints him writing one leaf and reading none of it back. Chapter 535 gave the last page of the record to two readers. Chapter 535 enumerated four of five things and Chapter 540 calls them five. Chapter 535 said a payment was in a column that does not contain it, said nine hundred people were on sand, and left a demonstrative with two live antecedents after an earlier repair had tried to fix exactly that. Chapter 537 gave a mechanism a rate an order of magnitude below the stock it had to produce and walked its man past the end of the lane and back. Chapter 539 merged two leaves into one, told on the page what another chapter said nobody would say, dated a copy to the day it had already gone out, and called one afternoon's cost a line and then a column. Chapter 536 said a thing had not happened in a room in four months on a page that shows it four times, and said two speakers were both right and one was wrong. Chapter 533 sent a band of sheets in when nine counties send it out. Chapter 531 gave two counts of ten days nothing supports. Two datelines named hours the bodies did not stage and one dropped the market day its own body prints. **A reader should take from this list exactly one habit and it is the same one this project has now paid for in five consecutive blocks: a number, a count or a claim about a person is not a measurement until somebody has gone to the page and read the evidence, and the fastest way to fail is to write a sentence that sounds like a fact and then not look at what is on the page underneath it.**

291. **What Volume 11 Batch 0005 must not do, and the one thing above all the others: do not let a sentence in a state file stand in for a sentence in a chapter.** The last five blocks have each shipped a state-file figure that the chapter pages do not reproduce, and this phase's own first certification of Batch 0004 claimed a duplication figure of 8 words where the files give 20. **Recount on the files. Count each file on its own and add. Re-run every check after the last prose edit and not before it, and if a figure in a prompt or a state file does not reproduce when it is counted, the figure is the error and not the count.** To that, everything in item 289, and these four: **do not give the number of people in a room in one paragraph and a different number in another in the same chapter; do not give a reader a page and then give it to somebody else; do not give a mechanism a rate that cannot produce what the mechanism is said to have produced; and do not date an event to a day on which the page has already sent it.**

292. **What Batch 0005 inherits from the review repair, and it is three sentences and two figures.** **Write straight apostrophes: the block behind you shipped 44 curly ones into ten chapters that had never carried one, and it cost a duplication figure, because the straight apostrophes in the older chapters broke a shared sentence word by word and turned a 24-word run into a 20-word one on the declared basis. The straight apostrophe is the manuscript's convention and Volume 11's first thirty chapters hold to it without exception. When two measurements of the same thing disagree, print both and say which is which; do not silently adopt the one that makes the earlier sentence in the file true.** **Block 0004's longest run against the prior manuscript is 24 words and it is the cost-column frame; the longest inside the block is 20 words and it is the bold dateline frame between Chapters 539 and 540; the longest one money column makes against another is 7 words. Those three replace a 20, a 20 and a 17 that a reviewer could not reproduce, and the two readings are in `state/continuity.md` 1294 if the next writer wants to know why the number moved.** The exact duplicate paragraph figure at manuscript scope is 30 on a blank-line split with bold markers retained at twelve words, and it is 33 with the markers stripped, 31 at thirteen words with the markers stripped, and 43 on an every-newline split, and whichever one is quoted must be quoted with its own split beside it. Nothing else in Block 0004 changed: the plot, the ending, the prohibitions in item 291, the name budget at 3 of 5, and all forty-one chapters of the volume are as they were.

## Threads Volume 11 Batch 0005 carries to the volume close

**The highest number in this file before this section was 292, and this section continues from it. The volume is closed at Chapter 550: all fifty chapters of Volume 11 are on disk, the fifty chapters are 501 to 550, and the manuscript is 1,588,924 words in five hundred and fifty chapters, which is the count after the Batch 0005 review repair and was 1,588,994 before it.**

293. **The finding, and the room's book, and the fact that it is under a head that is not its own.** The room's finding is that an order may not act on a road without that road's own answer to a question put to it in its own words, and that a rule about procedure is not a promise about what anybody will do. **It is in the room's book since the thirteenth of Mudmonth YR 320, written with a pen and not a knife, under a head cut into the wood over one line for a different thing, and the man of about fifty-eight says out loud that this is the correct condition for it.** The finding was published in nine counties in the fourth line of a sheet of foolscap on the ninth of Mudmonth, with the man of thirty-two with a limp's name in it. **The volume close may not rule a second head over it, may not take the name out of the fourth line, may not move the finding out from under the head that is not its own, and may not rule a line under the head at the top of the record.**

294. **The half leaf, face down for nineteen days, and nobody has turned it over.** It is the half the man of thirty-two with a limp kept when he cut the ninth leaf in two in a room over a weigh-house on the eighth of Thawmonth YR 320. **It came back on the sixth of Mudmonth and it is on a table in a hired room over a saddler's and it has not been turned over, and the volume close may turn it over, may have anybody turn it over, may schedule anybody turning it over, and may not say what is on the other side of it.**

295. **The two readings of a man who published his own mistake, and the fact that nothing chose.** About four hundred people say he is the reason the rest of it stands up; about four hundred say he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it; the two sets do not overlap; he was asked in Block 0004 whether he was going to correct them and said no, and that was the whole of the answer. **The volume close may not choose, may not print a paragraph accounting for his silence, and may not have him correct either reading.**

296. **The cost of being findable, and it is not finished.** About four hundred people in a market town of about two thousand can say where a man of thirty-two with a limp sleeps; a man of about twenty-nine with a handcart says the name out loud twice in a street; on the last day of the volume he was in a market and did not go up the stairs. **The volume close may not make him safe, may not make him invisible, and may not have him stop being a person a town can find.**

297. **The two men.** The breach is open. **Neither of them is on a page of Block 0005, the word for what they are is printed nowhere in it, and the two of them were in one room in Chapter 542 for about seven hours and said nothing about it then and have said nothing since.** The volume close may not repair the breach in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, may not put either of them in a room with a third person in it, and may not print the word.

298. **The hearing.** It stands. It is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and it is said out loud on the first day of every month by a man with a chain of office that is a badge, and nobody has stopped him. **There is no bench in that room, there has never been one, no clerk of a court, no sentence and no penalty, and the volume close may not build one.**

299. **The two sets of four ruled heads and the half leaf that came back, and the fourth head that is a sixteenth of an inch narrower.** Nobody outside two rooms knows the other one exists, neither is an institution, neither has a name, a keeper, a register or a day, and the copyist of about twenty-six ruled the narrower one herself in Fallowmonth and has not told anybody in that county why and is not going to. **The volume close may not make either set an institution, a keeper, a register, a roll, a meeting, a council or a fixed day, and may not fill the fourth head of either.**

300. **The four names, still off the letter.** The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat offered to put four names at the top of a sheet going to nine counties so that a road may complain to a person. The answer was that they go, in a sheet of oiled paper with nothing on the outside of it, and the four names are still off it and the letter is in a coat in that room. **Nobody has been named and nobody may be, and the volume close may not write the four names on a sheet and may not print a fifth.**

301. **The question, asked three times, and the answer that was allowed the third time.** *Is a rule still a rule when one man changed it in a hand?* was put by the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel in Frostmonth, again in Goatmonth, and again on the eighteenth of Thawmonth, and the third time it was answered by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow, who is not paid, is not a clerk, keeps no book of forms, was not asked the question, and had not been in that room before the second of a month carried at thirty. **It is written on a bare leaf with no head over it, was not read back, and the man with the barrow asked that it turn up in about four years in a room that is not that one with a man in it who has never heard of him. Nobody thanked him for the answer and the volume close may not thank him either, and may not have anybody answer the question again.**

302. **The two documents that are two.** The Crown's form's empty fourth head and the coast's own word in a fourth head are two documents in two counties, cut with a knife out of a flat stone, and they have not been in one room in this county since the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth. In Block 0005 four such documents were named in one chapter: the Crown's form, the returned form from a county two hundred miles off with a woman's word in its fourth head, the sheet of foolscap with fourteen words under the fourth head, and the piece of stone a foot and a half long. **Any chapter that puts any two of them near each other says on the page that they are two documents, and the volume close may not put the fifth line and the fourth head in the same clause without saying so.**

303. **What did not move, in one list, and it is the volume's whole negative achievement.** The fourth head of the Crown's form is empty and no office has filled it. The twenty-second notch is not cut and nobody has been sent to that coast, and the notches are years and are not tides. A shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled and nobody was sent, and about nine people are on the wrong side of it. The three words for one lane are still three. The register is at seven questions and no findings, the day's book at nine, the count under the fourth step at nine, the bench's nine is a fourth nine, the clerk's nine leaves are a fifth, and none of the five is added to another. About eleven hundred people are on nothing. Nothing was opened and nobody was sent. No crossing was conducted in this volume and none is scheduled. A System panel is printed nowhere in the volume. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The press was not dated and the floor was not washed. The antagonist is not named and nobody said whose plan it is.

304. **The name budget, and it closed with two slots unspent.** The budget for Volume 11 is five. **Block 0001 spent two, Block 0002 spent none, Block 0003 spent none, Block 0004 spent one, and Block 0005 spent none, so the arithmetic is 2 + 0 + 0 + 1 + 0 = 3 of 5, and the volume closed on Chapter 550 with two names unspent.** They are not for the volume close to spend either unless the close needs a person on whom a prohibition lands, and that person is not a keeper, a clerk, a court officer or a keeper of records. A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns only established names and every hit is to be read before the sweep is reported, and `Cray` is spent at `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0254.md`.

305. **The measurements of Block 0005, with every basis named, so that the volume close inherits figures it can check and not figures it has to trust.** **25,128 words in ten chapters as this item was first written and 25,058 after the review repair, per chapter 2,537 / 2,714 / 2,322 / 2,420 / 2,357 / 2,388 / 2,290 / 2,415 / 2,414 / 3,271 before it and 2,537 / 2,714 / 2,314 / 2,405 / 2,344 / 2,387 / 2,293 / 2,376 / 2,417 / 3,271 after it, and item 307 is the authority and the list before it is kept here because a record of what a repair changed is worth more than a record of the latest number, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added; the five hundred and forty prior chapters recount to 1,563,866 on the same basis, which reproduces the Batch 0004 close exactly, and the manuscript is 1,588,994 in five hundred and fifty chapters. Both of those figures were the count before the review repair and item 307 is the count after it: the block is 25,058 and the manuscript is 1,588,924.** Ten footed money columns at **113, 121, 131, 141, 155, 171, 208, 186, 194 and 226 pence**, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, 105 items in columns of eight to twelve, prices from 1 to 41 pence, a mark at forty-eight pence in every conversion, **every column verified item for item, sum for sum, listed-price for listed-price, total for total and mark-conversion for mark-conversion against its own page after the last prose edit, and each of the ten is unique to its own chapter when checked against every column-style total printed in the five hundred and fifty chapters on disk, which is eighty-one distinct hundred-and-over figures.** **The forty recurring totals in item 36 of this file are printed nowhere in this repository and were not re-read, and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The longest run one money column in the block makes against another column in the block is 7 words and reproduces, and against a money column in the five hundred and forty prior chapters this item printed 16 and the true figure is 8, being *a third pot of tea at three, a* in Chapter 542 against `chapter-0516.md`; 16 is Block 0004's figure carried forward. **No speech-length figure is printed in the block**; a sweep of the same shape returns twenty-two *N words* matches and all twenty-two are figures about a document, a short utterance or an idiom. Duplication, raw token, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight words or more, a file never compared with itself, and the basis as this item first printed it said *bold markers removed* and carried a number that basis does not return, which item 307 corrects: **26 words against the prior manuscript when both kinds of emphasis marker are removed and 25 when only the double ones are, and it is the same run either way, a quoted document — the four lines from the coast in Chapter 547 against `chapter-0525.md`, which are the same four lines — and 23 words within the block, reached by five separate pairs, one of which is the money-column tail and four of which are chapter-opening and bare-leaf frames.** Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more: **0 among the ten new and 1 between the ten new and the manuscript, and that one is the question *is a rule still a rule when one man changed it in a hand?* printed identically in `chapter-0536.md` and `chapter-0544.md` because it is the same question asked three times.** Manuscript scope, blank-line split with bold markers retained at twelve words: **31 paragraphs in 63 chapter instances, and the inherited figure of 30 in 61 reproduces exactly on the five hundred and forty prior files on the same basis, chapter instances being counted as (paragraph, file) pairs.** Punctuation **8 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 3 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules**, and the em dashes were 5. `about` **701 in 25,128 at 27.86 per 1,000 case-insensitive and 699 at 27.78 lower-case-only**, and `the whole of` 30, and **zero instances of *about* in front of a figure somebody in the scene could have counted**. Curly quotation marks **100 and 100** across the block and **10 / 21 / 6 / 14 / 1 / 10 / 9 / 5 / 15 / 9** per file, straight quotes 0, **curly apostrophes 0 and straight apostrophes 102**, bold parity even on every line of every file, and doubled words, space-before-punctuation and double blank lines all **0**. Calendar derived twice from day 930, which is the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday: **0 / 0 / 0 mismatches** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, and **eight market mornings**, and **no chapter falls on a Sunday.** The chapter days are **1597, 1603, 1606, 1613, 1615, 1622, 1627, 1634, 1641 and 1650** and the year is **YR 320 in all ten**. Barred tokens, the four barred numerals and the kin-term at **0** at block scope, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` `hundredweight` at **0** at volume scope over all fifty chapters. A self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns at **0** at block scope, and item 307 records that the twenty-six were not named and did not include `this project`, which stood at two, and that the list is now printed in `state/current.md`. **`Aren` 12 and `Kest` 12, reported separately, in all ten chapters.** Name budget **2 + 0 + 0 + 1 + 0 = 3 of 5**, two slots unspent. **And one instrument in this phase was wrong and its wrongness is the last thing this section has to say: a duplicate-run figure was printed first as 8 words against the prior manuscript and 8 inside the block, and the script that produced it extended a run by testing a one-word tuple against a set of eight-word tuples, so the window could never advance and it could only ever return its own threshold. The true figures on the declared basis were 132 and 82. A figure produced by an instrument that cannot advance its own window is not a measurement, and it is the same class of error as a number checked against a number instead of against the prose, only quieter.**

306. **What Volume 11's close must not do, and it is the last item in this file that carries canon; item 307 follows it and carries the review repair.** **Do not reconcile anything.** Do not choose between the two readings of a man who published his own mistake. Do not turn the finding into a promise, and do not let a rule about procedure be described as a promise about what anybody will do. Do not fill the Crown's form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not fill either fifth line, do not rule a sixth, and do not have anybody say a second person has filled a fourth head. Do not rule a line under the head at the top of the record and do not add a page to the record. Do not cut the twenty-second notch, do not send anybody to that coast, and do not decide whether the word under a first head is a place or a name. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not schedule it, and do not use the word *failed* about anything. Do not build a court: no bench, no bench-list, no hearing room, no clerk of a court, no sentence, no penalty, and no office that did not already exist in that room. Do not answer the question the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked three times, do not answer the four lines from another county, and do not thank or villainise either asker. Do not turn the half leaf face up, and do not say what is on the other side of it. Do not name the antagonist, do not say whose plan the inquiry is, and do not add an enemy, an office, a council, an order, a mechanism or a person with a want that is about this book. Do not name the First Witness, do not name the mechanism, do not print the word that names the founding paper, do not identify the hand that made the second cut, do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose, and do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping. Do not explain the mechanism, print a System panel, name the works under the abbey, say what the sign on the third page of the record does, say why anybody can cross, or look at the roadmark. Do not conduct a crossing or schedule one, and do not print *stage*, *ledger* or *qualification*. Do not resolve the three words for one lane, do not schedule choosing, and do not let the room adopt one. Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right. Do not add any two refusal figures together and do not add any of them to a figure of persons, and in particular do not put the about four hundred who came to the chandler's into the pair that is the same figure and is not the same figure, which is the lane's about four hundred with the about four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth. Do not print a figure for any of the three bars. Do not put the city on a page, summarise it, or restore it, and its three protected numerals stay at zero. Do not put the wall and the driftwood on the same page. Do not reconcile the refusal figures, do not add the clerk's nine leaves to the day's book or to the nine under the fourth step or to the bench's nine, and do not merge the three series of the room over the weigh-house. Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, cross anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, answer a question about a form, or be the reason a thing works, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence. Do not have the copyist say the thing about the fourth head a second time, do not have anybody agree with her out loud, and do not turn her into an instrument. Do not give Hester Ollett a want, thank her, or have her read a second thing aloud. Do not make the woman of about fifty-three a villain, a witness, a symbol or a subject, and do not thank her. Do not spend a name without a person on whom a prohibition lands, and do not spend a name on a keeper, a clerk, a court officer or a keeper of records. **Do not let a sentence in a state file stand in for a sentence in a chapter, and do not quote a figure from this file without its basis: every number above was counted on the files after the last prose edit, and the three things this project has paid for in six consecutive blocks are a weekday copied instead of derived, a money total moved instead of a column corrected, and a duplication figure returned by an instrument that cannot advance its own window.**

307. **What Batch 0005 inherits from its review repair, in one item, and the two of its eight findings that are new classes for this file.** **Eight findings, all applied, none in a controller file, no plot moved, no chapter restarted, no date, weekday, money total, name, canon figure or ending touched. Two chapters were printing a phrase lifted verbatim out of their own prompt — *a figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person is the defect no instrument in this project owns* — and the words *this project* are the author's and no register in this book has them; both are revoiced in the room's terms and keep every fact. A whole bold paragraph in 0548 whose content was the non-existence of a man of about thirty-three, and which explained its own arrangement, is gone and four people now do four physical things because of what was decided. The retired reporting frame was certified at 0 on four enumerated forms while standing at six in a fifth, *have it since that*, and is now swept as a family and is at 0 across the whole of Volume 11. The prompt's cap of one use per chapter on *about four of the nine thought X and about five thought Y* was broken twenty times in four chapters and is now one use in nine chapters and none in the tenth, and the over-cap chapters were revoiced into *half* and *the other half* rather than cut, so every chapter keeps a use with a consequence on the page. A self-reference sweep across twenty-six unnamed patterns was certified at 0 and was false, because `this project` was not in the twenty-six. And two figures about a person that the review did not report: 0549 said a man of thirty-four **with a satchel** asked the one-word question about four days ago, and the asker is the man of thirty-four with a **sheet** on Goatmonth the twentieth, YR 319, which is day 1526 against Chapter 549's day 1641 and is about four months; and 0543 describes a man narrated as having said three things as *a man who says nine things*.** **The counts, recounted on the files after the last edit: the block is 25,058, per chapter 2,537 / 2,714 / 2,314 / 2,405 / 2,344 / 2,387 / 2,293 / 2,376 / 2,417 / 3,271, and the manuscript is 1,588,924; ten money columns foot unchanged at 113, 121, 131, 141, 155, 171, 208, 186, 194 and 226, because no repair touched an item list; punctuation 8 / 0 / 2 / 3 / 0 / 0 with the two lost em dashes standing in the paragraph that named a man who was not in the room; `about` 672 in 25,058 at 26.82 case-insensitive and 670 at 26.74 lower-case-only; `the whole of` 30; curly quotation marks 100 and 100 and straight apostrophes 102 and curly apostrophes 0; `Aren` 12 and `Kest` 12; quoted spans 10 / 21 / 6 / 14 / 1 / 10 / 9 / 5 / 15 / 9; the calendar unmoved at 0 / 0 / 0 on the ten datelines; the barred tokens and the four barred numerals and the kin-term at 0.** **And three figures in the live records did not hold and are corrected on the page rather than carried: the money-column run against a prior money column is 8 words and not 16, which is Batch 0004's figure inherited without a count; the duplication figure of 26 words is real but its stated basis did not produce it, since 26 needs both kinds of emphasis marker removed and the declared basis removed only the double ones and returns 25, and the basis is corrected to *emphasis markers removed* with 25 printed beside 26; and the 23-word self-comparison is reached by five separate pairs and the record named one of them.** **The new class, and it is the one a Volume 12 writer should take from this: a token sweep cannot catch authorial register, because the words in it are ordinary English and every one of them is a word this book uses, and so a rule about register has to be a rule somebody reads for. The second: a pattern list that is not printed cannot be checked, and a sweep of twenty-six unnamed patterns that returns zero is a claim about a list nobody can see. The third, and the oldest: three of the eight findings are a number inherited from an earlier block instead of counted, and they live in the file a phase reads first and trusts.** Nothing else changed. Item 306 stands in full, the plot and the ending stand, the name budget stands at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent, and the forty-one chapters before this block are as they were.


## Threads the Volume 11 close hands to Volume 12

**The highest number in this file before this section was 307, and this section continues from it. Volume 11 is closed at Chapter 550 and the manuscript is 1,588,902 words in five hundred and fifty chapters after the close's forty-one repairs in twenty-three chapters, which was 1,588,924 before them. The close record is `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md`. Every figure in this section was counted on the chapter files after the last prose edit and carries its basis; a successor that quotes one without its basis is quoting a claim.**

308. **The frame, and the seven things three files got wrong about it. Confirmed and corrected.** Day 1381, the twenty-sixth of Rainmonth YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day, is Volume 11's first day; day 1650, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 320, a Saturday and a market day, is its last. **Two hundred and sixty-nine days elapsed, two hundred and seventy inclusive, and the year turns on day 1596, the first of a month and the last day of Block 0004.** The five blocks are 54 / 51 / 54 / 52 / 54 days inclusive and begin on days 1381, 1438, 1489, 1545 and 1597. All fifty datelines foot on month, day of month, weekday and market flag, the two half-derivations were run separately and returned 0 and 0, and thirty-four of the fifty chapters fall on a market morning. **Corrected by the close: the duration is 270 inclusive and not 271, and 271 is what the close prompt, the plan twice, the live handoff and the Batch 0004 batch summary all printed; `outline/volume-11.md` §17 is wrong about six further calendar facts and the plan is not edited.** A Volume 12 writer derives both halves and reports them as two figures.

309. **The Sunday rule, and the floor, and the day the two of them are the same day. Confirmed and extended.** **Two chapters of Volume 11 fall on a Sunday — `chapter-0510.md` at day 1434 and `chapter-0518.md` at day 1483 — and the rule held in the first and was broken in the second, in the dateline, in front of nine people.** The room over the saddler's is shut on Sundays. It was opened on the eighth of Wolfmonth YR 319 because a cart comes back from a weigh-house two hundred miles off on Sundays, and a woman of about thirty-four said out loud that anybody who came in on a Sunday in that room would be washing the floor of it afterwards and nobody else. **Nobody washed the floor, and the volume says so in Chapter 0545 on a Saturday two months later, and the floor has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth.** A Volume 12 chapter may wash it, may have somebody wash it, may mention it, and may not report the rule as unbroken, and may not say who washed it and was thanked, because nobody was thanked and nobody washed it.

310. **The finding, and the head that is not its own, and the fourth line that is not a fourth head. Confirmed.** It is in the room's book since the thirteenth of Mudmonth YR 320, with a pen and not a knife, under a head cut into the wood over one line for a different thing, and the man of about fifty-eight says out loud that this is the correct condition for it. It went to nine counties in the fourth line of a sheet of foolscap on the ninth of Mudmonth, with the protagonist's name in the fourth line, and **the fourth line is a fourth line and not a fourth head, it is on a different paper in a different hand, it is not a fifth line, and a head is a shape and not an order.** About four hundred people read it standing up in a street and about four hundred carried it. **The close may not rule a second head over it, may not take the name out of the fourth line, may not move the finding out from under the head that is not its own, and may not rule a line under the head at the top of the record.**

311. **The half leaf. Confirmed, and the interval foots.** It is the half the man of thirty-two kept when he cut the ninth leaf in two on the eighth of Thawmonth YR 320, which is day 1603; it went down a made road to the landward end of a coast four days off on the Monday after that, in the hand of a man of about thirty-four with a sheet who was not sent and is not nobody-sent; it came back on the sixth of Mudmonth, which is day 1631, in a bundle with nine returns, and it is face down on a table in the room over the saddler's. **1650 − 1631 = 19, and Chapter 0550's *nineteen days* is exact against the volume's last day.** It has four heads on it with a question under the fourth one and it is not a form and it is not the form and it is not a return and it is not on a bundle in nine counties. **A Volume 12 chapter may turn it over, may have somebody turn it over, may schedule somebody turning it over, and may not say what is on the other side of it, and may not print how many days it has been there, which is the one figure the object does not give up.**

312. **The two readings, and the cost of being findable. Confirmed.** About four hundred people say he is the reason the rest of it stands up; about four hundred say he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it; the two sets do not overlap; he was asked in Block 0004 whether he was going to correct them and said no. **About four hundred people in a market town of about two thousand can say where a man of thirty-two with a limp sleeps, and on the volume's last day he was in the market and did not go up the stairs, and the man of about fifty-eight refused out loud to say what happens to him on the grounds that a room that says what happens to a man is a room that has started keeping a book of people.** **A Volume 12 chapter may not choose between the readings, may not print a paragraph accounting for his silence, may not make him safe, may not make him invisible, and may not have him stop being a person a town can find.**

313. **The two men. Confirmed.** The breach is open. **They were in one room in Chapter 0542 for about seven hours for the first time since a Tuesday in Rainmonth in the year before this one, and neither of them said one word about the four and a half years, and he asked for the leaf to be cut, and nothing has been said in the forty days since.** The word for what they are is printed nowhere in Volume 11 and the barred kin-term is at two across five hundred and fifty chapters and is spent. **A Volume 12 chapter may not repair the breach in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, may not put either of them in a room with a third person in it, and may not print the word.**

314. **The hearing, and the four documents, and the fifth line. Confirmed and extended.** The hearing stands, unadjourned, unrefused, unretold and unclosed, and it is said out loud on the first day of every month by a man whose chain of office is a badge. **There is no bench in that room, there has never been one, no clerk of a court, no sentence and no penalty, and nobody in that county is sentenced to anything by anything said in it.** The Crown's form's empty fourth head, the returned form from a county two hundred miles off with a woman's word in its fourth head, the sheet of foolscap with fourteen words under the fourth head, and the piece of stone a foot and a half long in a room in another county are **four documents in four counties, none of them the Crown's form, and the two nearest of them have not been in one room in this county since the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth.** A fifth line was ruled in about four minutes on a Monday morning by a clerk of about twenty-eight with a form and nobody ordered it and the room has not asked who did. **Any chapter that puts any two of the four near each other says on the page that they are two documents, and a Volume 12 chapter may not put the fifth line and the fourth head in the same clause without saying so.**

315. **The question, the four names, and the five nines. Confirmed.** *Is a rule still a rule when one man changed it in a hand?* was asked in Frostmonth, again in Goatmonth, and again on the eighteenth of Thawmonth, and the third time it was answered by a man of about fifty-three with a barrow who is not paid, is not a clerk, keeps no book of forms, was not asked it, and had not been in that room before the second of a month carried at thirty: **a rule is a rule when one man changed it in a hand, and a rule nobody can name the maker of is a rule anybody can be shown.** It is on a bare leaf with no head over it, was not read back, and he asked that it turn up in about four years in a room that is not that one. **Nobody thanked him and a Volume 12 chapter may not thank him and may not have anybody answer the question again.** The four names are still off the letter and the letter is in a coat. The count under the fourth step, the day's book, the bench's nine things, the clerk's nine leaves and a room's ninth hour are **five nines and none is added to another and none is added to a figure of persons**; the register is at seven questions and no findings; and about nine hundred sheets a month, about a hundred and four in one band and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month are **three counts of three series, never added and never derived from one another, and the volume had one of the three wrong in its own enumerating column and the close corrected it to about nine hundred.**

316. **The three words for one lane, the three bars, the two roads, and the rest that did not move. Confirmed.** The Green Lane at the rail of a common, the Long Lane at the eleventh of eleven houses, and the Chapel Way behind the chapel are still three, the room adopted none and scheduled no choosing. The kerb bar, the market bar and the fourth-months bar four miles west of Marl are three objects and **Volume 11 printed no figure for any of them.** The nine-mile road with a gate at the end of it and the made lane that stops being made a mile and a half out of the market town are two roads and no chapter compared them, no chapter sent anybody from one to the other, and neither was opened. The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled, about nine people are on the wrong side of it, and the word *failed* is at zero about anything in the volume. The twenty-second notch is not cut, the notches are years and not tides, and nobody has been sent to that coast; about eleven hundred people are on nothing. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. Nothing was opened, nobody was sent, **no crossing was conducted in this volume and none is scheduled, and a System panel is printed nowhere in it.** The press was not dated. The antagonist is not named and no chapter said whose plan the inquiry is. **The name budget closed at 3 of 5 with two slots unspent, and the close does not spend them.**

317. **The measurements of Volume 11, with every basis named, and the seven figures a successor must not inherit without a count. Item 319 is the current one: a review of the close pass that followed took five words out of `chapter-0547.md`, so the volume, Block 0005 and the manuscript are each five lower than printed here, and nothing else in this item moved.** **105,401 words in fifty chapters; Volumes 01 to 10 recount to 1,483,501; manuscript 1,588,902 in five hundred and fifty.** Blocks **20,919 / 20,940 / 20,717 / 17,767 / 25,058**. Fifty footed money columns at 74 to 226 pence, 944 item lines, one collision at 101. Fifty-two speech certifications, all in Chapters 501 to 520, all footing on the page, none in the last thirty chapters. Duplication on the declared basis **34 against the prior, 35 within, 18 for the last block against the prior, 24 within the last block with punctuation retained and 35 with it stripped.** Exact duplicate paragraphs **33 in 67, 34 in 69, 32 in 65, none touching Volume 11.** Calendar **0 / 0 / 0** over fifty datelines on both halves and the market claim. Punctuation **68 / 0 / 10 / 9 / 0 / 0**. Curly quotation marks **749 and 749**, straight apostrophes 410, curly apostrophes 0. `about` **2,752 at 26.11** and **2,728 at 25.88**. `the whole of` **67**. `about four people` **54**, which no ledger in this project tracks. `Aren Kest` 71. **The seven to re-count rather than inherit: the speech certification count, which `state/current.md` had at zero; the Sunday count, which the close prompt and the live handoff had at zero and which is two; the duration, which four files had at 271 and which is 270; the money-column count, which the close prompt had at a hundred and forty and which is fifty; `sixty-one`, which the live handoff had at 142 and which is 143; the block-internal duplication run, which the Batch 0005 record named one run for and which is reached by eight pairs; and the 26-word duplication figure, which is real on a basis the record did not name and which is 25 on the basis it did.**

318. **And what Volume 12 must not do, in one list.** **Do not reconcile anything.** Do not choose between the two readings of a man who published his own mistake. Do not turn the finding into a promise, and do not let a rule about procedure be described as a promise about what anybody will do. Do not fill the Crown's form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not fill either fifth line, do not rule a sixth, and do not have anybody say a second person has filled a fourth head. Do not rule a line under the head at the top of the record and do not add a page to the record. Do not cut the twenty-second notch, do not send anybody to that coast, and do not decide whether the word under a first head is a place or a name. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not schedule it, and do not use the word *failed* about anything. Do not build a court: no bench, no bench-list, no hearing room, no clerk of a court, no sentence, no penalty, and no office that did not already exist in that room. Do not answer the question the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked three times, do not answer the four lines from another county, and do not thank or villainise either asker. Do not turn the half leaf face up, and do not say what is on the other side of it. Do not name the antagonist, do not say whose plan the inquiry is, and do not add an enemy, an office, a council, an order, a mechanism or a person with a want that is about this book. **Do not name the First Witness, do not name the mechanism, do not print the word that names the founding paper, do not identify the hand that made the second cut, do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose, and do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping.** Do not explain the mechanism, print a System panel, name the works under the abbey, say what the sign on the third page of the record does, say why anybody can cross, or look at the roadmark. Do not conduct a crossing or schedule one, and do not print *stage*, *ledger* or *qualification*. Do not resolve the three words for one lane, do not schedule choosing, and do not let the room adopt one. **Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right.** Do not add any two refusal figures together and do not add any of them to a figure of persons, and in particular do not put the about four hundred who came to the chandler's into the pair that is the same figure and is not the same figure. Do not print a figure for any of the three bars. Do not put the city on a page, summarise it, or restore it, and its three protected numerals stay at zero. Do not put the wall and the driftwood on the same page. Do not merge the three series of the room over the weigh-house, and do not use about four hundred and nine for anything but parish rolls a month. Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, cross anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, answer a question about a form, or be the reason a thing works, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence. **Do not have the copyist say the thing about the fourth head a second time, do not have anybody agree with her out loud, and do not turn her into an instrument.** Do not use the bare-leaf clause twice and do not use it for two different acts. Do not give Hester Ollett a want, thank her, or have her read a second thing aloud. Do not make the woman of about fifty-three a villain, a witness, a symbol or a subject, and do not thank her. Do not thank anybody. Do not spend a name without a person on whom a prohibition lands, and do not spend a name on a keeper, a clerk, a court officer or a keeper of records. **Do not let a sentence in a state file stand in for a sentence in a chapter, and do not quote a figure from this file without its basis and without the figure it replaced.**

319. **The current measurements of Volume 11, and the five things a Volume 12 writer takes from the review of the close rather than from the close.** **The volume is 105,396 words in fifty chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own; Volumes 01 to 10 recount to 1,483,501 and the manuscript is 1,588,897. Blocks 20,919 / 20,940 / 20,717 / 17,767 / 25,053, and the five block lengths are 57 / 51 / 56 / 52 / 54 days inclusive, which is 270, and 270 is the inclusive span of the volume's own first and last datelines.** **The first is that a repair pass can be right about a class and still miss an instance of it, and the miss was in the same chapter it had already repaired and one line away from a repair it had made four chapters earlier: the woman of about thirty-one with a pen is a woman in ten places in `chapter-0506.md` and was a man in one. A sweep that counts occurrences per chapter would have found it and a sweep that counts per volume would not, and a reader finds it in one second.** The second is that a repair can create the defect it was sent to remove, and this one did it in the same clause: the close's revoicing of *it is on the page* to *it is here* reached for a clause that was already on the line and printed it twice, **and the close's own duplication metric was an eight-word window, which cannot see a five-token repeat inside a clause — so a metric that is stated with its basis can still be blind, and the basis is part of the finding.** The third is that a document which counts its own repairs must re-add them, and this one printed *forty-one* in three paragraphs and *thirty-eight* in five and never noticed, **which is the whole argument of `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md` §11.2 arriving four days after that document made the argument.** The fourth is that a table can have one counted column and one estimated column and only the estimated one is wrong: five correct start days stood beside five block lengths that were 5 short, in two blocks, invisibly. The fifth is the one that is not repaired and is a fact about this repository rather than about the book: **`workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` is still on disk asking for ten chapters that already exist, it has no `.done` and no `.retired`, and the dispatcher reads `.retired`; a Volume 12 phase is not the thing that should write it, and until somebody with the dispatcher's keys does, the next run of the loop will hand a writer a batch that is finished.** The volume's plot is untouched by all five: the founding paper's word is still printed once at `chapter-0523.md`, the half leaf is still face down, the fourth head is still empty, the finding is still in that room's book under a head that is not its own, and `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md` §11.3 is the list.

## Threads the Volume 11 Batch 0003 verification pass carries forward, items 320 to 327

**This section is written by the phase that ran the verification pass on `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0530.md` and it is the successor's whole instruction, along with everything above it. Six defects were found and repaired and every figure below was counted after the last prose edit.**

320. **The false scope, unrepaired, and it is the successor's first problem and not a writing problem.** `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` carries a `# SCOPE OF THIS RUN` header generated by `scripts/rescue_overscoped.py`, which read the prompt's first four lines, found *Chapters 511 to 520* in the sentence describing the previous batch, and scoped the run to **Chapters 511 to 515** — five finished chapters of a retired batch that Chapters 516 to 520 are built on. **A successor that obeys that header will destroy five finished chapters. It should verify a scoped range against the chapter files before writing one word of it, and treat a header whose range is already on disk and already finished as a false scope and report it, exactly as item 1318 does.** The header is controller-owned and was not edited.

321. **What the pass changed, in five lines and one reversal, so that no successor re-finds them as if they were open.** Chapter 522's claim that a man was asked nothing and said four enumerated things; Chapter 528's claim that the protagonist spoke twice; Chapter 528's month for the unanswered question, moved from Harvestmonth to Fallowmonth to agree with Chapters 524 and 525; Chapter 528's four words *he went on in the same turn*, so that nine sentences foot in ten spans; Chapter 529's paragraph that reported three things a man said instead of printing them, and Chapter 529's one sentence from Aren Kest, now said rather than reported. **The sixth line of the earlier printing of this item — Chapter 530's *once before*, now *twice before, in Embermonth and in Wolfmonth* — was a false finding and has been reversed: the Wolfmonth occasion at `chapter-0524.md` l.29 is the woman of about thirty-four's and the chapter names the man as the second of the three, so the man said it once, the page was right, and the fourth pass restored the chapter to *once before, in Embermonth*. A successor must not re-apply it.**

322. **The counts that were moved and the counts that were not.** Block 0003 is **20,672** in ten chapters. Volume 11 is **105,351** in fifty. The manuscript is **1,588,852** in five hundred and fifty. Volumes 01 to 10 are **1,483,501** and did not move. **A successor carrying any of these figures must recount them on the chapter files rather than carry them forward, because this pass is the fourth to move them and each pass has moved a different one, and because the third pass moved three chapters' words and did not move one mark, number or sweep in `state/current.md`, which is the failure this file's own sentence about not carrying figures forward exists to prevent.**

323. **The four references a successor will want and that are now on a page.** The word that names the founding paper is at one hit in the whole manuscript, `chapter-0523.md`, spoken aloud, with the nine words *Nothing shuts but two who did not propose it* printed before it and the chapter saying on the page that it is printed once. The nine words in the margin of the copy of the copy are *Leave the last line blank until you are told*, printed once at `chapter-0521.md`, and **no chapter says whose they are and no chapter may.** The nine-word sentence on the strip is *A thing once asked is a thing twice agreed*, at `chapter-0524.md`. The two words in the margin are not the two words of the refusal and not the two words of the selection.

324. **The three nines of the block and the four other nines of the volume, all separate, none to be added.** The margin instruction, the refusal, and the selection are three nines of words on three pieces of paper. The count under the fourth step is nine. The day's book is at nine. The register is at seven questions and no findings. The bench's nine is a fourth nine. The clerk of about twenty-two's nine leaves are a fifth. **A successor may not merge any of them, may not add any of them to another, and may not add any of them to a figure of persons.**

325. **The anchor error in `outline/volume-11.md`, reported and not repaired, and a successor must not silently propagate it.** The outline's calendar table gives day 1626 as a Thursday. **From the same 1596-is-Monday anchor that gives the table's own day 1650 as a Saturday, day 1626 derives as a Wednesday.** The outline is not this project's to edit and no chapter of Batch 0003 is set on or after 1596. **A successor setting any date from day 1626 onward must derive the weekday from the day number rather than read it off that table.**

326. **The lesson carried into the successor's own checks.** All six defects in this pass were claims about people or figures of time checked against the page, and **none was visible to any sweep, and the arithmetic of the ten money columns was correct in all ten.** The cheapest was a month. **A successor's ninth check should be the claims sweep and not the arithmetic sweep, and should be run by reading and not by counting.**

327. **What the successor must not do, in one list.** **Do not obey a scoped chapter range that is already on disk and already finished, and do not overwrite a finished chapter to satisfy one.** Do not edit the scope header, the prompt, the markers, the outline, or any controller file. Do not write a `.done` or a `.retired` marker. Do not name the antagonist, the First Witness or the mechanism. Do not print the word that names the founding paper a second time, in a document, in a quotation or in narration. Do not fill the fourth head of the Crown's form, and do not have an office fill any head on any form. Do not say whose the nine words in the margin are. Do not identify the hand that made the second cut. Do not cut the twenty-second notch, put the wall and the driftwood on one page, schedule choosing for the three words for one lane, reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, send anybody to find out, or open anything. Do not reconcile the refusal figures or add any two of them. Do not rule a third fifth line. Do not print a System panel or a stage, ledger or qualification word. Do not make the copyist say the same thing a fourth time. Do not repair the breach between the two men. Do not thank anybody. **And do not treat a claim about what a person said as a measurement: go and find it on the page and read it, which is the whole of what this pass found and the whole of what it fixed.**

**328. The two things the successor inherits that are not writing, in order of how much they will cost.** **The first is the marker, and it is the higher-impact of the two and it is not this project's to close.** `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/` still carries `.attempts`, `.deferred`, `.retry-after` and `.wip-conflict` and **no `.done` and no `.retired`**, and its `PROMPT.md` still opens *This run writes Chapters 511 to 515 and nothing else*, which is five finished chapters of a retired batch. **The dispatcher reads `.retired`; until somebody with the dispatcher's keys writes it, the next run of the loop will hand a writer five chapters that are already on disk and already finished, and a writer who obeys the header will destroy them.** Three passes have now reported this and none has been able to repair it, because writing a marker is a change to phase selection and phase selection is controller-owned. **It is restated here as the successor's first problem and not as a writing problem, and the successor must not fix it by writing the marker.** **The second is that there is no forward phase at all:** neither `workspace/volume-12/` nor `outline/volume-12.md` exists, Volume 11 is complete and closed at Chapter 550, and **so once 0003 clears there is nothing for the loop to select.** A Volume 12 writer cannot be handed a volume that has no outline, and this pass did not create one, because a new volume's outline is a plan-level decision and not a repair. **Whoever holds the next phase has two pieces of work in front of them and neither is prose: retire the finished batch, and outline Volume 12 against `outline/ending.md` and the list at item 318.**

**329. The lesson from the review of Batch 0003, and it is three lessons and the third is the one to keep.** **The first is that a repair pass can leave a defect in the exact class it was sent to eliminate.** The third pass's own closing note said *a sweep counts tokens and a claim about a person has to be read*, and then it broke a claim about a person by not reading one: it matched two dates, decided two dates meant two speakers, and never opened the second chapter. **Matching a detail is not the same as verifying it, and the detail that matched was the one that was already written into the finding.** **The second is that a record which states its own standard can fail it in the same paragraph.** `state/current.md` said *a count taken before the last repair is a count of a different book* and then carried nine stale figures, because the pass recounted the denominators it had moved and not the numerators it had not; **a successor should treat any figure in a state file as a claim about a moment, and the moment is named or it is not a figure.** **The third is the parity gate's own blind spot, and it is worth more than the two defects it would have caught.** The per-line bold-parity check counts emphasis markers without excluding code spans, and a recursive path glob written inside backticks contributes a doubled marker that is not emphasis: **at `state/continuity.md` l.57, which is a file path, the gate reports an odd line and the line is correct.** Four further lines in that file — l.275, l.351, l.516 and l.779, items 1062, 1090, 1161 and 1279 — are genuinely odd and belong to earlier batches, and **this pass did not repair them, because a line with five markers can be one stray closer and not one missing opener and guessing which is how a record gets corrupted.** A successor that wants the gate to be worth running should make it strip code spans first, and should then decide deliberately about those four lines rather than by a script.

## Threads Volume 12, Batch 0001 hands forward, items 330 to 336

**The highest number in this file before this section was 329, and this section continues from it. Item 318's list is superseded by item 336 below and by `outline/volume-12.md` §16 item 7. Nothing above this line changed. Volume 12 Block 0002 is Chapters 561 to 570 and its prompt is `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`.**

330. **The frame, confirmed and derived.** Day 1651, the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth YR 320, a Sunday, is Volume 12's first day and Chapter 0551. Day 1830, the twenty-fifth of Embermonth YR 320, a Thursday and a market day, is its last. **One hundred and seventy-nine days elapsed, one hundred and eighty inclusive, six months on the carried thirty-day rule. The five blocks are thirty-six days inclusive each and begin on days 1651, 1687, 1723, 1759 and 1795. The year does not turn inside Volume 12: every date in all fifty chapters of it is YR 320, and Thawmonth the first of YR 321 is day 1956.** Block 0001 ran day 1651 to day 1679 across ten datelines, 0 / 0 / 0 on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, with four market mornings and two Sundays. **A Batch 0002 writer derives the weekday and the day of month separately and never copies a row out of `outline/volume-11.md`, whose own table is wrong about seven calendar facts, and never copies a row out of `outline/volume-12.md` either without deriving it, because a printed list is a certificate and a certificate is the last place a defect survives.**

331. **The one place where the inherited prohibition and the plan of record disagree, and it is now closed on the record.** Item 318 said *do not conduct a crossing or schedule one.* **`outline/series.md` is fixed and gives Volume 12 eight months in the Quiet Orchard and a five-beat progression that cannot happen without a path arc. The crossing is therefore made in Block 0002 and not in Block 0001, and `outline/volume-12.md` §16 item 7 is the authority.** **What a Batch 0002 writer may not do: explain the mechanism, name the works under the abbey, say why anybody can cross, look at the roadmark, print a System panel, print the words *stage*, *ledger* or *qualification*, make a cut, say what a Stage is called, or have the crossing be a door that opens because a man touched a bell.** The crossing is made on a road that a person opened by answering a question, it costs a night he cannot place and a habit that no longer fits, and the protagonist's competence is what makes it dangerous.

332. **The finding that governs the whole volume, and it is on the page in Chapter 0558 before anybody in that room can say it.** *A road may be described, opened, refused or certified, and in every one of those four things it may be done by its own answer and by nothing else.* **The valley has not had an answer in twenty-two years and nobody has asked it whether it wants one, and the person who would have to ask was standing in the market and was not asked by anybody including the man who brought the letter.** **A Batch 0002 chapter may not soften this, may not have anybody answer it out loud in Block 0002, and may not have the answer appear because somebody in nine counties wanted a certificate. The answer, when it comes, is a person and not an office, and she can be refused by her own valley.**

333. **The three documents that are not the form, and the two that now are not a document at all.** The Crown's form's fourth head, empty for about five months and about to be six. The foolscap sheet with four ruled lines and a man's name in the fourth of them. The returned form from a county two hundred miles off with a woman's word in its fourth head. The piece of stone a foot and a half long in a room in another county. **And now a leaf about nine inches by six with a mark at the top of it and nothing under it, which is the fifth, and the mark is a scar in a tree and not a drawing, a seal or a stamp, and it cannot be refused, answered, copied or produced.** **Any chapter that puts any two of the five near each other says on the page that they are five documents in five places and none of them is the Crown's form, and the fifth line ruled on a Monday morning by nobody who ordered it is a fifth line and is not a fourth head and is not a sixth.**

334. **The two paths the block opened and Batch 0002 has to close or hold.** **First, the pot of loam with a scion of about nine inches, standing in a hired room over a saddler's and going home on a Saturday cart with the woman who sent it, about whom nobody knows whether it will take in the place it came from.** **Second, the leaf with four ruled heads and a question under the fourth one, going down a made road in a coat that is not a sent coat, and what comes back on it is the whole of Block 0002's first movement.** **The nine words are the same nine words that went under the fourth head of nine leaves in a room two hundred miles off in Thawmonth, and they have now been written twice and are one question and not two, and Chapter 0560 says on the page that a question written a third time in the same words is still one question, and the block did not reprint it a third time.**

335. **The people who are on this block's page and what each of them has not been given.** **A woman of about thirty-eight who wants to change her mind and does not know what she would change it to, and who said out loud that what comes out of her mouth is a voice four generations old, and who is not thanked.** A woman of about forty-five who said one word once and will not say it twice and is not asked what it means. A man of about fifty-three who was sent by an office that is not named and who admitted it and is not a knave. A man of about seventeen with a rod in Merefen who keeps a column on a board and who wrote one line on it that is not money and was not paid for it and was not asked to rub it out. **A woman of about thirty-eight at the gate of a yard in Merefen who put a pot of tea down without being asked, which nobody has thanked her for and which is not a scene anybody is going to turn into one.** **No Volume 12 chapter may thank anybody, may make any of these five a witness, a villain, a symbol or a subject, and may not have a person be found out to be reasonable.**

336. **What Volume 12 must not do, in one list, and it is the item 318 list with one clause overridden and named.** **Do not reconcile anything.** Do not choose between the two readings of a man who published his own mistake, and do not print a paragraph accounting for his silence. Do not fill the Crown's form's fourth head, do not have an office fill any head on any form, do not fill either fifth line, do not rule a sixth, and do not have anybody say a second person has filled a fourth head. **Do not rule a line under the head at the top of the record and do not add a page to the record.** Do not cut the twenty-second notch, do not send anybody to that coast, and do not decide whether the word under a first head is a place or a name. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not schedule it, and **do not use the word *failed* about anything.** Do not build a court: no bench, no bench-list, no hearing room, no clerk of a court, no sentence, no penalty, and no office that did not already exist in that room. Do not answer the question the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked three times in Volume 11, do not answer the four lines from another county, and do not thank or villainise either asker. **Do not turn the half leaf face up, and do not say what is on the other side of it.** **Do not name the antagonist, do not say whose plan the letter is, and do not add an enemy, an office, a council, an order, a mechanism or a person with a want that is about this book.** Do not name the works' oldest protocol, do not print the word that names the founding paper, do not identify the hand that made the second cut, and do not print a word about a roof or the tile on a coping. Do not explain the mechanism, print a System panel, name the works under the abbey, say why anybody can cross, or look at the roadmark. **Do not print *stage*, *ledger* or *qualification*, and do not make a cut in this volume.** Do not resolve the three words for one lane, do not schedule choosing, and do not let the room adopt one. **Do not regulate copying and do not make it an offence or a right.** Do not add any two refusal figures together and do not add any of them to a figure of persons, and do not merge the three series of the room over the weigh-house. Do not put the city on a page, summarise it, or restore it, and its three protected numerals stay at zero. Do not put the wall and the driftwood on the same page. **Do not put a main-world month, a weekday or a tide into an Orchard's mouth, do not have an Orchard dateline carry one, and do not convert the eight-month path interval and the four-month main-world interval into one another after the single conversion in Block 0002.** Do not have the protagonist conduct anything, get anything back, overrule anybody, answer a question about a form, be the reason a thing works, or be thanked. Do not have the copyist say the thing about the fourth head a second time, do not have anybody agree with her out loud, and do not turn her into an instrument. **Do not use the bare-leaf clause twice for two different acts, do not use a line-through clause twice for two different acts, and do not have the word *continuation* said by any other person in the volume.** Do not repair the breach between the two men. **Do not thank anybody.** **Do not let a sentence in a state file stand in for a sentence in a chapter, and do not quote a figure from this file without its basis: every number above was counted on the files after the last prose edit, and a successor that quotes one without counting it is quoting a claim about a different book.**

## Threads Volume 12, Batch 0001's review hands forward, items 337 to 345

**The highest number in this file before this section was 336. These are the open ends of the review of Batch 0001, and the prose side of each is closed; what is open is what a successor must not undo.**

337. **Block 0002 begins on day 1680, not 1687, and the seven days are not a gap.** `outline/volume-12.md` §8 and §17 and `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` have all been corrected. **A writer who reads the old figure of 1687 anywhere — in a prompt, a summary, or from memory — will open seven days late and the volume's own arithmetic will no longer foot.** The volume's five blocks are 29 / 43 / 36 / 36 / 36 days and the total is still 180.

338. **A money column in this block was a clone of an earlier volume's and a sweep cannot see it.** Two were, at 62 and 37 words, and both are re-worded. **The standing instruction for Batch 0002 is therefore stronger than the one it inherited: build each column's item list without reference to any column on disk, and check it against the 91 distinct prior totals on the house form before the day is over.** A column that foots, that carries a fresh total and that re-words somebody else's list is still a defect.

339. **The retired reporting frame cannot be a zero gate.** It is 0 in these ten chapters and 939 in the prior five hundred and fifty, and the commonest lead words in the 939 are *said*, *out* and *saying*. **A writer who trusts a zero gate on this pattern will either break four correct sentences or invent a rule the book does not have, and the pattern is printed here instead of enforced.**

340. **The nine and the eleven are one party on two days and must stay that way.** Nine were in the town on the Sunday the room was shut and two came on the Monday. **The two are never added, never converted, and never printed as a bare figure of persons beside one another without saying which is which.** Chapter 0560 is the model sentence.

341. **The outline's four-and-seven split of the eleven is not on the page.** `outline/volume-12.md` §6 item 4 holds it and Batch 0002 is free to put it there. **The only writer who may put it there is somebody standing in that room, and it may not be printed as a narrator's figure of persons.**

342. **A speech of the previous volume's closing page was lifted into a different mouth and is revoiced.** The lesson is not about this speech. **It is that Volume 11's last page is the most quoted page in the manuscript, and any Volume 12 writer reaching for a cadence near it should check `chapter-0550.md` first.**
