343. **The three corrected figures, so a successor does not re-correct them.** `about` is 440 case-insensitive whole-word and 434 lower-case in 18,829 words, rates 23.37 and 23.05. The block's item count is **99**, not 106. The item prices run **1 to 40**, not 1 to 41. The block is **18,829 words** and the manuscript **1,607,681**.

344. **The collision claim in the state file was false and the method beside it could not have found the collisions.** Both are corrected and the working basis is at `state/continuity.md` 1338. **A successor printing a collision figure must name the extraction form it used, and the house form is the only one that isolates a column's total.**

345. **The two things still open and neither is a writer's to close.** `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` still says Chapters 511 to 515 while the directory now carries a `.done`, and both are controller-owned; the earlier claim that the missing marker was blocking the next run was true during the run, was never re-checked, and is corrected. **And the ninety prior footed columns named in a prompt in `workspace/` are not in a file in this repository, so the prior column list was not re-derived and that check still has not been run.** It is named rather than implied, in both files, for the third block running.

## Threads Volume 12, Batch 0002 hands forward, items 346 to 356

**The highest number in this file before this section was 345 and this section continues from it. Item 336's list is the standing prohibition list for the volume and nothing above this line changed. Volume 12 Block 0003 is Chapters 571 to 580, days 1723 to 1758, and its prompt is `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. Every figure below was counted on the chapter files after this block's last prose edit and carries its basis.**

346. **The block's own figures, with the basis of each, and the two a successor must re-count rather than inherit.** **18,411 words in ten chapters**, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added; per chapter 561 2,278 · 562 1,726 · 563 1,518 · 564 1,769 · 565 1,749 · 566 1,909 · 567 1,693 · 568 1,554 · 569 2,201 · 570 2,014. **Volumes 01 to 11 recount to 1,588,852 and do not move, Batch 0001 recounts to 18,874 and does not move, and the manuscript is 1,626,137 in five hundred and seventy chapters.** **The Batch 0002 prompt printed the manuscript as 1,607,681 and the count at the moment this block opened was 1,607,726, a difference of forty-five, and the reason is that the prompt carried Batch 0001's *after-the-review* figure of 18,829 out of `state/batch-summaries.md` while `state/current.md` and the files carry 18,874. The files are the book; the prompt's figure is the error, as the prompt itself said it would be.** Ten footed money columns at 147, 128, 159, 173, 183, 187, 199, 178, 164 and 191 pence, 102 items in columns of eight to twelve, prices 2 to 40, ten distinct totals, **0 collisions against the 131 distinct totals in 143 instances extracted on the house form from the five hundred and seventy chapters before this block.** `about` **429 case-insensitive whole-word at 23.30 per 1,000** and **426 lower-case at 23.14**, in 18,411 words. Punctuation **13 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0**. Curly quotation marks **145 and 145**. Duplication **10 words against the five hundred and seventy prior chapters and 10 within the block**, on the basis at item 1350. Barred tokens, barred numerals, the twenty self-reference patterns and the retired reporting frame all at **0**. `Aren Kest` **13 in nine of ten chapters**. **Name budget 2 of 5, three slots unspent, and this block spent none.**

347. **The one instrument that changed, and why.** **The collision check is now run on three forms of the house sentence — `A mark is forty-eight pence and X pence is`, `Forty-eight pence is a mark, and X pence is`, and `One hundred and X pence is …, and a mark is forty-eight pence` — and the extraction returns 131 distinct totals in 143 instances, not the 91 in 93 that the Batch 0002 prompt reported.** The prompt's figure was produced by a parser that read only the first form and only spelled *one hundred*, and **it missed forty of the prior columns' totals, every one of which is a total a Batch 0003 column could have collided with.** A successor that uses a single form will report collisions that do not exist and miss collisions that do. **The bare `is … pence` sweep remains unusable: it returns 154 hits for the mark's own definition and cannot separate a column's total from a price in a sentence.**

348. **The two things a Batch 0003 writer must not undo.** **First, the crossing is done and it is not to be done twice or explained:** the bar of wood at the Low Gate, the woman of about fifty-five, the four questions and a person answering; it is not a door and it is not a bell; going out is free and coming in is not. **Second, the decision is made and it is not to be unmade:** in Chapter 0570 he says out loud that he is not going to say what is wrong with the cut, and that the woman is going to have to say it, and that she is going to have to be asked first, and that he is not going to ask her on anybody's behalf including his own. A Batch 0003 chapter may show the cost of that decision and may show him being wrong about it, and may not have him decide it again and may not have him be asked for a name and give one.

349. **The three open things this block opened on purpose, and each is Batch 0003's material.** **The woman of about thirty-eight asked four people one question at the second hour in the open and has not been told she may**, and the board off the middle terrace has a leaf on it with four lines under a fourth head and one word in the margin and nothing on the fourth. **About nine households on the four lowest terraces of the Long Set would lose their ground for a season if the cut were mended, and the four sets have to find the money and two of them have not said anything yet.** **And a carter of about forty is out on a made road with two sacks of pears that are on no road in nine counties, and nobody in that valley has been told what to do about him.**

350. **The two figures a Block 0003 writer may not print as a distance.** **The market town is about four days of carting from the end of the made road, and the valley's own measure is about four days and about nine of hard going from the nearest made road, and the two are different measures of different things and the difference between them is roughly a day's walk up the Low Gate.** **No figure is printed anywhere in Block 0002 for the distance between the gate and the settlement at the middle terrace, and that silence is deliberate: a third number would be an invention, and the two that exist are quoted in their own measures and never added.**

351. **The nine persons of that valley that a Batch 0003 writer may use and the two that are not on the page.** On the page, all by age and trade and all with wants that are not about the protagonist: **a woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a block of the Long Set; a man of about forty-four who holds the second hoe; a man of about fifty-one who keeps the water at the top of the Long Set; a woman of about fifty-eight who turns about nine hundred drying cloths in a sitting; a woman of about sixty-four who ties about nine hundred picking cords in a sitting and counts stones; a woman of about fifty-five who keeps the Low Gate and its board; a man of about sixty and a man of about thirty-one of the Standing, and a woman of about forty-four who keeps the board; a woman of about seventy who said one thing about a leaf; a carter of about forty who is out and a carter of about nineteen who came in; a boy of about fifteen who rings the bell and is kind about a limb; a boy of about nineteen who carried a pot and slept on a drying floor.** Not on the page and not to be produced: **the man who drove the cart to the cross of two stones has a name in no file and does not come up the lane; the man of about thirty-eight who heard the question in a made road in Chapter 0569 has corrected himself on the page and never spoke; and the eleven households and eleven terraces of the Long Set are one figure told two ways and are not a figure of persons.**

352. **The three things a Batch 0003 writer must not print a word about.** **The mechanism, the roadmark, and what the works under the abbey are** — the crossing is on the page as four questions and a person answering and nothing in this block says why it works, and the one time a character came closest, in Chapter 0562, she said she did not know and had decided not to find out, which is a refusal to explain and not an explanation. **A night that a man cannot place is on the page in Chapter 0566 and nothing in it is accounted for**, and Batch 0003 may show him still not accounting for it and may not account for it. **And the left palm** is warm in Chapter 0567 and 566 and in every chapter after, and nobody has been told, and a Batch 0003 chapter may have a person notice it and may not have a person hear him explain it.

353. **A rule of the valley that is new in this block and is local, and the trap it sets.** **A person who holds a block of ground cannot be the answer for the road, because a person who can be moved off it will find that out before anybody else does.** It is said out loud by a man of about sixty in Chapter 0565 and it is a custom of about nine people in a valley, not a law, and no office in nine counties has heard of it and no form has a head for it. **It is the volume's trap with a different shape from the previous nine: the person from outside is the only person who can certify the cut, and the person inside cannot be certified at all while she owns anything, and the cheapest way out of that is the outside man, and the block refuses him.** A Batch 0003 chapter may deepen it and may not resolve it, and Block 0004 is the block in which about nine households lose the water.

354. **The two defects this block's own first drafting contained, and the class is the one this project has paid for in ten consecutive batches.** **Both were figures of persons that a person in the scene could have counted, and neither was visible to a sweep.** Chapter 0565 had a five-seat room holding *about four people believed and about five did not* and *about four and about four* around a man standing by the door, which is nine people in a room of four stools plus a visitor; both are now two, one and one other, and the two-sentence claim was revoiced rather than deleted. **Chapter 0570 had *about nine days ago* for the washing of a floor that was washed forty-three days earlier, which is the whole of the class: a figure that is right for a different day.** It now says the floor was washed before he came up here and prints no interval. **A third, caught on the same pass: Chapter 0561 had a bar of wood at the end of the made road and a second bar of wood at the Low Gate, which is two gates for one crossing, and the end of the made road is now a cross of two stones and a heap of broken metalling.** And a fourth: two women of about thirty-one were doing two jobs in the same valley and the block now has the block-keeper at thirty-one and the board-keeper at forty-four.

355. **The one thing a Batch 0003 writer has to be careful about that is not on anybody's list.** **`Ilsa Wend` is named once in this block, in Chapter 0564, and is a woman of about thirty-eight in the other nine.** A sweep for a name and a sweep for a figure of persons will both return her correctly, and **the risk is a chapter that puts the woman of about thirty-eight and the woman of about thirty-one in one clause and leaves a reader to decide which is meant.** The two are on the same slope in Chapter 0570 and are named by their work in the sentences that follow, and a successor should do the same.

356. **The two things still open and neither is a writer's to close.** `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` still says Chapters 511 to 515 while the directory now carries a `.done`; both are controller-owned and neither was touched. **And the ninety prior footed columns named in a prompt in `workspace/` are still not in a file in this repository, so the prior column list has still not been re-derived; what this block ran instead was the extraction on the house form described at item 347, which found 131 distinct totals in 143 instances and is a wider net than the ninety the prompt refers to, and a successor should re-run it rather than trust the ninety.** It is named rather than implied for the fourth block running.

## Threads Volume 12, Batch 0003 hands forward, items 357 to 368

**The highest number in this file before this section was 356 and this section continues from it. Item 336's list is the standing prohibition list for the volume and nothing above this line changed. Volume 12 Block 0004 is Chapters 581 to 590, days 1759 to 1794, which is the cost block, and its prompt is `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`. Every figure below was counted on the chapter files after this block's last prose edit and carries its basis.**

357. **The opening state of the cost block, in one place, and it is the whole of what Block 0004 inherits.** **The water question is on the table and was not begun. Four sets of people have to find the money between them and have not been asked, and two of them have not heard of it at all. About nine households on the four lowest terraces of the Long Set are the people who will pay and are not the people who were wrong, and a woman of about thirty-one has said that out loud in the open.** A question asked at the ninth hour of Chapter 0580 — will anybody in that valley say out loud that the water has been let out at the foot of a wall and put back in at the head of it — was answered by nobody, and the man of about sixty said out loud that he was not going to answer it and gave the reason that in about nineteen years the only questions put to that board have been ones somebody in that valley already knows the answer to. **Nobody has been scheduled to answer it and nobody has said they will, and the funding argument may be begun in Block 0004 and must not have been begun in Block 0003, and it has not been.**

358. **The record is open and cannot be shut, and three people have seen what is under the lip and none of them has written anything down.** A plank of green wood about two hands long is lying flat side up in the light a hundred yards up the slope, and the man of about fifty-one has said out loud that it is not going back in the ground, and nobody has said where it ought to be kept. A line of chalk is across the lip of the fourth hundred pace and **a line of chalk is not a finding until three people have written it down, and none of the three has.** The three are the man of about fifty-one, the woman of about thirty-eight and the boy of about fifteen, and the boy of about fifteen is paid a penny for the bell and was not paid for the chalk. **A Batch 0004 writer may have the three of them write it down and may not have the man from outside write it down, and the reason is item 1367 and not a rule anybody made.**

359. **The reason the cut wants mending is on a plank and it is a mark with no reason attached, and a man has refused to join the two halves.** Water, out at the foot of a wall and in again at the head of it, on the day the strip was let go. The man of about fifty-one read that half aloud and **said he is not going to say the two things were done together unless somebody puts it in front of him, and nobody did.** **A Batch 0004 writer may put it in front of him, and it should be put in front of him by somebody who can be moved off the ground, and it should not be put in front of the man from outside.**

360. **The two things a Batch 0004 writer must not undo.** **First, the two decisions are made and are not to be unmade.** In Chapter 0570 he said out loud that he is not going to say what is wrong with the cut, that the woman is going to have to be asked first, and that he is not going to ask her on anybody's behalf including his own; in Chapter 0579 he said no out loud to the one job he is able to do, in three reasons, of which the third is that a finding about a road in this valley has to be made by somebody who can be moved off the ground here and he holds nothing. **A Block 0004 chapter may show the cost of either decision and may show him being wrong about one of them, and may not have him decide either again and may not have him put a straight edge on that lip or hold one end of it.** **Second, the crossing is one crossing with one bar at the Low Gate, it was made in Block 0002, and it was not conducted again in this block; no Block 0004 chapter may conduct one either unless it is written out loud in four questions and a person answering, and nothing may say why it works.**

361. **A woman of about thirty-eight wants to be told she was wrong and nobody has done it for her, and that is now a debt the book is carrying.** She said it out loud in Chapter 0578 and again in Chapter 0580, in the open, in front of about nine people, and said she would not say it a third time until somebody has actually done it. **The man of about fifty-one declined to do it and gave the reason, which is that he did not ask to go and did not go either and was about nine, and that if he says it, it will be him being kind.** She said she had hoped he would. **A Block 0004 chapter may pay this and may not pay it cheaply, and whoever pays it must be a person the reader can hold to it, and the payment must not be a speech.**

362. **The four holders of ground inside nine yards of the oldest tree all said she would rather not and it made no difference, and that is the volume's standing shape.** Nobody wrote it down. Nobody has asked any of the four what she would have said if it had been asked a different way, and one of the four has said out loud that she would rather not twice and both of those are on nobody's sheet. **The rule that let them say it and then not stop it was made by the man of about sixty in about four minutes and he said out loud that it is not a good one and that it is the one they have, which is the only thing he has ever been able to do in that room.** A Block 0004 writer may show that rule under pressure and may not repeal it off the page.

363. **The word the outside uses is now on the page and the chapter does not say what the valley calls itself.** A carter of about thirty said it at the Low Gate and the woman of about thirty-eight said it once at the head of the Long Set at the ninth hour and has not said it again. It is in a report of about four hands of writing that went out of the valley twenty-two winters ago with a party and did not come back, and **no one in nine counties can produce it and no chapter of this volume may produce it.** The woman of about seventy said she has a name for the place and that it is not a word anybody from outside can use, and did not give it. **A Block 0004 chapter may use the word again and may not have anybody in that valley adopt it, and no chapter of this volume may print what the valley calls itself.**

364. **The sentence is on the page and it is not attached to anything.** *A beautiful place is not a free place*, said by a woman of about twenty-nine on a made road with nobody else on it, and she said in advance that she would not write it down for him, and gave him no paper and no method and no name. **He was told that if he ever explains the sentence to a room it has already stopped being the thing she said, and that is the constraint Block 0005's delegate has to work inside and it is the volume's whole argument in one line.** **The exchange stands as it is: neither of them said one word about the four years and a half, there was no room on either side of that road with a third person in it, nothing was repaired on a milestone and nothing was asked to be, and the breach is still open.**

365. **The protagonist is further from the work at the end of this block than at the start of it, and that is the shape Block 0004 and Block 0005 are built on.** He is four days and about nine above a bar of wood lying in the grass and nobody in nine counties has been told. His left palm is warm and he has still never told anybody and nobody in this block noticed it. **A Batch 0004 chapter may have a person notice it and may not have a person hear him explain it.** His notebook's eleven lines and the separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* are not looked at in this block and may not be looked at in the next one. About four hundred people say one thing about him and about four hundred say the other and the two sets do not overlap and he said no when he was asked whether he was going to correct them, **and no chapter chooses between them and no chapter prints a paragraph accounting for his silence.** He was given nine barrows to empty and the man who gave them to him did not thank him and did not tell him he had done it well.

366. **The block's own figures with the basis of each, and the two a successor must re-count rather than inherit.** **18,121 words in ten chapters**, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added; per chapter 571 1,774 · 572 1,769 · 573 2,002 · 574 1,666 · 575 1,957 · 576 1,680 · 577 1,823 · 578 1,873 · 579 1,942 · 580 1,635. **Volumes 01 to 11 recount to 1,588,852 and do not move, Batch 0001 recounts to 18,874 and does not move, Batch 0002 recounts to 18,411 and does not move, and the manuscript is 1,644,258 in five hundred and eighty chapters, counted on the files after this block's last prose edit.** Ten footed money columns at 174, 158, 231, 112, 205, 190, 163, 216, 127 and 172 pence, 92 items in columns of eight to ten, prices 2 to 41, ten distinct totals, **0 collisions**, and every column's printed total, printed sum line and printed mark conversion were parsed out of its own file and checked, not read. `about` **495 case-insensitive whole-word at 27.32 per 1,000** and **491 lower-case at 27.10**. Punctuation **21 / 0 / 3 / 0 / 0 / 0**. Curly quotation marks **195 and 195**. Duplication **22 words against the five hundred and seventy prior chapters and 18 within the block**, and both runs are the ceremonial cost-paragraph and coda frame and not prose. Barred tokens, barred numerals, `continuation`, the twenty self-reference patterns and the retired reporting frame all at **0**. `Aren Kest` **6 on a direct count in five of ten chapters**. **Name budget 2 of 5, three slots unspent, and this block spent none.**

367. **The instrument that changed this block, and why a successor should print the figure it actually got.** The prior footed-column set was re-extracted from the five hundred and seventy chapters before this block on the three house forms and **returned 110 distinct totals in 113 instances, where the Batch 0003 prompt printed 131 in 143 and Batch 0002's own pass printed the same 131.** **This file records 110 in 113 because that is the figure this pass computed with its parser named, and a successor that quotes 131 is quoting a figure whose basis it has not checked.** The bare `is … pence` sweep remains unusable: it returns 154 hits for the mark's own definition and cannot separate a column's total from a price in a sentence. **The ten new totals were chosen against the re-extracted set and not against the printed one, so a successor that re-extracts and gets a different set should re-check the ten against its own set rather than assume they are safe.**

368. **The five defects this block's own first drafting contained, and the class is the one this project has paid for in eleven consecutive batches.** **Four are counts that were checked against nothing at all: Chapters 0576, 0578, 0579 and 0580 each called a list of figures one or two shorter than the list actually was, and every one of the four was in a coda that enumerates the figures of its chapter that are not in its column, and every coda in this block has now been counted item by item.** **One is a register slip only a sweep could see — *the readers* in Chapter 0575 and *this block* in Chapter 0578 — and both are ordinary English that this book uses, which is the standing reason a rule about register cannot be enforced by a token count.** **One is a barred word that arrived in a repair, a *roof* in Chapter 0576, and that is the fifth time in three blocks that a repair has created the defect it was sent to remove, so the barred list is run after the repairs.** **And one is a rule with no exit: Chapter 0573's digging rule as first drafted depended on four holders not saying no, and all four of them say she would rather not, so the block as first drafted could not have had its own middle event; a successor writing a rule in a room should check before the scene whether anything the scene needs can still happen afterwards.** The two things still open and neither is a writer's to close: `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` still says Chapters 511 to 515 while its directory carries a `.done`, and both are controller-owned and untouched; **and the ninety prior footed columns named in a prompt in `workspace/` are still not in a file in this repository, so that list has still not been re-derived, and what this block ran instead was the extraction on the three house forms described at item 367.**
